Lua API names were diffed mechanically (`Lua_helper.add_callback` / `Convert.addCallback` vs our `registerFunction` / `addCallback` **and** the `Py*Lib`/HScript mirror sets): SPE registers **484** names, we register **420**, and **253** SPE names have no equivalent here — but a large share of those are SPE-side wrappers around things Phoenix already exposes under a different name, or are *declared and never read* in SPE itself. Both cases are called out per entry; do not port dead toggles.

**Honest headline:** SPE is **bigger on editors, on one long list of HUD/gameplay toggles, on i18n and on Windows-native scripting**; Phoenix is far ahead on shaders, filters, chart-format breadth, accessibility, security, low-end performance and test/spec discipline. Below is only what we actually lack.

---

### 32.1. SCRIPTING & MODDING API (the 253-name delta, grouped)

Names below were verified absent from `source/` by exact-string search. Everything registered goes through `psychlua/callbacks/*` and is inherited by Lua, Python and HScript at once (`PythonScript.hx:388`, `HScript.hx:249`), so **one registration covers all three languages** — that is why these are cheap.

- **[P1] Script-local variable store — `setVar`/`getVar`/`setGlobalVar`/`getGlobalVar` (+`getValue`/`setValue`).** SPE registers all five in `psychlua/backend/FunkinLua.hx` and they back its whole "share state between scripts" story (`getGlobalFromScript` only crosses *named* scripts here, `callbacks/ScriptCallbacks.hx:107`). Ours has no per-script key/value bag at all. Add one `Map<String,Dynamic>` per `FunkinLua` instance with a `global` flag for the shared one, expose in all three languages, and clear it on script close. Fits §12.1's "managed runtime scripts" entry. Effort: S.
- **[P1] State/substate scripting surface — `switchToState`, `exitToState`, `getStateName`, `getParentStateName`, `callStateFunction`, `callSubstateFunction`, `openScriptedSubstate`, `getScriptHostType`, `getLuaMode`, `isRawLua`, `launchMod`, `exitToEngine`, `setExitTarget`.** We have `switchCustomState`/`openModSubState`/`customStateExists` (`psychlua/CustomSubstate.hx:21-23`) and nothing else: a script cannot ask *which* state it is in, cannot call a function by name on that state, and cannot relaunch a mod or return to the engine from a mod. SPE's `MenuScripts` + `ScriptableState` pair makes menu scripting a first-class scope (see §32.2). Effort: M for the introspection half (`getStateName`/`getParentStateName`/`call*Function`), M for `launchMod`/`exitToEngine`.
- **[P1] `callMethod` / `callMethodFromClass` / `createInstance` / `addInstance` / `instanceArg`.** SPE's `ReflectionFunctions.hx` (368 lines) lets a script invoke any public method on any object or class, and instantiate a class with arbitrary ctor args. We deliberately ship `Reflect.callMethod`/`Type.createInstance` as **blocked patterns in the security scanner** (`backend/ScriptSecurityScanner.hx:98`) and have no replacement, so a mod that needs `Character.new(...)` or `FlxTween.num(...)` cannot run here at all. Port it *as a sandboxed allowlist* — a per-class set of `{method, arity, arg types}` in `backend.ModSecurity`, defaulting to the Flx/engine classes mods actually use, with the scanner still blocking the raw `Reflect`/`Type` forms. That keeps the §6 security posture while closing a real capability gap. Effort: M.
- **[P2] Camera scroll API — `setCameraScroll`, `addCameraScroll`, `getCameraScrollX`, `getCameraScrollY`, `setCameraFollowPoint`, `addCameraFollowPoint`, `getCameraFollowX`, `getCameraFollowY`, `getCameraScroll*` on the modchart side.** `callbacks/CameraCallbacks.hx` only has `createCamera`/`addCamera`/`removeCamera`, and `callbacks/GameCallbacks.hx:223-243` has `cameraSetTarget`/`cameraShake`/`cameraFlash`/`cameraFade` — **camera position is not scriptable at all**. Every scroll/parallax mod chart needs this. Effort: S.
- **[P2] Object screen-space math — `getObjectScreenX`/`Y`/`Width`/`Height`, `gameToScreenX`/`Y`, `getScreenWidth`/`Height`/`Resolution`.** We have `getScreenPositionX/Y` and `getMidpointX/Y` (`callbacks/SpriteCallbacks.hx`) but not the object's *rendered* box or the game→screen transform, which is what touch hit-testing and multi-window drawing need. Effort: S.
- **[P2] `removeFromGroup` companion — `addToGroup`.** `PropertyCallbacks.hx:104` registers `removeFromGroup` but there is no way to *add* to a group from a script. One-sided. Effort: S.
- **[P2] Colour helpers — `getColorFromString`, `getColorFromName`.** `backend.CoolUtil.colorFromString` exists (`CoolUtil.hx:439`) but is not registered for scripts; mods must hardcode ARGB ints. Effort: S.
- **[P2] `updateScoreText`.** Force a score-text refresh after a script changes score/misses — today scripts must wait for the next hit. Effort: S.
- **[P2] Sound pitch — `getSoundPitch` / `setSoundPitch`.** `callbacks/SoundCallbacks.hx` covers volume/time/fade but not pitch, so a script cannot do the slowed/sped-up voice gimmick that SPE mods rely on (`playbackRate` on a `FlxSound` is the hook). Effort: S.
- **[P3] Text extras — `setTextAutoSize`, `setTextHeight`.** `callbacks/TextCallbacks.hx` has `setTextWidth` but not height/auto-size. Trivial. Effort: S.
- **[P3] FlxAnimate extras — `setFlxAnimateAutoDeactivate` / `deactivateFlxAnimateSprite`.** We ship `makeFlxAnimateSprite`, `loadAnimateAtlas`, `addAnimationBySymbol(Indices)` (`psychlua/FlxAnimateFunctions.hx:39,54`) — i.e. **more** SpriteMat symbol support than SPE — but no way to park an idle atlas off the GPU. Matters on phones with several atlases. Effort: S.

#### 32.1.1. Windows-native scripting (desktop-only, `sys && windows`)

- **[P2] Window geometry & lifecycle — `setWindowSize`/`X`/`Y`/`Pos`, `getWindowWidth`/`Height`/`X`/`Y`, `getGameWindowWidth`/`Height`/`X`/`Y`, `centerWindow`, `setWinRCenter`, `randomizeWindowPosition`, `setWindowResizable`, `setWindowVisible`, `setWindowAlwaysOnTop`, `setWindowBackground`, `setWindowBorderColor`, `setWindowIcon`, `setWindowBorderless`, `hideWindowBorder`, `setWindowView`, `windowExists`, `getWindowState`, `saveWindowState`/`loadWindowState`, `getWindowTitle`/`setWindowTitle`, `getWindowOpacity`/`setWindowOpacity`, `tweenWindowOpacity`, `winTweenSize`/`winTweenX`/`winTweenY`, `tweenWindowBorderColor`, `minimizeWindow`.** **`WindowCallbacks.hx` is 124 lines and registers exactly nine names**: the four transparency/click-through calls plus `applyWindowTransparency`, and one `setWindowFullscreen`. Everything else — size, position, opacity, border, icon, always-on-top, and the whole `winTween*` easing set (`psychlua/backend/WindowTweens.hx`, 884 lines upstream) — is missing even though `backend/Windows.hx`/`WindowNative.hx` already do the native work for transparency. This is the single largest *cheap* API gap in the review: port `WindowTweens` almost verbatim, gate each call on `PlatformSupport.DESKTOP` + `#if windows`, and default every effect off per the porting rule. Effort: M (mostly transcription).
- **[P2] Multi-window ("outside the window" / Unresponsive-style) — `createWindow`, `closeWindow`, `closeAllWindows`, `addSpriteToWindow`, `removeSpriteFromWindow`, `setWindowView`, `setWindowAlwaysOnTop`.** SPE's `psychlua/MultiWindowFunctions.hx` (478 lines) plus `WinNative.hx` (310 lines of Win32) open extra layered topmost windows that never steal focus and redraw chosen game sprites into them each frame. We have the *transparent-window* half (`backend/FlxTransWindow.hx`, click-through) but only on the main HWND. This is what the Unresponsive-style gag mods are built on, and it is genuinely absent here. Effort: **L** (Win32 C++ + per-frame BitmapData readback; do it after the geometry API above, which it reuses).
- **[P3] Screen/desktop info — `getScreenWidth`/`Height`/`Resolution`, `getGameWindow*`, `captureScreenshot`, `getDesktopWindowsXPos`/`Y`, `getWindowsVersion`.** `captureScreenshot` is a real gap: our `backend/SSPlugin` only fires from its F2 key check (`SSPlugin.hx:34,129`), so a script cannot take a shot. Wire one public entry point (see §22.5's auto-screenshot entry, which wants the same call). Effort: S.
- **[P3] Clipboard & notifications for scripts — `clipboardGetText`/`clipboardSetText`/`clipboardHasText`, `showNotification`.** We have `PlatformUtil.sendNotification` + a `Windows Notification` chart event and `docs/DESKTOP_NOTIFICATIONS.md`, but **no script binding** and no clipboard binding; SPE has both, and its clipboard helpers are what its "copy this Lua snippet" editors use. Effort: S.
- **[P3] `showToast` / `setActivityTitle` / `setOrientation` / `isScreenKeyboardShown` / `manualBackButton` on mobile.** Our `android/platform/AndroidStdFire.hx:65` already implements `showToast`, and `AndroidNotification`/`AndroidDisplay`/`AndroidIntents` have the rest of the native calls — **none of it is reachable from Lua/Python/HScript.** SPE's `mobile/psychlua/Functions.hx` (48 registrations) is exactly that binding layer. Add a `psychlua/callbacks/MobileCallbacks.hx` that calls the modules we already own. Effort: M, high payoff on Android.
- **[P3] Android device probes — `isRooted`, `isTablet`, `isChromebook`, `isDeXMode`, `isDolbyAtmos`, `isAndroidTV`.** Ours has `AndroidSystem.isAndroidTV`/`getDeviceInfo` and `AndroidTVPolicy`; the other five are unimplemented even natively. Mostly `Build.*` reads through the existing JNI bridge; low value, listed for completeness. Effort: S.
- **[P3] `getSoundPitch`/`setSoundPitch`, `getChartNotes`, `getBPM`/`getCurrentBeat`/`getCurrentStep`.** `getBPM`/beat/step are not script functions here (only `curStep`/`curBeat` *variables* pushed at `PlayState.hx:3372,3417`), and `getChartNotes` — read the whole chart from a script — has no equivalent. `getChartNotes` is the useful one: it turns a modchart script into a real chart-driven tool. Effort: S.

#### 32.1.2. The ActorFrame sprite-tree API

- **[P3] `_actorFrame*` (11 names) — a nested "actor frame" object model.** `_actorFrameCreate/Destroy/Set/Get/SetColor/Tween/CancelTweens/SetShader/SetTarget/Capture/Exists` in `psychlua/ActorFrameFunctions.hx` + a 4-file backend including a **Lua prelude** (`ActorFrameLuaPrelude.hx`) that builds a small OOP wrapper in Lua itself, plus `ActorFrameTextureSource` for render-to-texture capture. It is a step-game-style container hierarchy (parent tags, per-frame capture, group tween). Phoenix's `ModchartSprite` + groups cover the flat case; the *hierarchy with captured textures* does not exist. Port only if a real mod asks — the whole thing is ~1.2k lines for a use case our `createCamera` + `sprite` pipeline mostly covers. Effort: L. **Priority deliberately low.**

---

### 32.2. EDITORS — the biggest real gap

SPE's `MasterEditorMenu` lists **ten** editors (`states/editors/MasterEditorMenu.hx:13-24`: chart, character, stage, week, **hud**, menu character, dialogue, dialogue **portrait**, dialogue **pixel**, note splash); two more are reachable from inside the chart editor (**Event Creator**, `ChartingState.hx:4971`; **Cover Maker**, `:4960`) and a third from the master menu (**Modchart Converter**, `MasterEditorMenu.hx:169`). Ours has five (`editors/MasterEditorMenu.hx:19-25`: Menu Character, Character, Chart, Modchart Lab, Note Splash *Debug*). Below is everything they have that we do not.

- **[P1] HUD / layout editor (`states/editors/HudEditorState.hx`, 4,173 lines).** Drag the native HUD widgets (healthBar, scoreTxt, timeBar/timeTxt, comboGroup, both strum lines), import PNG overlays, reorder by zIndex, group-move (Strums Opponent / Boyfriend / All), and **record chained keyframe tweens** per element (A→B→C…) with per-segment duration/easing, then **export the whole thing as a Lua layout script** — overlays into `onCreate()`, moved strums into `onCountdownStarted()`, chained tweens into `onBeatHit`/`onStepHit`, draw order as `setObjectOrder` calls. Everything lives in `camHUD` only. We have nothing: `play/PlayState.hx` hardcodes HUD layout and the only way to move a widget is a script written by hand. This is the single highest-value port in the whole review — it turns hours of trial-and-error scripting into drag-and-drop. Their header documents the traps that will bite us too: strums are created in `startCountdown()`, *after* `onCreatePost` (so static strum positions must go in `onCountdownStarted()`); icons follow the health bar every frame and cannot be moved; `healthBar`/`timeBar`/`comboGroup` are groups with no reliable scale/angle; `missesTxt`/`accuracyTxt`/`timeBarBG`/`albumArt` do not exist as separate widgets in their build. Port the *generator* pattern first — the accessibility work in §16 wants a layout layer, and Photo mode (§21.1, now DONE) shows the HUD can already be hidden wholesale. 4K-only is fine initially — it matches our `play/helpers/PlayStateNoteHelpers.hx:257` (`for (i in 0...4)`). Effort: **L**, and worth it.
- **[P2] Cover Maker — an in-game mini-DAW (`states/editors/CoverMaker.hx` 2,308 lines + `CoverMidi`/`CoverVoice`; opened as a *substate* of the chart editor, `ChartingState.hx:4960`, which is why it is easy to miss in their menu).** Piano-roll timeline for Dad/BF/Instrumental lanes, MIDI import for the vocal parts, per-note preview and slice count, exports a `Voices`/`Voices-Player` pair so a mod can ship a mashup cover **without leaving the game**. We have no audio-authoring tool at all. Effort: **L**. Cheap half worth doing first: the MIDI **reader** (`CoverMidi`), which also feeds the chart editor's import below.
- **[P2] Event Creator (`states/editors/EventCreatorState.hx` 2,341 lines + a 4-file `eventcreator/` backend; opened from the chart editor, `ChartingState.hx:4971`).** A **visual editor for custom Lua events**: name/description/category, typed parameters (`text,int,float,bool,slider,dropdown,character,color,sound,image,stage` — the last five enumerated from actual game folders via `EventPresets`), a block-based body with a palette, `EventCodegen` emitting the `.lua`, and a **`Regenerate`** button that rewrites the chart's event list when a parameter is renamed. This solves a real hole: today a mod author hand-writes both the script and the event JSON, and they drift. Our `editors/charting/ChartingEvents.hx` only *consumes* event names. Effort: M–L. Pairs with §17's mod tooling and §21.1's Extras hub.
- **[P2] MIDI → chart import (`ChartingState.addMidiPicker` / `scanMidiFolder` / `parseMidi`, ~700 lines inside their 8,502-line chart editor).** Pick a `.mid` from `assets/midi/` or **Browse…**, then **Import as BF / as Dad / as Dad+BF / Auto split**, converting pitch→lane and length→sustain with a documented `MIDI_SUSTAIN_MIN_STEPS` threshold, plus a warning list for BPM changes the chart can't represent. We have **zero** MIDI code and no `moonchart` dependency (`hmm.json` has none; §12.4 already asked for `.sm`/`.ssc` import as a separate entry — this is the `.mid` sibling, and the same importer would serve SPE's OGG-pitch-detection path too). Effort: M.
- **[P2] Character editor — "Anims from XML" (`CharacterEditorState.hx:697`, XML picker at `:1698-1710`).** Pick any `.xml`+`.png` pair from `images/characters/`, get every frame name, and **auto-generate the character's `anims` array** (and its `flip_x`/offsets guesses) in place. Our character editor requires typing each animation by hand. This is a small, self-contained, high-annoyance-saving feature. Effort: S–M.
- **[P2] ~~Stage editor with drag-and-drop.~~ NO LONGER A GAP** — `main` merged `editors/StageEditorState.hx` (1,674 lines) + `data/StageEditorCore.hx` (886) with real dragging (`StageEditorState.hx:1528-1547`), `Ctrl` for free-drag, grid snapping, and `_editorMeta` round-tripping (`:763-765`). Theirs is still bigger (4,550 lines) — the concrete deltas worth a follow-up are **multi-select boxes**, **right-click deselect / click-again-to-cycle overlapping sprites**, and per-object **beat-sync + scroll-factor editing**. Effort: S–M as a patch to what we now have.
- **[P2] Dialogue pixel editor (`DialoguePixelEditorState.hx`, 670 lines).** Week 6 pixel dialogue, with **the real `DialogueBoxPixel` as the live preview** (WYSIWYG), portrait lookup from `images/custom_dialogs/dialogPortraits/`, add/delete/reorder lines, per-line expression, saves to `data/<song>/dialogue-pixel.json`. Our Master Editor has a plain Dialogue Editor but no pixel variant, and W6 is shipped content. Effort: M.
- **[P3] Note Splash editor *parity*.** We ship `NoteSplashDebugState` (offset nudging, min/max FPS, double-tap ENTER save). SPE's `NoteSplashEditorState` (1,093 lines) additionally does: **`Convert TXT`** (turn a legacy `noteSplashes.txt` into the modern config), **Allow RGB / Allow Pixel** toggles, **`Add/Update` a named animation** with its own prefix, and a **`Color to Replace`** shader pass. The three that matter are `Convert TXT` (migration for old mods) and the two allowance flags. Effort: S–M.
- **[P3] Chart editor — five missing tabs' worth of tooling.** Ours has 7 tabs (`ChartingState.hx:515-521`); theirs has 10 (`addChartingTab`, `addDataTab`, `addEventsTab`, `addNoteTab`, `addSectionTab`, `addSongTab`, **`addMetaTab`**, **`addFileTab`**, **`addEditTab`**, **`addViewTab`**). Concretely missing here:
  - **Edit tab** (the highest-value one): `Ctrl+C/X/V/A` on a note selection box, `Ctrl+Z`/`Ctrl+Y` undo **stacks** (`addUndoAction(ADD_NOTE/…)` with grouped actions), Cut/Copy/Paste of notes **and events**. We have `undos`/`redos` arrays and `ChartingSaveLoad.undo()` but **no copy/paste/cut at all** and no grouped undo. This is the most-used editing shortcut set in any charter. Effort: M.
  - **View tab**: waveform target switch (inst / player vox / opp vox), zoom in/out, **section jump list** (`Jump Section`), toggle grid/sections/events column.
  - **Meta tab**: a song-meta form (see §32.10) with license/link rows.
  - **File tab**: **Import MIDI…**, **New Chart**, **Browse…** file pickers for chart/events JSON, drop-a-JSON-onto-the-window loading (`'Drop JSON file here to load chart or events'`).
  - **Data tab**: `PreloadListSubState` — a per-chart **preload manifest editor** (see §32.10).
  Effort: M for Edit+View (highest value), S each for the rest.
- **[P3] Modchart Converter (`ModchartConverterState.hx`, 172 lines).** Paste an NVME/older-style Lua modchart on the clipboard, get a converted first pass with a preview pane, plus warnings about what it cannot translate ("reassign the steps, remove the NVMe radian helpers, `fieldYaw` is the Plus-side name"). Small, but it is the on-ramp for mods written against other modchart stacks. Effort: S.
- **[P3] Scriptable menu states — "Menu Scripting".** With `HSCRIPT_ALLOWED`, `MainMenuState` loads `scripts/menus/MainMenuState/init.lua|.hx`, and `FreeplayStateSelector` picks `FreeplayState_Psych` or a script-supplied `FreeplayState` per launched mod (`backend/ScriptableState`, `scripting/ScriptedStates`). We have `psychlua/StateScripts` (overlay-only, `data/states/<State>.hx`) and `ModState`/`ModSubState` as blank hosts — **no way to replace a whole menu**, and no Lua menu scripting at all. §12.1 lists "scripted states" only as *script scopes*; this is the concrete mechanism worth copying, including their `FreeplayStateSelector` indirection pattern. Effort: M.

---

### 32.3. OPTIONS & PREFERENCES

Thirty-odd keys exist in SPE's `ClientPrefs` with **no counterpart here** (`grep` of our `ClientPrefs.hx` for each: all missing). Sorted by whether SPE actually *reads* them — several do not, and those are marked.

**Read by SPE and genuinely absent here:**

- **[P1] `shadedTimeBar` — "Gradient Time Bar".** Tints the bar's fill with the **opponent's icon colour** and the empty part with **BF's** (`PlayState.hx:1051`: `timeBar.setColors(dadColor, bfColor)`), so it reads as a tug-of-war. `timeBarType` itself already exists here (`ClientPrefs.hx:111`, values `Time Left` / …), so this is *only* the tinting (`shadedTimeBar`, `VisualsSettingsSubState.hx:109`). One pref, two `setColors` calls at build time and again on icon swap. Effort: S.
- **[P2] `breakTimer` — "Break Timer" HUD.** Shows a countdown/timer when the next notes are far away (their `states/play/BreakTimerHud.hx`, drawn with an MD3 wavy progress ring). `breakTimer` is turned on at `PlayState.hx:2352-2353` through `breakTimerHud.updateDisplay(Conductor.songPosition, startingSong, playerStrums, downScroll, strumCenterX, strumTopY)`. Nothing like it here; useful on spam-mod charts where the next note is 8 seconds away.
- **[P2] `showKeyViewer` + `keyViewerColor` + `keyViewerOffset`, and the `objects/KeyViewer.hx` behind them (691 lines).** A **pressure-bar key viewer**: per-lane key rectangle, KPS counter, total-press counter, growing pressure bars, centring, colour presets with contrast-aware text colour, music-beat animation, and it re-labels itself live when the binds change. We have **no key viewer object whatsoever**, and `showKeyViewer`/`keyViewerColor`/`keyViewerOffset` do not exist as fields in our `ClientPrefs.hx` (verified by `find source -ipath '*keyviewer*'` → empty, `grep -r keyViewer source/` → 0). Their `NoteOffsetState` also embeds the viewer as a live preview of the offset you are editing, which is the better half of the idea. Effort: M; the pressure bars can start as plain rectangles.
- **[P2] `dynamicCombo` (combo digits grow with the number instead of always 3 wide), `nfRatingStyle` (ratings/combo "bop in place" instead of fly-and-fade), `timeBump` (kill the time-text bump), `showVersionText` (song + difficulty + build on the gameplay HUD), `endCountdown`/`endCountSecs` (last-seconds countdown), `pauseCountdown` (3-2-1 on unpause), `heyIntro` (BF/GF auto-"Hey!" on GO — never read, see the note at the end of this subsection).** All six that are left are read at real call sites in SPE: `dynamicCombo` (`PlayState.hx:3759` — `combo.lpad('0',3)` vs the raw number), `nfRatingStyle` (`:3648`, `:3712` — bop in place instead of fly-and-fade), `timeBump` (`:1832`), `showVersionText` (`:676`), `endCountdown` (`:2372`, `:3491`), `pauseCountdown` (`:2193`). We have `comboStacking`, `simplePopups`, `iconBounceType`, `ratingType`, `showNPS` — different axes. **Two of the six are already spoken for here**: `pauseCountdown` is our `resumeCountdown` (§21.3, DONE — *check whether it already matches their 3-2-1 behaviour before adding anything*), and the intro `countdownStyle` entry in §22.4 covers the same ground as their `endCountdown` sibling. That leaves **`dynamicCombo`, `nfRatingStyle`, `timeBump`, `showVersionText`** — four one-line `Option` rows reading a value the HUD code already computes. Effort: S each.
- **[P2] `badShitBreakCombo`** — Bad/Shit hits count as combo **breaks** instead of misses (SPE `PlayState.hx:3690`). Our combo logic has no such branch. Effort: S.
- **[P2] `guitarHeroSustains`** — sustains are judged as **one note**: the head hit covers the whole hold, sustain segments stop being individually hit-checked (`PlayState.hx:4068` returns early for `parent == null`, `:4090` for sustain notes), and the hold stops paying health per segment (`:4275` sets `gainHealth = false`). Ours judges every segment (`play/PlayState.hx` sustain handling) and pays health per segment. It changes scoring, so it is a *changer*-shaped option, not a cosmetic one — and it makes long holds survivable in dense charts. Effort: M.
- **[P2] `useFlawlessRating` + `flawlessWindow` + `systemScoreMultiplier` ('Psych' | 'Codename').** A **Flawless** judgement above Sick with its own window, and a switchable multi-note **score multiplier/weight table** (300 vs 350 for Sick, weighted 1/0.67/0.34/0). We have `perfect`/`noMarvJudge` and `AccuracyPresets`, but no extra top judgement and no per-judgement score weights. Their `backend/Rating.hx` is the clean 98-line shape to copy: `{name, image, hitWindow, ratingMod, score, noteSplash, hits}` built from prefs. This also cleanly supersedes the "Mic'd Up S/A ranking" wish in §0. Effort: M.
- **[P2] `naughtynessResults`** — flips the Results screen to its "naughty" flavour (`ResultsState.hx:537`). **Already present here** (`grep naughtyness source/` finds it used in our Results screen) — listed only because a naive pref diff flags it. Skip.
- **[P2] `showSustainSplash`/`hideSustainSplash` equivalent.** SPE has a pref that suppresses **sustain** splashes (`objects/SustainSplash.hx:64,85`) and a "Hide Sustain Splash" row in Visuals. We ship `play/objects/SustainSplash` (pooled via `ClientPrefs.maxSplashLimit`, `PlayState.hx:753`) but **no preference to turn it off** — on a hold-heavy phone chart it is pure cost. Effort: S.
- **[P2] Modchart settings block — `camera3dEnabled`, `zScale`, `arrowPaths`, `styledPaths`, `pathBounds`, `optimizeHolds`, `holdsBehindStrum`, `holdEndScale`, `holdCache`, `holdAlphaSteps`, `colMods`, `modchartDebug`.** Their `options/ModchartSettingsSubState.hx` surfaces all twelve, and their renderer reads them (`modchart/backend/graphics/CtxRenderer.hx:183,246`, `renderers/HoldRenderer.hx:160,374`, `renderers/ArrowRenderer.hx:155`). Of these we already expose the equivalent of `camera3dEnabled`+`zScale` as our single `setModchartPerspective` (§12.5) — but **arrow-path rendering** (draw each note's trajectory as a line, with colour/alpha and a pixel bound) and the **hold rendering knobs** (behind-strum, end scale, cached alpha steps, per-lane mods) are genuinely absent: `grep` for `arrowPaths`/`holdsBehindStrum`/`holdEndScale` in `source/` returns nothing on our side. Port behind `ClientPrefs` rows defaulting to their low-risk values (`arrowPaths` off, it is the expensive one). Effort: M for paths, S for the hold knobs.
- **[P2] Per-mod **defined** options — `ModSettingsSubState`.** A mod declares its own `Option` rows in `pack.json` (`settings: [...]`); the Mods menu shows a `SETTINGS` button that opens them and they persist per mod folder. **In Phoenix the whole thing is still commented out** — `states/ModsMenuState.hx:324-336` has the button inside a `// not yet` block, `find source -iname '*ModSettings*'` returns nothing, and the only hit for the name is that dead code. **Re-verified after the `main` merge.** §22.5 has "pre-mod settings *profiles*" (player prefs per mod) which is a **different feature**; this entry is mod-*author*-defined settings. Uncomment, wire `backend.Mods` to read the `settings` array, and keep the existing "no settings" disabled state. Effort: S–M, very high value for modders.
- **[P2] Mobile: `storageType` ('INTERNAL' | 'EXTERNAL_DATA') with `AndroidPermissionsState`, `infinityDisplay`, `showTouchPointer`, `showMobileDebugButtons`, `mobileReceptorAlign`, `controlsAlpha`, `extraButtons`, `hitboxPos`, `hitboxType`, `dynamicColors`.** Our `MobileOptionsSubState` has `mobileCAlpha`, `mobileCEx`, `mobileCExTaunt`, `mobileControlScale`, `androidTVNavigation`, `screensaver`, `hapticFeedback`, `androidNotifications`, `hitboxType`, `hitboxPos` — so we match or beat half of it. Missing: **a storage-location picker with a permission flow** (`android/platform/AndroidStorage.hx` already implements the whole SAF/content-URI layer and there is no UI in front of it — `ClientPrefs` has no `storageType`), a **touch pointer overlay**, a **receptor↔hitbox alignment** toggle, and **dynamic control colours** (pads tint themselves with the song's beat/health colour). These four are one `Option` row each plus wiring. Effort: S–M; the storage UI is the valuable one.
- **[P3] `fullscreenMode` ('Borderless' | 'Borderless Fix' | 'Exclusive') + `instantWindowClose`.** Our §22.2 `windowMode` entry covers the first (three-way Windowed/Borderless/Fullscreen); note SPE distinguishes *two kinds of borderless*, which is the reason people ask for the row at all — fold that distinction into the §22.2 implementation rather than adding a parallel pref.
- **[P3] `windowsGDIEffects` — a consent gate for the GDI/4th-wall payloads.** SPE refuses to run any GDI effect unless the player ticked the box (`options/GameplaySettingsSubState.hx:126`, enforced at `slushithings/windows/WindowsAPI.hx:35`). We own more GDI machinery than they do (`backend/GDIEffects.hx`, `psychlua/callbacks/GdiCallbacks.hx` — 20 functions, `WindowColorMode`) with **no consent gate at all**. Overlaps the "4th-wall effects" toggle in §2.1 — implement once there. Their `changeWindowBorderColorWithNoteHit` and `maxInstanceSlots` are **declared and never read** in SPE (the only reader of the latter is the value's own early loader, `Main.hx:67`), so ignore both. Effort: S.

**Declared in SPE but never read — do NOT port as options:** `heyIntro` (**the row exists at `options/VisualsSettingsSubState.hx:237` and nothing ever reads `ClientPrefs.data.heyIntro`**), `maxInstanceSlots`, `changeWindowBorderColorWithNoteHit`, `holdCache`, `popUpRating` (six reads, all UI-local), `gameOverVibration` (only their `GameOverSubstate`). Every entry above that I did *not* mark dead was confirmed at a real read site; keep that habit when porting, because SPE's `SaveVariables` class is wider than its behaviour.

---

### 32.4. GAMEPLAY, HUD & CHART EVENTS

- **[P1] `Change Stage` chart event (instant, zero-drop).** SPE's `backend/StageChanger.hx` (621 lines) reconstitutes a stage **as plain `FlxSprite`s, never instantiating the stage classes** (which is what avoids the classic `Limo.update()` null crash), queues and **preloads every stage the chart will use onto a hidden slot**, then swaps on the event's beat with a blackout/char/cam kill-switch set (`noblack | nochars | nozoom | nocam | nogf`). We do **not** have this: `grep -rn "'Change Stage'" source/` finds exactly one hit, in `states/LoadingState.hx:329`, which merely *reads* the event to collect characters for preloading — **the event does nothing at runtime**. This is a concrete, documented feature with a known-hard problem already solved in the source. Port with credits. Effort: M.
- **[P2] `Lyric Event`.** Value 1 = text, shown in a tweened `FlxText` at 72% height, empty clears it — the widget is built lazily only when the event is queued (`PlayState.hx:266` field, `:601-606` creation, `:2990` case). We have no lyrics anywhere. Effort: S. Pairs with §23.3's proposed `Add Subtitle` (same widget, so build the widget once).
- **[P2] `Hurt Note` as a **chart event**. The note **type** already works here: `'Hurt Note'` is in our chart-editor list (`editors/ChartingState.hx:76`), the texture swap is `objects/Note.hx:673` with `noteSplashes/HURTnoteSplashes`, and the editor preview already applies `missHealth 0.3` / `hitCausesMiss` (`editors/helpers/EditorPlayStateHelpers.hx:105`). What is missing is the **event form** that SPE adds at `PlayState.hx:4285` — spawn hurt notes from an event timeline instead of authoring each note by hand. Small, and it reuses the type above. Effort: S.
- **[P3] `Set Camera Bopping`, `Add Camera Zoom`, `Camera Follow Pos`, `Set GF Speed`, `Kill Henchmen`, `Play Animation`, `Play Sound`, `Alt Idle Animation` — all already here** (`play/helpers/PlayStateEvents.hx` accepts every one of these names). Listed so nobody re-ports them.
- **[P3] "Character JSON Generator (Anims from XML)"** — the character-editor feature in §32.2; listed here because their README pitches it as a headline and it is *not* a content feature.

---

### 32.5. MODCHART ENGINE

Their `source/modchart/` is upstream Plus Engine (§5.3, ~60 modifiers, macro registry, event timeline, Lua/HScript modifiers, **standalone adapter**), and ours is a smaller native port (35 registered names in `modchart/ModifierRegistry.hx:56-90`). Concretely:

- **[P2] Twelve named modifiers with no equivalent here.** Verified by name against `ModifierRegistry`: `Asymptote`, `Attenuate`, `Carousel`, `Cubic`, `Digital`, `Field`, `LuaPath`, `ModSpline`, `Orient`, `Parabola`, `RandomSpeed`, `StraightHolds`, plus the `false_paradise/` set (`EyeShape`, `SchmovinArrowShape` — we do have `Spiral`, `Vibrate`, `Wiggle`, `CounterClockWise`) and `psych_noteTween/` (`NoteTweenAngle`, `NoteTweenDirection` — we have the `noteTween*` **Lua functions** but not as modchart mods). `PathModifier` and `ArrowShape` are already tracked in §12.5/§23.1 — cross-referenced, not duplicated. The rest are small offset/curve math on the same `Modifier` base; port as a batch. Effort: M total.
- **[P2] Raw modifier access + scheduled-easing API — `setRawValue`/`getRawValue`, `addNow`, `easeNow`, `callback`, `repeater`, `scheduleCallback`, `queueSet/queueEase/queueFunc/queueFuncOnce` (+`*P` variants), `alias`, `node`, `quickRegister`, `getVisPos`/`getBaseVisPosD`, `getBaseX/Y`, `getArrowSize(Div2)`, `getHoldSize(Div2)`, `getHoldSubdivisions`/`setHoldSubdivisions`, `setModifierPath(Bound/Offset)`, `createModifier`/`createLuaModifier`/`addScriptedModifier`, `removePlayfield`, `getPlayerCount`.** We expose exactly **14** modchart functions (`callbacks/ModchartCallbacks.hx`: `addModifier, addPlayfield, getModchartModifiers, getPercent, isModchartActive, modAdd, modCallback, modEase, modRepeater, modSet, modSetAdd, resetModchart, setModchartPerspective, setPercent`). Those 14 are 1:1 with our manager API (`ModchartManager.hx` public methods) — i.e. **every gap above is also a gap in the engine layer**, not just the binding. Notably missing capabilities: *script-authored modifiers at runtime* (`createLuaModifier` — a big one for NotITG-style mod packs), naming playfields/`alias`es for multi-field charts, scheduled callbacks by beat, and any read of the post-transform visual position. Port in that order. Effort: L; split it — `alias` + `removePlayfield` + read-side getters first (S), scripted modifiers after (M), path/ease queue last (M).
- **[P3] Standalone adapter.** Their `modchart/backend/standalone/` runs the whole modchart engine with a `IAdapter`/`Adapter` pair and a `psych` implementation, so a preview or an external tool can drive it with no `PlayState`. Our `ModchartLabState` is the in-engine equivalent and already does exactly this (§21.1, DONE) — so this is **not** a gap. Recorded so it is not re-reviewed.

---

### 32.6. UI KIT & THEMING

- **[P2] Material Design 3 widget set (`backend/ui/md3/`, 12 files) + `OptionsMenuTheme` (365) + `ThemeAccentColorSubState` (586).** Buttons, boxes, chips, checkboxes, text fields, linear/wavy progress rings, loading indicator, a `NetworkCheckToast`, plus `MD3Theme`/`MD3Metrics`/`MD3ShapeTools` that derive a *full tonal palette* (primary/secondary/container/surface roles) from one accent colour, in light and dark. Our `objects/ui/CustomFlxUI.hx` is **243 lines** of FlxUI subclasses plus string-preset styling (`backend/IconStyle`, `scoreStyle`, `timeBarStyle`) — there is **no theme system and no accent-colour picker** (`grep -rn 'accentColor' source/` → nothing). Their `ThemeAccentColorSubState` even has HEX copy/paste and a live widget preview. This is the UI layer behind every screen they modernised, and it is the biggest *visual* gap in the review. Start with `MD3Theme` + `MaterialButton`/`MaterialTextField`/`MaterialWavyProgressIndicator` (that last one is what the Break Timer uses) rather than porting all twelve. Effort: L; `MD3Theme` alone (S) unlocks the accent-colour option.
- **[P3] `objects/Cursor.hx` — a global cursor abstraction.** `Cursor.show/hide/toggle/set(CursorMode)/loadCustom`, cursor-mode **names** (`Default`, etc.) with a cache of custom BitmapData, wired into both the Plus and the V-Slice runtimes. We have per-state `FlxG.mouse.visible = true` scattered around plus `backend.PointingCursorLogic` for the menu finger — no named cursor modes and no custom-cursor loader. Their file is small and the official Funkin cursor art is what it uses. Effort: S.

---

### 32.7. INTERNATIONALISATION ⭐

- **[P1] A real translation layer — 12 hardcoded language classes + a `.lang` fallback + an in-game language picker.** `backend/languages/` ships `EnUS, EsLA, EsES, PtBR, FrFR, DeDE, ItIT, NlNL, IdID, JpJP, ZhCN, ZhHK, RuRUS` (7,165 lines; **476 keys** in `EnUS` alone, incl. per-option `name_*`/`description_*` keys), loaded by `backend/Language.hx` with a phrase cache, a merged-mod-override path (`data/<locale>.lang`), `Language.getPhrase(key, fallback)` everywhere, a `LanguageSubState` picker, Android auto-detecting the device locale on first run, and `getFileTranslation`/`getTranslationPhrase` **also exposed to Lua** so mods can translate their own content. **Phoenix has nothing**: `grep -rn 'getPhrase' source/` → 0 hits, and the only trace of i18n is `ClientPrefs.locale = 'en'` (`ClientPrefs.hx:63`, **written and never read**) and one line in §12.4 (`TODO.md:234`, P3: *"Localization / translations. No language key system or locale files."*). This is under-prioritised at P3: Phx has a large Brazilian and Latin-American audience (the TODO itself is written with BR/LATAM idioms), and the whole system is *mechanical* to port — keys are already isolated in `Alphabet`/`Option` labels, and their language files are Apache-2.0 with named authors (`Hogich` for RU). Do it in two passes: **(a)** the `Language` loader + `getPhrase` + `LanguageSubState` + `en-US` only (S), **(b)** port the eleven other files as data (M, mostly transcription). Keep all thirteen, credit each translator in `CreditsState`. Effort: **M**, and it is the highest-value *non-editor* item in this review.

---

### 32.8. PLATFORM / NATIVE

- **[P1] Android **storage location** support (scoped vs public) with a permission state.** `mobile/backend/StorageUtil.hx` (898 lines of JNI) + `mobile/states/AndroidPermissionsState.hx` + a `storageType` pref, handling SDK-version differences, creating directories, **copying the user's existing `mods/` and `sm/` folders when the mode changes**, and a status string for the settings screen. We have a large native Android layer (`android/platform/AndroidStorage.hx` with the full SAF/`content://` implementation, `AndroidIntents`, `AndroidNotification`, `AndroidBridge`) — **more capable at the native level than theirs** — but nothing in Options selects a storage root and no migration copies mods. Ties into §11. Effort: M.
- **[P2] `backend/ShaderCompatibility.hx` — runtime shader *dialect* rewriting.** At runtime it parses incoming GLSL and: strips uniform initialisers, rewrites `mainImage`/`iResolution` Shadertoy entry points into `main`, **localises OpenFL global initialisers**, warns on unsupported uniforms, normalises strict constructors, and adapts **`notitg:`- and `shadertoy:`-flagged** sources. This is what makes 0.6.3-era and Shadertoy-pasted mod shaders compile without the modder touching them. Our `shaders/RuntimeShaders.hx` is a hardcoded enum of *engine* shaders and `ErrorHandledShader` only catches errors — we do **not** rewrite third-party GLSL. Given our far larger shader catalogue and §23.2's ports, this is the missing compatibility half. Port `adaptRuntimeShaderCode` + the two dialect adapters. Effort: M (700 lines, mostly regex + string work).
- **[P3] `backend/ClassResolver.hx` — old-Psych class-path compatibility map.** 371 lines of `'old.path' => 'new.path'` covering Psych **0.6.3 → 0.7.3 → 1.0.4 → PlusEngine**, so a script's `import funkin.objects.Note` or `funkin.backend.ClientPrefs` still resolves. We renamed a lot (`play/`, `psychlua/callbacks/`, `data/`) and ship **no alias table**, so old HScript mods fail at import. `docs/research/psych-fork-engine-changes.md` shows this is a fork-wide problem; ours is the most refactored tree of the set, so we need it most. Also add their `LEGACY_PREF_NAMES` pattern (`ClientPrefs.hx:196`) — a name-migration table for renamed prefs (`showEarlyLateSprites`→`showTimingTags`, etc.), which we currently handle ad hoc or not at all. Effort: M.
- **[P3] `lime/media/AudioManager.hx` + `alsoft.txt` → `plugins/alsoft.ini|conf`.** They shadow `AudioManager` to apply an OpenAL-Soft config on mobile (where it cannot be dropped next to the exe) and ship `alsoft` with HRTF/period settings. We already read the ALSoft version (`Native.getOpenALSoftVersion`) and have an `alsoft` string in `project.hxp` — worth checking whether our shipped config actually reaches Android. Effort: S, verification first.
- **[P3] `backend/RenderInterpolation.hx` + `PsychAnimationController`.** Frame-time interpolation of camera positions between fixed steps, and an animation controller that scales with a global `FlxG.animationTimeScale` (so animations keep real-world speed at 30/60/240 fps). We already set `FlxG.animationTimeScale` (`PlayState.hx:641`) and patch `FlxGame._accumulator` (`source-haxelib-patches/flixel/FlxGame.hx:589`), so **the interpolation half may already exist** — verify before porting; if not, their 69-line `RenderInterpolation` is the reference. Effort: S.
- **[P3] Build/asset ergonomics worth copying:** `assets/base_game/` + `assets/week_assets/` + `assets/translations/` split with `rename=` in `Project.xml` (so a translated asset set overlays by path, not by code), `backend/AssetLoader`/`AssetCache`/`AssetResolver`/`TextureOptimizer`/`ThreadUtil` (the last two explicitly "for low-end devices, does NOT resize textures — that breaks Sparrow XML coordinates" — the exact trap our §0 "compress all images" item would fall into), and `BuildInfo`/`BuildInfoGenerated` for an in-game build stamp. Our `backend.CommitFreshness`/`HaxeCommit` covers the stamp; the **`TextureOptimizer` warning is the valuable part** — record it next to the §0 downscale goal. Effort: S to document, M to implement.

---

### 32.9. GAME CONTENT SHIPPED

- **[P3] Two extra base-game songs: `ridge` and `smash`.** `assets/base_game/shared/data/{ridge,smash}/` (chart + `song_meta.json`, 28 KB each) — chart/editor test songs (both are empty-section skeletons in the tree), plus `song_meta.json` for all 29 songs. We ship 29 song folders in `assets/preload/data/` but **no `song_meta.json` anywhere** and no `ridge`/`smash`. Content itself is trivial; what matters is the **`song_meta.json` schema** — see §32.10.
- **[P3] Note skins & HUD art:** `NOTE_assets-notitg` (a fifth note skin), `HURTNOTE_assets` + `HURTnoteSplashes` (we ship these — see §32.4), `holdCovers/` (their hold-splash art set; ours is `noteSplashes/holdSplashes/holdSplash*` per `play/objects/SustainSplash.hx:33`, so nothing is dangling here — cited only as art they ship and we do not), `comboBroken.png` + `pixelUI/comboBroken-pixel.png` (a **combo-broken** popup, absent here), `albumRoll/` + `Menu_Tracks.png` variants, `early.png`/`late.png`/`flawless.png` (we have early/late/perfect via `ratings/<style>/`), `miss.png`, `controllertype.png`, and per-locale image overrides under `assets/translations/shared/images/<locale>/`. The two worth adding to our asset set are the **note-skin #5** and **`comboBroken`**; the locale-override folder layout only matters with §32.7.
- **[P3] Week-1..7 stage polish** (BF/GF "Hey!" reactions on specific songs, Tank lightning flash, henchmen death anims, Week 6 HUD-hide during the cutscene, slower GF head-bop). Song-specific tuning rather than engine work; port only if we want FNF-vanilla parity marketing. Not a TODO item beyond a note.

---

### 32.10. DATA FORMATS

- **[P2] `song_meta.json` per song, with a real schema.** `{coverAlbum, albumId, card, songAuthor, artist, composer, freeplayWeekName, freeplaySongLength, freeplayPrevStart, freeplayPrevEnd, links{newgrounds,bandcamp,youtube,x}, licenses[], licenseText}` — read by `FreeplayState.hx:2703-2720` (album art, author, preview window, license badges, social links) and by the chart editor's Meta tab (`ChartingState.hx:4407`). This is the data behind their whole V-Slice-style Freeplay. Ours has **no meta file**: `assets/preload/data/<song>/` holds only chart JSONs + `notetypes.txt`, so author/preview-window/license/link data has nowhere to live. Cross-references §12.2's `meta.json` entry (CNE) — **implement both from one schema**, and keep it optional so existing mods load unchanged. Effort: M.
- **[P2] Consume the stage `preload` map at load time.** `data/StageData.hx:36-40` now declares `objects`, `preload` and `_editorMeta`, the Stage Editor writes them (`data/StageEditorCore.hx:299`, `editors/helpers/StageEditorHelpers.hx:164`), and chat loading calls `buildPreloadQueue()` (`states/LoadingState.hx:164`) — but **nothing reads `stage.preload` to warm those images/sounds** (`grep -rn '\.preload' source/` finds only the writers). So the manifest round-trips and does nothing. The read side is what SPE's `PreloadListSubState` + loader pair provides: `{'images/spraycanAtlas/': 3}`, where the number is a quality/keep-alive class. Also missing: the in-editor list UI for it. Effort: M.
- **[P3] `psych_v2` chart format + editor support (`Song.hx:395,531` auto-detect and convert `format:"psych_v2"` → v1, flat note/event/BPM arrays instead of sections) and the V-Slice import button (`states/editors/content/VSlice.hx` 490 lines, `PsychJsonPrinter` 220, plus `VSliceFreeplayState`).** Our `data/Song.hx:256-264` **already** handles `format` / psych-v1 (`psychV1Chart` flag, strips `format`, returns the object), so psych_v1 is covered — **psych_v2 and the V-Slice *import path*** are not, and V-Slice charts are what modern mods ship. Note §12.2 asks for CNE's `meta.json`+`charts/<diff>.json` layout; a V-Slice importer is the same work seen from the other side. Effort: M–L.

---

### 32.11. ARCHITECTURE IDEAS WORTH TAKING

- **[P2] Scripted classes with real native superclasses (`psychlua/backend/ScriptedClass.hx` 361 + `ScriptedNativeFactory.hx` 670).** An HScript file can declare `class MySprite extends FlxSprite { … }` and get an actual `FlxSprite`-compatible instance, with `FlxSprite`, `FlxSpriteGroup`, `FlxState`, `FlxSubState`, `MusicBeatState`, `MusicBeatSubstate` as supported parents, `IScriptCustomConstructor`/`hnew`, per-instance interpreters seeded from the parent's globals, and `IScriptSuperProxyProvider` for `super` calls. We build on `hscript-improved` (`hmm.json`), which *does* have a `ScriptedClass`-adjacent `ClassDecl` code path — but we register no supported-parent factory, no per-instance interpreter seeding and no `super` proxy, so a script class cannot extend a native sprite in practice. There is **no `addScriptedClass`-style surface anywhere in our tree** — a script cannot define a type, so `new` of a script-declared class is impossible and `addScriptedClass`-style mods cannot run. This is why SPE runs mods we cannot. Cross-reference §12.1's "HScript module system". Both trees now sit on `hscript-improved`, so this is **not a dependency swap** — it is the missing factory/seeding layer. Effort: L, and it is the deepest change in this review.
- **[P3] Macro helpers — `ScriptClassExportMacro`, `ScriptedBridgeMacro`, `PlusFlxMacro`.** Compile-time generation of HScript-visible wrappers around Haxe classes, so "expose this class to scripts" is an annotation rather than hand-written glue. Interesting, but it depends on §32.11's first entry. Record only.
- **[P3] Gameplay-changers as a **collection** with a public API — `addGameplayOption` / `removeGameplayOption` / `getOptionByName` / `getCurrentOption` / `selectOption(index)` / `rebuildOptionsVisuals()` + six companion callbacks (`onGameplayChangerCreatePost/Rebuild/SelectionChange/Reset/Accept/Back`).** Our `GameplayChangersHelpers` builds a fixed list. Note **their list is not better than ours** — they have 14 changers, we have 20+ including `bothsides`, `flip`, `onekey`, `jacks`, `randommode`, `stairmode`, `wavemode`, `thetrollingever`, and most of theirs (`opponentplay`, `opponentdrain`, `nodroppenalty`, `perfect`, `botplayType`) are **registered but never read in their `PlayState`** (`grep` finds only the option rows). Port the **API shape**, not the changers. Effort: S–M.
- **[P3] `objects/GlobalLoadingOverlay` — a persistent Material spinner usable from anywhere.** A static `show/pulse/showPersistent/hide` overlay in the `openfl.display` layer, above every Flx state. Our `states.LoadingState` + `LoadingPieDial` are per-state. Useful for the mod-import, reload and chart-load paths. Effort: S.
- **[P3] `TransitionManager` + `CustomFadeTransition` as a **scriptable** transition (`ScriptableSubstate.tryCreate('TransitionSubstate')`), with door/gradient/watermark/event-text/icon art and `cancelCurrentTransition()`.** Ours (`shaders/CustomFadeTransition.hx`) is a bare gradient + black bar and is not replaceable by a script. Replace-the-transition is a popular mod ask; tie it to §12.1's "scripted transitions". Effort: M.

---

### 32.12. CHECKED AND **NOT** WORTH PORTING

- **`2hot`-era song list and weeks.** Same as ours; `ridge`/`smash` are empty test charts (§32.9).
- **Their `AccuracyPresets`-less rating table.** Their `backend/Rating.hx` is a *nice shape* (§32.3 Flawless entry) but it has no preset system at all — ours (`backend/AccuracyPresets.hx`, `Vanilla/Psych/Kade/ITG/Custom`) is strictly ahead. Do not replace ours.
- **`backend/MemoryManager.hx` (458) / `backend/Cache.hx` / `AssetCache`.** Our `backend.AssetCache`, `MemoryLedger`, `MemoryProfiles`, `LowMemory`, `PoolStats`, `Paths.clearUnusedMemory` stack is broader and tested (`tests/haxe/*Memory*`). Skip.
- **`debug/TraceDisplay.hx` (675) + `TraceButton`.** An in-game coloured trace console. Ours routes traces to `VLog` + `DebugLuaText` + the crash bundle; an on-screen console is §12.3's ImGui Console entry, which is a better target.
- **`debug/FPSCounter` FPS "modes"** (Visible with/without Background, Basic/Extended Debug). We have `showFPS`, `legacyFPSUI`, `show1PercentLows`, `showRamUsage/Graph`, `debugInfo`, `FrameProfiler` — a superset. Skip.
- **`objects/MusicPlayer` / `MusicPlayerPsych` preview players.** We have `music/MusicPlayer.hx` wired into `FreeplayState.hx:352` plus the auto-preview entry in §22.5. Skip.
- **`objects/Watermark`, `objects/SustainSplash`, `objects/TypedAlphabet`, `objects/CheckboxThingie`, `objects/AttachedSprite/Text`, `objects/Alphabet`, `HealthIcon`, `MenuCharacter`.** All present here under the same names. Skip.
- **`objects/StickerSubState`** (a sticker-board transition from a `stickerPack` JSON, `FlxTransitionableState` based). Fun, purely cosmetic, no consumer in their tree. Skip.
- **`states/FreeplayState_Psych` / `VSliceFreeplayState` / `FreeplayStateSelector`.** Our `FreeplayState` already has a filter page, search, favourites, stars and per-mod filtering (§21.2 DONE) — richer than their legacy-Psych fallback. Only the **selector indirection** is worth taking, for §32.2's menu scripting.
- **`substates/OutdatedSubState` rework / `backend/Discord` (old RPC) / `backend/Achievements` placement.** Ours is newer (`backend/UpdateChecker`, `DiscordClient`, `AchievementsMenuState` + score API). Skip.
- **Their Lua API *wrappers* that duplicate ours under another name:** `doTweenX/Y/Angle/Alpha/Zoom/Color`, `noteTween*`, `startTween`/`cancelTween`, `runTimer`/`cancelTimer`, `mouseClicked/Pressed/Released`, `keyboardJustPressed/Pressed/Released`, `anyGamepad*`, `gamepadAnalogX/Y`, `getRandomInt/Float/Bool`, `stringStartsWith/EndsWith`, `getProperty*`/`setProperty*`/`getObjectOrder`/`setObjectOrder`, `setHealthBarColors`/`setTimeBarColors`/`setObjectCamera`/`screenCenter`/`objectsOverlap`/`getPixelColor`, `makeLuaSprite`/`makeAnimatedLuaSprite`/`addAnimation*`/`playAnim`/`scaleObject`/`updateHitbox`, `makeLuaText`/`setText*`, `playMusic`/`playSound`/`fade*`/`getSoundVolume`…, `loadGraphic`/`loadFrames`, `precacheImage/Sound/Music`, `addLuaScript`/`removeLuaScript`/`addHScript`, `startDialogue`/`startVideo`, `cameraSetTarget`/`cameraShake`/`cameraFlash`/`cameraFade`, `setRatingPercent`/`setRatingName`/`setRatingFC`, `getMouseX/Y`, `characterDance`, `triggerEvent`, `startCountdown`/`endSong`/`restartSong`/`exitSong`/`getSongPosition`, `addCharacterToList`, `getScore`/`getMisses`/`getHits`/`addScore`/`addMisses`/`addHits`/`setScore`/`setMisses`/`setHits`/`setHealth`/`addHealth`/`getHealth`, the `luaSprite*`/`objectPlayAnimation` deprecated aliases, `getRunningScripts`/`callScript`/`isRunning`, `FlxColor`, `getColorFromHex`, `debugPrint`, `getMidpointX/Y`, `getScreenPositionX/Y`. **All present here** — verified one by one. Every one of these was in my initial 253-name diff and is a false positive from the `add_callback` vs `registerFunction` naming split; they are listed here so the next reviewer does not re-diff by hand.
- **Their `addAnimationByIndicesLoop` deprecated alias.** We have `addAnimationByIndicesLoop` (`callbacks/SpriteCallbacks.hx`). Skip.
- **Meme/joke content:** `assets/secrets/` Pessy/Shadow/River bumpers + jingles, `menuThemeMode` joke presets, `curses`/"Little Timmy Mode". Some of these are *nice* (we have `ClothEasterEgg`/`MetaHorror` in the same spirit) but they are their engine's mascot; not portable content.
- **Two behaviour changes that would be regressions here:** their `BeatTimerHud`-style timer lives inside `PlayState`'s `update` (per-frame string formatting — ours would go in `play/helpers/`); and `HudEditorState` hardcodes 4K strums **and** states in its own header that icons cannot be moved because `PlayState` rewrites them every frame — if we ever generalise lane count (§25.1), the ported HUD editor must not inherit that constraint.

---

### 32.13. SUGGESTED ORDER (highest value ÷ effort first)

1. **Free wins, one day each:** `Hurt Note` event (§32.4), `Lyric Event` (§32.4), the missing single-key `Option` rows — `dynamicCombo`, `nfRatingStyle`, `timeBump`, `showVersionText`, sustain-splash toggle, `badShitBreakCombo` (§32.3), `addToGroup`, `getColorFromString/Name`, `updateScoreText`, `getSoundPitch/setSoundPitch`, camera-scroll getters/setters (§32.1), and a `comboBroken` popup (§32.9).
2. **Scripting parity pass:** script-local variables, state introspection, mobile bindings, clipboard/notification/screenshot bindings, `getChartNotes` (§32.1). Then `WindowTweens` + window geometry (§32.1.1) — transcription, big payoff for desktop mods.
3. **i18n pass (a):** `backend.Language` + `getPhrase` + picker + `en-US` (§32.7). Then (b) the other twelve files as data.
4. **`Change Stage` event + `StageChanger`** (§32.4) and **`ModSettingsSubState` uncomment** (§32.3) — both are already-written code sitting unused in one tree or the other.
5. **Chart editor Edit/View tabs** (copy/cut/paste/select-all/grouped undo/section jump/waveform target) + **MIDI import** + **`song_meta.json` + Meta tab** (§32.2, §32.10).
6. **`ShaderCompatibility`** (§32.8) — unlocks every legacy mod shader at once.
7. **`HUD Editor`** (§32.2) — the single biggest feature in this review; do it after step 5 so it can reuse the editor's undo/selection work.
8. **Scripted classes** (§32.11) — a dependency decision as much as a port; scope it separately.
9. **Material 3 theme + accent colour** (§32.6) and **Android storage location** (§32.8).
10. **Modchart modifier batch + scripted modifiers** (§32.5), **Cover Maker** and **Event Creator** (§32.2) as the long tail.

Nothing in this section should land without an entry in `docs/` (+ `website/docs/` where it is a subsystem), a `tests/haxe/run-*.sh` spec for the pure-logic half, and per-feature credits as the rule at the top of this file requires.

---

## 33. CANDIDATE HAXELIBS & ECOSYSTEM DEPENDENCIES (2026-11-21)

Audited against Phoenix Engine's current `hmm.json` (20 dependencies), `project.hxp`, `SIZE.md`, **Apache-2.0** license requirements (MIT / BSD / Apache-2.0 only), and single-branch multi-target builds (`cpp` Desktop + Android/iOS + `html5`). Overlaps with §0–§32 are cross-referenced rather than duplicated.

### 33.1. High-Payoff Roadmap Libraries

- **[P1] `moonchart` ([`MaybeMaru/moonchart`](https://github.com/MaybeMaru/moonchart) — MIT, pure Haxe, all targets).** Converts rhythm-game charts across **FNF** (Legacy, Psych, V-Slice, Codename), **osu!mania** (`.osu`/`.osz`), **StepMania** (`.sm`/`.ssc`), **Quaver** (`.qua`/`.qp`), Guitar Hero (`.chart`), and MIDI (`.mid`). Directly unlocks cross-game import/export in `ChartingState` and CNE/V-Slice chart loading (§12.2, §31.4, §32.10) with zero native NDLL overhead. Note Frozen Engine's `ChartConvertCore.hx` fixes for >7K lane wrapping and `offsetHolds` (`docs/research/psych-fork-engine-changes.md` §5.4). Effort: M.
- **[P2] `rulescript` ([`Kriptel/RuleScript`](https://github.com/Kriptel/RuleScript) — MIT) or `hscript-iris` ([`crowplexus/hscript-iris`](https://github.com/crowplexus/hscript-iris) — MIT, pure Haxe, all targets).** Modern HScript upgrades over `CodenameCrew/hscript-improved` adding scripted `class`/`enum` declarations, `import ... as`, `using`, package/module sharing, string interpolation, and stricter `#if` preprocessor support — advancing §12.1 (*HScript module system*) and §32.11 (*Scripted classes*). Keep all reflection/imports gated through `ModSecurity` and `ScriptSecurityScanner`. Effort: M–L.
- **[P2] `hxgamejolt-api` ([`MAJigsaw77/hxgamejolt-api`](https://github.com/MAJigsaw77/hxgamejolt-api) — MIT, pure Haxe, all targets).** Actively maintained GameJolt API bindings by MAJigsaw77 (same maintainer as Phoenix's `hxdiscord_rpc`, `hxnativefiledialog`, and `hxgamemode`), replacing the legacy Screwed Engine `GJClient.hx` snippet (§0) and unlicensed/unmaintained `tentools` (§27.8). Effort: S–M.
- **[P2] `flxgif` ([`MAJigsaw77/flxgif`](https://github.com/MAJigsaw77/flxgif) — MIT, all targets).** Animated `.gif` playback directly on Flixel sprites. Pairs with the planned in-game GameBanana mod browser (§0, §31.3) so mod thumbnails, avatars, and banners render without converting GIFs to Sparrow atlases. Effort: S.
- **[P2] `FunkinModchart` ([`TheoDevelops/FunkinModchart`](https://github.com/TheoDevelops/FunkinModchart) — Apache-2.0, Flixel `cpp`/`html5`).** Implements true 3D arrow meshes and subdivided sustain-strip triangle rendering (`FlxDrawTrianglesItem`) for HaxeFlixel VSRGs. Either adopt directly or use as an Apache-2.0 reference for `modchart/SustainStripGeom.hx` so bent/reversed modifiers in `source/modchart/` no longer detach rigid sustain sprites (§28). Effort: M–L.

### 33.2. UI, Editors, Localization & Vector Assets

- **[P2] `haxeui-core` + `haxeui-flixel` ([`haxeui/haxeui-core`](https://github.com/haxeui/haxeui-core), [`haxeui/haxeui-flixel`](https://github.com/haxeui/haxeui-flixel) — MIT, all targets).** `project.hxp` (`configureCompileDefines`) already defines `haxeui_no_mouse_reset`, `haxeui_focus_out_on_click`, and `haxeui_dont_impose_base_class` without the library installed. Provides dockable panels, tree views, rich tooltips (§0), color pickers, and scroll containers for `StageEditorState`, `ChartingState`, and `ModchartLabState`, reducing reliance on `flixel-ui 2.6.0` (which currently requires 5 overrides under `source/source-haxelib-patches/flixel/addons/ui/`). Effort: L.
- **[P2] `firetongue` ([`larsiusprime/firetongue`](https://github.com/larsiusprime/firetongue) — MIT) or `kotoba` ([`MAJigsaw77/kotoba`](https://github.com/MAJigsaw77/kotoba) — MIT, pure Haxe, all targets).** Localization/i18n frameworks (§12.4, §31.2, §32.7). `firetongue` provides TSV/CSV/JSON translation tables, pluralization, and **per-locale font replacement rules** (pairing naturally with `AlphabetFont` and §0's custom game-wide font goal); `kotoba` offers a lightweight XML-based alternative if minimal footprint is preferred. Effort: M.
- **[P3] `svg` ([`openfl/svg`](https://github.com/openfl/svg) — MIT, pure Haxe, all targets).** Rasterizes vector `.svg` assets into `BitmapData` at runtime at the exact target DPI (`backend/ScreenDensity.hx`, widescreen `16:10` / `9:17`), keeping UI/editor icons crisp across 4K desktop and mobile without inflating APK/ZIP asset size (`SIZE.md`). Effort: S–M.
- **[P3] `hxluau` ([`ShadowEngineTeam/hxluau`](https://github.com/ShadowEngineTeam/hxluau) — MIT, `cpp`).** Optional Luau scripting backend alongside `hxluajit` for built-in VM sandboxing, faster cold start on JIT-restricted targets (iOS), and optional typed Lua scripts. Phoenix's `FunkinCrew/hxcpp` C++17 + `ml64` toolchain setup in `BUILDING.md` already documents Luau compatibility. Effort: L.

### 33.3. Performance, Binary Formats, Networking & Testing

- **[P2] `json2object` ([`elnabo/json2object`](https://github.com/elnabo/json2object) — MIT, compile-time macros, all targets).** Generates reflection-free, type-safe JSON parsers and writers at compile time with line/column schema validation errors. Ideal for `SwagSong`, `CharacterFile`, `StageData`, and `WeekData` — cutting chart parse/reflection overhead (§8) and safely catching malformed mod JSONs (§6) even with `-dce no`. Effort: M.
- **[P2] `hxbit` ([`HeapsIO/hxbit`](https://github.com/HeapsIO/hxbit) — BSD, pure Haxe, all targets).** Fast, compact macro-driven binary serialization. Fits frame-accurate **Replays** (§14, `phoenix://replay/<id>` in §11) and binary chart caching (§26) without platform-specific C++ `mmap` glue. Effort: M.
- **[P3] `hxWebSockets` ([`ianharrigan/hxWebSockets`](https://github.com/ianharrigan/hxWebSockets) — MIT) & `colyseus` ([`colyseus/colyseus-hx`](https://github.com/colyseus/colyseus-hx) — MIT, `cpp`/`html5`).** Cross-platform WebSockets and multiplayer room state synchronization across Desktop, Android/iOS, and `html5` if opt-in online multiplayer or live spectator rooms (§31.3) are built. Effort: L (with online feature work).
- **[P3] `hxdr_libs` ([`MAJigsaw77/hxdr_libs`](https://github.com/MAJigsaw77/hxdr_libs) — MIT, `cpp`).** Single-header native `dr_wav` / `dr_flac` / `dr_mp3` audio decoders for Lime, handy for importing `.wav`/`.flac`/`.mp3` audio directly into desktop editors and chart converters (§31.4). Effort: S–M.
- **[P3] `utest` ([`haxe-utest/utest`](https://github.com/haxe-utest/utest) — MIT, all targets incl. `--interp`).** Lightweight unit/async assertion and fixture runner compatible with `haxe --interp`, complementing Phoenix's Haxe-only engine spec requirement (`tests/haxe/*Spec.hx` in `AGENTS.md` §7). Effort: S.

### 33.4. Libraries Explicitly Ruled Out & Integration Rules

- **Skip `polymod` ([`larsiusprime/polymod`](https://github.com/larsiusprime/polymod)):** Overlaps Phoenix's `Mods` + `Paths` + `ModZipInstaller` + `PlatformSupport` pipeline; implement the narrow `_polymod_meta.json` folder importer from §29 instead of hooking OpenFL asset libraries.
- **Skip `extension-androidtools` / `hxCodec` / `extension-webm`:** Redundant with `quack.fnf.phoenix.android.*` Java/JNI extensions (`source/android/`) and `hxvlc`.
- **Integration checklist (`AGENTS.md` §8 rule 7):** Every added library must be (1) pinned in `hmm.json`, (2) added across all three setup script dialects (`setup/windows.{bat,ps1,sh}` and `setup/unix.{sh,ps1,bat}`) so `python3 tools/check_dependencies.py` passes, and (3) gated in `project.hxp` (`configureHaxelibs()`) behind a `FeatureFlag` (and `isCPP()` when native-only) so `html5` and compact CI builds keep compiling cleanly.


---

## 34. CHEESE ENGINE (`whenkenas/Cheese-Engine`) PARITY — DIFFERENCES NOT YET IMPLEMENTED

**Source:** [whenkenas/Cheese-Engine](https://github.com/whenkenas/Cheese-Engine) at `368242a` ("xddmain adsasdasdaskdpoasd", 2026-07-19), `gitVersion.txt` = `1.61`. A Psych Engine fork by **Chris** (whenkenas) whose stated goal is "an engine with V Slice + Codename + Psych Engine features, and trying to fix bugs!" — it is *not* an upstream Psych fork, it is a third-party fork layered on top of Psych Engine (upstream credit: ShadowMario, Riveren, plus bbpanzu / crowplexus / KadeDev / SqirraRNG / MAJigsaw77 / superpowers04 / CheemsAndFriends and others).

**License:** Apache 2.0 — **identical to Phoenix's**, so ports are license-compatible. Per the rule at the top of this file, anything ported from here must ship **enabled by default with a toggle**, and must carry **explicit credit to `whenkenas` / Cheese Engine** (and to the upstream Psych authors where the code is really theirs).

**Method:** full clone + read of `source/` (7,929 lines in `backend/`, 7,973 in `psychlua/`, 7,420-line `ChartingState.hx`, 3,345-line `StageEditorState.hx`, 3,242 in `backend/ui/`), diffed against this tree. The Lua/HScript API surface was extracted mechanically from both engines (`Lua_helper.add_callback` vs `Convert.addCallback` / `registerFunction` / `python.set`) and compared as sets: **Cheese registers 300 script callbacks, Phoenix 380, and 77 exist only in Cheese** — every one re-verified against this tree to rule out a renamed equivalent before it was listed here.

**Section numbering:** this was drafted as §27 while main already carried a §27 (the Mixtape Engine review, `383c761c`), and was renumbered each time a sibling audit landed on main (now **§34**). Overlap with the earlier sections is cross-referenced rather than repeated: the per-mod `settings.json` + `getModSetting()` contract is **§27.1.1's** item, and §34 only records Cheese as a second reference for it. The Joalor64 Engine Rewrite audit merged alongside this one moved to **§35**.

**Scope filter applied (per request):** engine/mod-specific **content** is excluded — Cheese's week-specific work (the per-week "Hey!" animations, Dad Battle spotlight, Philly Glow, Kill Henchmen, hair physics, Christmas-tree bopping, Thorns HUD hiding, the spooky background girls), its character/song art, and its "at least one change to every week" pitch. Those are not engine features. What remains below is engine-level: **modding APIs, scriptable state control, editor tooling, options/preferences, UI systems, and platform features.** Cheese's `defaultEvents` list is treated the same way — the *specific* events (`Dadbattle Spotlight`, `Philly Glow`, `Kill Henchmen`, `BG Freaks Expression`, `Trigger BG Ghouls`) are week content and are **not** ported; only the *generic* engine events are (§34.3).

### 34.1 SCRIPTING API GAPS — the biggest win

**~70 Cheese-only script callbacks (77 by raw set difference), verified absent from Phoenix.** Most are small and mechanical; the whole set is a few days of work and closes a real "my mod can't do X" gap. All must be registered in **all three languages** (Lua / Python / HScript) via the existing `psychlua/callbacks/*` split, since Phoenix's whole identity is cross-language parity.

The count is a *raw* set difference (Cheese 300 callbacks vs Phoenix 380, mechanically extracted from both trees), so it **over-states** the true gap by the handful of names Phoenix already provides under a different name. Those renames were checked by hand and are called out where they matter: `openCustomSubstate` / `closeCustomSubstate` / `insertToCustomSubstate` exist in Phoenix as `switchCustomState` / `openCustomSubstate` / `closeCustomSubstate` / `insertToCustomSubstate` / `openModSubState` / `customStateExists` (`psychlua/CustomSubstate.hx`, all three languages — see §34.2), `removeFromGroup` exists but `addToGroup` does not, and the achievement callbacks (`unlockAchievement`, `getAchievementScore`, `setAchievementScore`, `addAchievementScore`, `isAchievementUnlocked`, `achievementExists`) all exist in Phoenix via `backend/Achievements.hx:277-329` under `Convert.addCallback` / `python.set` — the raw diff missed them because Phoenix registers them outside `psychlua/`.

- **[P1] Camera read/write API.** `getCameraZoom`, `setCameraZoom`, `getCameraScroll(X/Y)`, `setCameraScroll(X/Y)`, `getCameraScrollRawX/Y`, `getCameraFollowX/Y`, `setCameraFollowPoint`, `addCameraFollowPoint`, `addCameraScroll`. Phoenix can tween cameras but a script cannot *read* the live zoom/scroll/follow point, nor set a follow point directly. This is the single most-requested capability in the group. Note Phoenix already has camera *shader* setters (`setCameraShaderFloat` etc. exist in both) — the gap is the camera transform itself, not the shaders.
- **[P1] State navigation API.** `switchState`, `switchStateDirect`, `openSubState`, `closeSubState`, `resetState`. Phoenix **already has** the script-state half of this — `psychlua/CustomSubstate.hx` registers `switchCustomState`, `openCustomSubstate`, `closeCustomSubstate`, `insertToCustomSubstate`, `openModSubState` and `customStateExists` for Lua, Python and HScript (see §34.2 for what is still missing there). What is genuinely absent is the **engine-screen** navigation: a script cannot `switchState("FreeplayState")`, `openSubState("GameOverSubstate")` or `closeSubState()` — there is no callback that takes a state *name* and drives `FlxG.switchState` / `FlxG.state.openSubState` / `FlxG.state.closeSubState`. `resetState` (a full `FlxG.resetState()`) is also missing. Cheese's `switchState` / `openSubState` / `closeSubState` are exactly this, and they are what a "mod replaces the Freeplay screen" workflow needs. Note the security angle: these must go through the existing `backend/ScriptSecurity` policy, because switching to an arbitrary state is a much bigger capability than a property setter.
- **[P1] Group & instance manipulation API.** `addToGroup`, `addInstance`, `createInstance`, `callMethod`, `callMethodFromClass`, `instanceArg`, `addBehindBar`. Phoenix has `removeFromGroup` (so the remove half exists) but **not** `addToGroup` — an odd asymmetry: a script can take an object out of a group but cannot put one in, so a mod cannot place its own object into the HUD, a strumline or a stage group. `addInstance` / `createInstance` / `callMethod` / `callMethodFromClass` / `instanceArg` are a general reflection surface; Phoenix deliberately has a narrow, safe scripting surface, so if this is added it must go through `backend/ScriptSecurityScanner` / `backend/ModSecurity` so a mod cannot call arbitrary engine methods. **Do not** add these as unguarded reflection. `addBehindBar` adds an object to the **loading screen's** behind-bar layer, which is the missing hook for custom loading art.
- **[P2] Global variable & save access.** `getVar`, `setVar`, `getSave`, `setSave`, `flushSave`. Phoenix's `PlayState.variables` map is reachable only from HScript; a mod in Lua/Python cannot read/write a shared global or poke the save blob directly. `getSave`/`setSave`/`flushSave` are thin wrappers over `FlxG.save` and are safe to add behind the existing `SaveDataCallbacks` policy.
- **[P2] Instance-returning helpers.** `instanceArg`, `addInstance`, `createInstance`, `callMethod`, `callMethodFromClass` — a general reflection surface (`callMethod` invokes a method on a named object). Phoenix deliberately has a narrow, safe scripting surface; if this is added, it must go through the existing `ScriptSecurityScanner` / `ModSecurity` policy in `backend/` so a mod cannot call arbitrary engine methods. **Do not** add these as unguarded reflection.
- **[P2] FlxAnimate symbol API.** `makeFlxAnimateSprite`, `loadAnimateAtlas`, `addAnimationBySymbol`, `addAnimationBySymbolIndices`. Phoenix has `Paths.loadAnimateAtlas` and the `flxanimate` haxelib, but **no script callback for symbol-based animation** — a mod cannot add a symbol animation to a character atlas at runtime. `addAnimationBySymbolIndices` additionally accepts a comma-string of indices.
- **[P2] Colour & math helpers.** `getColorFromName`, `getColorFromString`, `getColorFromHex` (Phoenix has `getColorFromHex` only), `lerp`, `flxLerp`, `flxRandomInt`, `flxRandomFloat`, `flxRandomBool`. The `flx*` aliases are the Flixel-flavoured names; Phoenix uses unprefixed `getRandomInt` etc. Provide **both** spellings so ports of Cheese mods work unmodified.
- **[P2] Sound pitch & music state.** `getSoundPitch`, `setSoundPitch`, `isMusicPlaying`, `getCurrentSong`, `songExists`. Phoenix can play/pause/fade sounds but cannot **pitch-shift** a playing sound from a script — a notable gap for a rhythm game (sound-test pitch, "tape" effects, speed-run audio).
- **[P2] Score & highscore read API.** `getHighscore`, `getHighscoreMisses`, `getHighscoreRating`, `updateScoreText`. Phoenix's `backend/Highscore.hx` stores these but exposes no script callback; a "stats overlay" mod cannot read them.
- **[P2] Song/difficulty metadata read API.** `getDifficulties`, `getDifficultyName`, `getSongDifficulties`, `getSongsFromWeek`, `getDifficulties`. A mod cannot enumerate what the base game ships — needed for "random song from all difficulties" mods and for building custom freeplay screens.
- **[P2] Locale/translation script API.** `getTranslationPhrase`, `setTranslationPhrase`, `getFileTranslation`, `setFileTranslation`. Phoenix has **no translation system at all** — there is no `backend/Language.hx`, no phrase table and no `.lang` support; the only trace is `ClientPrefs.locale` (a locale *string* pref, `backend/ClientPrefs.hx:63`), with no engine behind it. This is therefore not "add a script callback to an existing translator" but the **tail end** of the localisation work §12.4 lists as a **[P3]** ("Localization / translations — no language key system or locale files"). Cheese's `backend/Language.hx` (127 lines) + its `.lang` file convention (§34.5) is a small, self-contained shape to adopt; add the script callbacks **after** the translator exists, not before.
- **[P3] Mod preference read API.** `getModSetting` — lets a mod read a value from the mod-settings menu so its own options screen and its gameplay code agree. Pairs with the mod-settings screen in §34.5. **Already covered by §27.1.1** (Mixtape's per-mod `settings.json` contract) — this is a cross-reference, not new work; implement it once, there.
- **[P3] Text box sizing API.** `setTextAutoSize`, `setTextHeight`. `setTextHeight` sets `FlxText.fieldHeight` directly; Phoenix's `TextCallbacks` has `setTextSize`/`setTextWidth` but not the height field.
- **[P3] Hold-cover property API.** `getHoldCoverProperty`, `setHoldCoverProperty`, `updateHoldCoverHitbox`. Lets a mod restyle the hold cover (the cap sprite on sustains) at runtime. Phoenix ships hold covers but does not let scripts mutate them.
- **[P3] Asset-loading progress API.** `getLoaded`, `getLoadMax` — exposes the loading-screen counters so a loading-bar mod can render its own progress instead of the engine's.
- **[P3] Score text refresh.** `updateScoreText` — forces the HUD score label to re-render after a script changed the score directly.
- **[P3] Build-target introspection.** `getBuildTarget` — a script can tell whether it is running on desktop/Android/web. Useful for platform-conditional mods (e.g. skip touch-only UI on desktop).
- **[P3] Script-host plumbing.** `setOnHScript`, `setOnLuas`, `setOnScripts`, `callOnHScript` — invoke a named function across every loaded script of a given language. Phoenix loads many scripts at once but has no "broadcast to all my scripts" primitive, which is how a mod does "pause everything" or "apply a theme".

### 34.2 SCRIPTED STATES — Cheese's headline feature, and where Phoenix actually stands

**Cheese's implementation.** Cheese ships `backend/HScriptStateLoader.hx` (`HScriptState`) and `psychlua/LuaStateLoader.hx` (`LuaState`, `LuaStateLoader`, `LuaStateScript`, `LuaStateCustomSubstate`), wired through `backend/StateManager.switchState()`: a mod can drop `data/states/<Name>.hx` **or `data/states/<Name>.lua`** into its folder and the engine will **run that file as a whole screen** (its own `create` / `update` / `stepHit` / `onCloseSubState` hooks, its own camera, its own substate stack) instead of the compiled class. Cheese resolves scripts in this priority: mod-compiled Haxe state → mod HScript state → mod Lua state → engine HScript state → engine Lua state → compiled class → `TitleState` fallback. The README advertises this as "**States in HSCRIPT and LUA**" and points at a `readme.txt` in the mod folder.

**Where Phoenix actually stands (verified, and further along than the headline suggests).** Phoenix already has the *whole* scripting surface Cheese has here, in **all three languages**: `psychlua/CustomSubstate.hx` registers `switchCustomState`, `openCustomSubstate`, `closeCustomSubstate`, `insertToCustomSubstate`, `openModSubState` and `customStateExists` for **Lua, Python and HScript** alike, and `psychlua/StateScripts.hx` backs them with `states/ModState.hx` and `states/substates/ModSubState.hx` — a blank `MusicBeatState` / `MusicBeatSubstate` whose behaviour comes entirely from `data/states/<name>.hx`. `MusicBeatState.loadStateScripts()` / `MusicBeatSubstate.loadStateScripts()` (`backend/MusicBeatState.hx:252-262`, `backend/MusicBeatSubstate.hx:25-35`) load and dispatch the scripts for **every** engine screen, so a mod can already hook any state.

So the remaining gaps are narrow and specific:

- **[P1] Lua/Python state files — the real gap.** `StateScripts.SCRIPT_EXTENSIONS` is `['hx', 'hscript', 'hxs']` (`psychlua/StateScripts.hx:20`) and `StateScripts.load()` is wrapped in `#if HSCRIPT_ALLOWED` (`:151`). So a state can be written in **HScript only**; a **`.lua` or `.py` state file is silently ignored**. Cheese's headline feature is specifically the *Lua* half (`psychlua/LuaStateLoader.hx`, 2,915 lines) and Phoenix has no equivalent. This is the single biggest item in this section: add `.lua` / `.py` to the extensions and give `StateScripts` a per-language loader, so `data/states/MyMenu.lua` becomes a real screen. Phoenix's `psychlua/LuaFunction.hx` / `PythonScript.hx` already have the runtime pieces; the missing part is the **state-level** wiring (the `LuaState` / `LuaStateScript` equivalent of `states/ModState.hx`).
- **[P1] The script screen must be able to *be* an engine screen.** Phoenix's `ModState` **replaces** a screen wholesale (it is a blank state that runs the script's hooks), which is the right model — but there is no way for a script to **replace `MainMenuState` itself**. Cheese can: `StateManager.switchState("MainMenuState")` finds the mod's HScript/Lua file and runs *that* instead of the compiled class. Phoenix's `StateScripts` are loaded as *hooks on top of* whichever state is running (`MusicBeatState.loadStateScripts()` is called by the real `MainMenuState`), so a mod can hook the main menu but not **replace** it. §0's "expose almost ALL game screens through the Modding API" needs the replace case, not just the hook case. Design note: a *replace* must not silently break the game's own `MainMenuState` internals (the Phoenix main menu has engine behaviour a mod must be able to call into) — a hybrid "script state that can call the original" is the safe shape.
- **[P2] Script screen resolution order across mod folders.** Phoenix's `StateScripts.collectFiles()` (`:76-100`) already searches `assets/preload/data/states/` → **every global mod** → `mods/data/states/` → the active mod's `data/states/`, across all three extensions, and `load()` runs *every* file it finds. Cheese instead picks **one** script by priority. Phoenix's "run all of them" is arguably better for stacking mods, but it means a mod cannot *replace* another mod's state — the two models need to be reconciled deliberately, not by accident.
- **[P2] Compiled Haxe state inside a mod.** Cheese's `StateManager.getStateClass()` first tries `states.mods.<sanitizedModName>.<StateName>` — a **compiled Haxe class shipped inside a mod** — then the engine's `states.<StateName>`, then `options.<StateName>`. Phoenix resolves compiled mod states differently; check `source/headers/States.hx` before porting so the two do not conflict. The *sanitised-name* rule (spaces, `.`, `!`, `-`, `'`, `&` → `_`) is worth copying verbatim — it is what makes a mod folder name usable as a Haxe package segment.
- **[P3] Script-side "return state" plumbing.** Cheese stores a `returnState` so a script screen can hand control back where it came from; Phoenix's `StateScripts.switchState(name, data)` passes an opaque `data` blob instead, which covers the common case but not "remember and go back".
- **[P3] Scripted state *transitions*.** Cheese's `backend/CustomFadeTransition.hx` is a **gradient wipe** transition (a `FlxGradient` sliding over a black bar, with a `finishCallback`) that a script can open and chain.
