Phoenix's transitions are the Flixel defaults plus `backend/FlxTransWindow.hx`; there is no script-callable custom transition. §12.1 already lists "scripted transitions" as a missing script scope — Cheese's is a concrete, small implementation to draw from.

### 34.3 CHART EVENTS — the generic verbs Cheese has that Phoenix lacks

**Both engines already have chart-embedded events.** This is not a "build it from scratch" item. Phoenix stores them in `songJson.events` (`data/Song.hx:17`, `:52`, with a legacy migration from negative-lane notes at `:78-104`), loads them into `PlayState.eventNotes:Array<EventNote>` (`play/PlayState.hx:238`, via `play/helpers/PlayStateChartLoader.hx:425`), sorts them by time (`PlayStateChartLoader.hx:376`) and dispatches them in `play/PlayState.hx:2732-2741` via `triggerEventNote(event, value1, value2, strumTime)` — the **same `[time, [[name, value1, value2]]]` shape** Cheese uses. The dispatcher lives in `play/helpers/PlayStateEvents.hx` (496 lines).

**Measured delta.** Phoenix implements **26** event names; Cheese implements **28**. The set difference (`phx` vs `chz`, both extracted from source) is:

- **In Cheese but not Phoenix — 15 names.** `Target Camera`, `Target Follow Pos`, `Flash Camera`, `Play Sound`, `Video Player`, the four skin swaps (`Change Note Skin`, `Change NoteStrum Skin`, `Change Hold Cover Skin`, `Change Note Splash Skin`), and the three `(STEPS)` variants (`(STEPS) Set Cam Zoom`, `(STEPS) Target Camera`, `(STEPS) Target Follow Pos`). One more name in the raw diff, `Set Cam Zoom`, is **already covered** — Phoenix handles both spellings in one `case` (`PlayStateEvents.hx:273`: `case 'Set Camera Zoom', 'Set Cam Zoom'`).
- **In Phoenix but not Cheese — 16 names.** `Camera Bopping`, `Camera Twist`, `Change Note Multiplier`, `Change Song Name`, `Credits Popup`, `Enable Bot Energy`, `Disable Bot Energy`, `Set Bot Energy Speeds`, `Enable Camera Bop`, `Disable Camera Bop`, `Fake Song Length`, `Popup`, `Popup (No Pause)`, `Rainbow Eyesore`, `Tween Camera Zoom`, `Windows Notification`.

So **Phoenix is already ahead on the event set** (26 vs 28, and ahead on the ones that are not in Cheese at all). The gap is not "more events" — it is **the specific verbs below, plus the authoring side**.

- **[P1] `Target Camera` and `Target Follow Pos` — camera *locking*.** Phoenix has `Camera Follow Pos` (`PlayStateEvents.hx:335`) and `Set/Add Camera Zoom` (`:273`, `:294`), but **no event that moves the camera to a target**. Cheese's `Target Camera` takes a target (a character, a strumline index, or an absolute `X, Y`) **optionally offset** (`BF, 300`), plus a tween spec (`easeName, duration`) and the rule that it **overrides `mustHitSection` until the tween completes**; `Target Follow Pos` **locks** the camera follow point to a target or position and **stays locked until cleared with an empty value**. This is the "lock the camera onto the singer for a verse" authoring feature, and the lock/unlock semantics are the interesting part: without a lock, every section change snaps the camera back.
- **[P2] `Flash Camera`.** A full-screen colour flash as a chart event: duration + a **named or hex colour** (`white`, `black`, `red`, `green`, `blue`, `yellow`, `cyan`, `magenta`, `purple`, `orange`, `pink`, `lime`, `gray`, `brown`). Phoenix has camera flashes at runtime (`backend/CameraFlash.hx`, and `cameraFlash` is exposed to scripts) but **not as a chart event**, so a chart cannot script its own flash timing.
- **[P2] `Play Sound` as a chart event.** Name + volume (0–1). Phoenix's `play/helpers/PlayStatePlayback.hx:422` already iterates `state.eventNotes`, so the dispatch point exists — the handler does not.
- **[P2] The four skin-swap events.** `Change Note Skin`, `Change NoteStrum Skin`, `Change Hold Cover Skin`, `Change Note Splash Skin` — each takes a skin name/path and a target of **BF / opponent / both**. These are the ones most mods want *without writing a line of code*: today Phoenix can swap the note skin only through the Options pref (`ClientPrefs.noteSkin`), not per-section from the chart. Note the interaction with §34.1's missing hold-cover property API — the hold-cover swap needs `get/setHoldCoverProperty` from the same change.
- **[P2] `Video Player` as a chart event.** Plays a video on a named camera (`game` / `hud` / `other`) and layer, with four flags: can-skip, mid-song, loop, play-on-load. Phoenix has `objects/VideoSprite.hx` and the hxvlc video support, and `backend/` video plumbing, so the runtime exists; the *chart-event* trigger does not.
- **[P3] The `(STEPS)` camera variants.** `Set Cam Zoom`, `Target Camera` and `Target Follow Pos` each have a second form whose tween duration is expressed in **steps** instead of seconds, so a camera move lands on the beat grid. This is a genuinely better authoring model than seconds and is cheap to add once the base camera events exist (a step count × the section's beat length = the duration).
- **[P3] Cheese's camera-lock semantics as a spec.** Worth copying verbatim when implementing `Target Camera` / `Target Follow Pos`: the camera **stays locked** until the event is cleared with an empty value or a `Target Camera` event overrides it, and the override wins only until its tween completes. Phoenix's camera is otherwise driven by `mustHitSection` every section, so the lock needs an explicit override flag on the camera owner — that is the actual implementation work, not the event handler.

**Authoring side — Phoenix is already ahead.** Phoenix's chart editor has a full **Events tab** (`editors/ChartingState.hx:518`, UI built by `editors/charting/ChartingUISections.hx:1259 addEventsUI`), and it is *more* capable than Cheese's in one specific way:

- Phoenix's Events tab discovers **custom event scripts** from a `custom_events/` folder — scanned across the base `mods/custom_events/`, the active mod's `mods/<mod>/custom_events/` and **every global mod's** `custom_events/` (`ChartingUISections.hx:1268-1294`), each `.txt` file becoming an event whose **file content is the event description** (`state.eventStuff.push([fileToCheck, File.getContent(path)])`, shown in `state.descText`). Cheese hard-codes its event list in `ChartingState.defaultEvents` with the descriptions inline. **Phoenix's mechanism is the better one** — a mod can ship a new event without touching the engine — and it should be the model for anything added here.
- Phoenix has an **event dropdown** (`eventDropDown:CustomFlxUIDropDownMenu`, `editors/ChartingState.hx:934`), **value 1 / value 2 input fields** wired into the event data (`ChartingState.hx:1181-1190`: `curSelectedNote[1][curEventSelected][1] = value1InputText.text`), **multi-event selection within one event note** (`curEventSelected`, `ChartingEvents.changeEventSelected`), event notes in the grid (`ChartingUIGrid.hx:231-236` reads `eventName` / `eventVal1` / `eventVal2`), and the `EventNote` type itself (`objects/Note.hx:14`, with the fields at `:108-111`).
- Phoenix's `EventNote` and the runtime `songJson.events` shape already match Cheese's, so the *event data model* is done.

So the authoring gaps are narrow, and they are about **ergonomics and the missing verbs**, not about building the UI:

- **[P2] Event column toggle and event locking in the grid.** Cheese's `SHOW_EVENT_COLUMN` (a static toggle, `states/editors/ChartingState.hx:111`) and `lockedEvents` let the author collapse the event column out of the grid and lock it while placing notes. Phoenix's editor shows events in the grid (`editors/charting/ChartingUIGrid.hx:231-236`) but has no equivalent hide/lock switch — add it as a toggle in the new View tab.
- **[P2] Per-event special-case input widgets.** Cheese changes the input widgets per event (a FlxEase-name dropdown for the tween events, a boolean selector, a colour-name field for `Flash Camera`, a video-options field for `Video Player`). Phoenix has a generic "value 1 / value 2" text pair. A generic pair is the right minimum; per-event widgets become worth it only once the tween/camera/flash events in the list above exist.
- **[P3] Codename-event import compatibility.** `backend/CodenameEventCompat.hx` converts Codename's `{time, name, params}` event array into the engine's `[time, [name, ...params]]` shape, sorting by time and grouping same-time events. Phoenix has no such importer, so CNE charts with events cannot be loaded at all. This is an **importer**, not a runtime feature — it belongs with §12.2's "CNE song folder layout" work. Phoenix's `data/Song.hx:78-104` already has a legacy event migration, so the target shape exists.
- **[P3] Codename character import compatibility.** `backend/CodenameCharacterCompat.hx` reads CNE's XML character format (including the `icon` attribute) into the engine's character shape. Same story: importer, belongs with §12.2.

### 34.4 EDITORS & TOOLING

- **[P1] Chart-editor toys / character stage-posers.** Cheese's `ChartingState` has a `toyGroup` of `Toy` objects (GF, BF, and a pixel-BF toy) that can be dragged around the editor to **pose the stage characters while charting**, and the pose is **replayed live in the chart preview** (`gfToy.performPose(animName, sustainLength, noteColor)`) — the editor's live preview shows GF and BF actually hitting the pose the author just dragged them into. This is a genuinely good authoring tool: it removes the "change the stage JSON, reload, guess" loop. Phoenix has `editors/EditorPlayState.hx` and `ChartingUIWaveform` but no interactive stage poser. Port as a **toy/poser layer** in the editor grid, on by default, with a toggle in the editor's View menu.
- **[P1] Discover note types from `custom_notetypes/` instead of a hardcoded list.** This is a smaller and sharper version of what Cheese does, and it is a real gap:
  - Phoenix's editor note-type dropdown is populated from a **hardcoded static array**, `editors/ChartingState.hx:71-81` (`noteTypeList` = `''`, `Alt Animation`, `Hey!`, `Hurt Note`, `GF Sing`, `No Animation`, `Cross Fade`, `GF Cross Fade`) — the comment even says *"Used for backwards compatibility with 0.1 - 0.3.2 charts, though, you should add your hardcoded custom note types here too"*. The dropdown is then built from it (`ChartingUISections.hx:994-998`).
  - But Phoenix already ships **11 real custom note types as Lua scripts** in `example_mods/custom_notetypes/` (`Bad Poison`, `Bomb Note`, `Boo Note`, `Bullet Bill`, `Bullet`, `Coin Note`, `Invisible Note`, `Jumpscare Note`, `Poison Note`, `Ring Note`, `Water Note`), and `backend/NoteTypesConfig.hx` already knows how to parse and apply a note-type property file (`loadNoteTypeData` `:17`, `applyNoteTypeData` `:61`).
  - The gap: the **editor cannot assign any of them**. The runtime can apply a note type, but the author cannot pick one from the dropdown, because the dropdown only knows the 8 hardcoded names. So the feature is half-built and effectively unusable from the editor.
  - Cheese does the scan: `states/editors/ChartingState.hx:5083` loads `custom_notetypes/` from the mod folders (`loadFileList('custom_notetypes/', exts)`) and merges those with its built-in defaults, and `backend/NoteTypesConfig.hx` parses `custom_notetypes/<name>.txt` into `property.path = value` assignments applied to a `Note` by reflection (including array indexing — `extraData.foo = 1` — and dotted paths, with `:` **and** `=` separators, and typed values: string / bool / null / int / float, via `_interpretValue`).
  - So: make the editor dropdown **discover** `custom_notetypes/` across the base mods folder, the active mod and every global mod (the same folder list the Events tab already uses for `custom_events/`, `ChartingUISections.hx:1268-1274`), merge with `noteTypeList` for backwards compatibility, and make sure `backend/NoteTypesConfig` is pointed at the same discovery list. This makes 11 shipped note types usable, and makes third-party ones usable for the first time.
- **[P2] Fine strum-time editing is already there — but it is a text field, not a nudge.** Phoenix's Note tab has `strumTimeInputText` (`editors/ChartingState.hx:914`, built at `ChartingUISections.hx:987`), a free-text **millisecond** timestamp for the selected note or event, with a tooltip saying exactly that (`ChartingUISections.hx:989`). So the "millisecond precision" authoring Cheese advertises is **already implemented** in Phoenix, and in a more flexible form (free text) than Cheese's stepper. Cheese's `strumTimeStepper` (`states/editors/ChartingState.hx:4570`) is the *nudge-by-step* variant. The gap is therefore only the **stepper** form — a +/- nudge that moves the selected note by a fixed step (e.g. 1 ms / 5 ms / 1 beat) without retyping the number — which is a small ergonomic addition on top of the existing field, and a better fit for a touch screen than typing milliseconds.
- **[P2] Decimal BPM is already supported — no action.** `data/Song.hx:18` and `:53` declare `var bpm:Float` / `public var bpm:Float`, and the editor's BPM steppers are float-valued (`ChartingUISections.hx:462` `CustomFlxUINumericStepper(..., 1, Conductor.bpm, 0, 999999, 1)`). Cheese's "BPM can now have decimal values" is already true here. Listed only so the audit record shows it was checked and deliberately dropped.

- **[P2] Chart editor "View" menu.** Cheese's editor has a `PsychUIBox` with `['File', 'Edit', 'View', 'Chart']` tabs; the **View** tab holds the toys toggle and other display toggles. Phoenix's editor has a different tab layout (`Song`, `Section`, `Note`, `Events`, `Charting`, `Data`) and no View tab, so the display-only toggles (toys, show note-type labels, event column, event lock) have no home. The **View** tab is the natural container for the two new toggles this section proposes — the toy/poser layer (§34.4 toys) and the event column show/lock — so add the tab once and hang both off it.
- **[P2] Event-column toggle + event locking in the grid.** `SHOW_EVENT_COLUMN` and `lockedEvents` let the author collapse the event column out of the grid and lock it while placing notes.
- **[P2] Editor picker with icons.** Cheese's `states/editors/EditorPickerSubstate.hx` is a **graphical editor chooser** (a list of editors, each with an icon) instead of a text menu; it explicitly credits Codename's team for the idea. Phoenix's `editors/MasterEditorMenu.hx` is a plain text list. Port the icon-bearing picker (the icons are Cheese's/Codename's art — redraw or use Phoenix's own, do not copy the PNGs without checking their licence).
- **[P3] Stage editor that exports Lua.** Cheese's `StageEditorState` (3,345 lines) can **export the stage it is editing as a Lua script** (`generateLuaScript()` emits `makeLuaSprite`, `setBlendMode`, `setScrollFactor`, `scaleObject`, `setProperty` calls into `function onCreate()`), and can load a stage back from an existing Lua file (`loadLuaStageIfExists`). It also exposes a "Name (for Lua/HScript)" field, i.e. the editor output is *addressable by name* from scripts. Phoenix has **no stage editor at all** (already noted in §12.3) — but the **Lua export** and the **named-stage addressability** are separable, cheap, and valuable, and can be added on top of whatever stage editor gets built. The README also says Haxe and JSON export are planned there, which is exactly the direction §12.2 wants.
- **[P3] Note-splash editor and hold-cover editor.** Cheese has `NoteSplashEditorState` and `HoldCoverEditorState`; Phoenix has `editors/NoteSplashDebugState.hx` (a debug view, not an editor). The hold-cover editor in particular pairs with the §34.1 hold-cover property API.

### 34.5 OPTIONS, PREFERENCES & UI

- **[P1] Mod settings screen driven by the mod.** Cheese's `options/ModSettingsSubState.hx` reads a mod's own option list and renders it as a real options menu, storing each value in a per-mod map (`FlxG.save.data.modSettings[folder]`), with full **keybind** support inside it (a mod can ship a KEYBIND option and have the engine's key rebinder handle it).
  > **Duplicate of §27.1.1 (Mixtape) — do not implement twice.** §27.1.1 already specifies this exact feature from the Psych 1.0 / Mixtape side: the same commented-out button in `ModsMenuState`, the same missing `options/ModSettingsSubState.hx`, the same `FlxG.save.data.modSettings[folder]` save map, the same `getModSetting()` callback, and the same "keybind support" requirement. Cheese's implementation is a **second independent reference** for the same spec and is worth reading alongside §27.1.1's Mixtape one, but the work item itself is §27.1.1's. This entry is kept only as the cross-reference. Note also §21's "Per-mod settings profiles" ([TODO.md](TODO.md)), which is a *different* feature (per-mod engine preferences, not mod-defined options) and must not be conflated with either.
  **Phoenix has the plumbing but the feature is switched off.** `states/ModsMenuState.hx:308-325` contains the settings button and the `openSubState(new ModSettingsSubState(curMod.settings, curMod.folder, curMod.name))` call **entirely inside a `/* ... */` block labelled `// not yet`**, and the import at `states/ModsMenuState.hx:19` is likewise commented out (`// import options.ModSettingsSubState;`). The `ModSettingsSubState` class **does not exist** in the tree. Separately, `ModsMenuState.hx:934-950` has the loader for `mods/<folder>/data/settings.json` also commented out — but `ModItem.settings:Array<Dynamic>` (`:925`) and `ModItem.pack` (`:927`) are still declared, and `ModItem.new` still calls `Mods.getPack(folder)`, so the data side is half-there.
  So the work is: (a) write `states/substates/ModSettingsSubState.hx` (Cheese's is a fair reference — it also renders the option's own `description` string, which is what makes a mod's settings screen readable); (b) uncomment/replace the `data/settings.json` loader in `ModItem.new`; (c) uncomment the button and the import, keeping the "not yet" placement — the button should only be enabled when the selected mod actually has settings, as the commented code already does; (d) add the per-mod save map (`FlxG.save.data.modSettings[folder]`) — note Phoenix's save is one monolithic blob (§13), so this needs the namespace discipline §13 asks for.
  This is the natural home for the `getModSetting` script callback in §34.1, and it is the mechanism that makes the "every ported feature must have a toggle" rule in this file enforceable **by the mod author** — a mod can expose its own toggles instead of shipping a wiki page.
- **[P2] Per-mod window colour & theme override.** Cheese reads `pack.json`'s `windowColor` (string or array) and applies it to the native window (`Main.hx:574-597`), with `ClientPrefs.allowModWindowColor` gating it, plus `windowTheme` (`PC Theme` / other) and `uiTheme`. Phoenix already has the **primitives** — `backend/WindowColorMode.hx` exposes `setWindowBorderColor`, `setWindowTitleColor`, `setDarkMode`/`setLightMode` — but nothing ever calls them from a mod: `backend/Mods.getPack()` parses `pack.json` via TJSON and returns the raw object, and no code path reads a `windowColor` key out of it. So the work is small: read `windowColor` from the active mod's pack, feed it to the existing `WindowColorMode` calls, and re-apply on mod switch. Worth upgrading §12.6's "Mod-supplied window title/icon" **[P3]** to a **P1** with the colour included.
- **[P2] Mod-supplied window icon and EXE name.** Cheese's `backend/ModSelectorSubstate.hx:316-363` sets the native window icon from the active mod's `pack.png` (falling back to `icon.png`), and the README advertises "Custom EXE Icon & Name — Edit with pack.png and pack.json". Phoenix's icon is a compile-time asset set once in `Main.hx:261` (`Lib.current.stage.window.setIcon(Image.fromFile(iconPath))`) and never changes with the active mod. Extends §12.6's "mod-supplied window title/icon" item with the actual mechanism — the `setIcon` call already exists, it just needs to be re-run with the mod's `pack.png` when the mod changes.
- **[P2] Detailed ranking HUD overlay.** Cheese's `ClientPrefs.data.detailedRanking` adds a live HUD text block in `PlayState` (`Note Hits / Sicks / Goods / Bads / Shits`) with a `vcr.ttf` format, toggleable in Options → Visuals. It is a **five-line diagnostics overlay**, not a new scoring system — the README's "Detailed RANKING" is marketing for exactly this.
  Phoenix has every counter it would display — `play/PlayState.hx:315-317` (`sicks` / `goods` / `bads`), `:471` (`songHits`) and `play/helpers/PlayStateRating.hx` (`perfects` / `poorRatings`) — but **no overlay**: there is no `detailedRanking` symbol anywhere in the tree. So this is a cheap, self-contained HUD addition over existing counters, and it is a good companion to Phoenix's existing `debug/` overlays. It should be a pref (`ClientPrefs.detailedRanking`, default **off**) with an Options → Visuals toggle, and it must respect the existing `hideHud` pref the way Cheese's does (`PlayState.hx:721` in Cheese gates the text on `!hideHud && detailedRanking`).
- **[P2] In-game TAB mod switcher with per-mod modes.** Cheese opens `backend/ModSelectorSubstate.hx` from `MainMenuState` on **TAB**, and the selector offers **four modes** beyond "one mod at a time": each installed mod, plus **`MODS + FNF SONGS`**, **`ALL MODS`**, and **`DISABLE MODS`** — i.e. mods can be *layered* over the base game rather than replacing it, and the base game can be kept alongside them. The substate also renders a per-mod icon.
  Phoenix §12.3 lists "In-game TAB mod switcher" as a **[P3]** and §12.6 lists the **addon system with load priority** as a **[P1]** — Cheese's mod-mode selector is a concrete, cheap UI for a first slice of that: the "MODS + FNF SONGS" / "ALL MODS" / "DISABLE MODS" modes are exactly the "mods layered over base game" case, and Phoenix already has `pack.runsGlobally` (global mods) so the machinery is partly there. Note the priority inversion: §12.6 wants the *addon priority system*, Cheese offers the *mode picker* — do the picker first, the priority rules after.
  Implementation notes for this tree: Phoenix currently **blocks TAB globally** (`states/InitState.hx:32` — `FlxG.keys.preventDefaultKeys = [TAB]`), and TAB is already bound in `states/AchievementsMenuState.hx:321` and `states/StatsState.hx:198`, so the new binding must be scoped to the Main Menu and must not steal TAB from those screens. The switcher also needs to be reachable on **touch** platforms, where TAB does not exist — Phoenix's `mobile/` input layer has no TAB equivalent, so the mod switcher needs a touch-reachable entry point as well (the Mods menu button in the main menu is the obvious candidate).
- **[P3] Mirror Chart gameplay changer.** Cheese's `options/GameplayChangersSubstate.hx` has an **`Opponent Mode`** (`opponentmode`) and a **`Mirror Chart`** (`mirrorchart`) toggle — the latter mirrors the player's chart onto the opponent's strumline. Phoenix's `states/helpers/GameplayChangersHelpers.hx` has 24 options and includes `Play as Opponent` (`opponentplay`) and `Play Both Sides` (`bothsides`), but there is **no mirror-chart option** anywhere in the tree (verified: no `mirrorchart` / `opponentmode` symbol exists). Mirroring is not the same as "play as opponent" — the first changes *which notes* the opponent gets, the second changes *which side you control* — so this is a genuine, small, standalone gameplay changer to add next to the existing ones.
- **[P3] UI theme system.** Cheese's `ClientPrefs.data.uiTheme` ('Default' / 'Cheese') is threaded through the whole UI layer (`backend/ui/AnimScrollList.hx`, `backend/ui/PsychUIBox.hx` both branch on `isCheese`). The **theme-switch plumbing** is the portable part: a single `uiTheme` pref read by every UI widget, so a theme can be added without touching each screen. Phoenix has `objects/ui/CustomFlxUI.hx` but no such global theme switch. Port the *mechanism* (a `UiTheme` backend that widgets query), not Cheese's "Cheese" theme art.
- **[P3] Developer-mode shortcut to the editors.** Cheese has `options/DeveloperSettingsSubState.hx` with a `developerMode` flag that enables developer shortcuts (e.g. press 7 in `PlayState` to open the Editor Picker). Phoenix has `options/SuperSecretDebugMenu.hx` and `states/DiagnosticsState.hx`, and its `editors/MasterEditorMenu.hx` is already the editor picker — but there is **no `developerMode` pref** anywhere in the tree (verified) and no in-game shortcut that jumps from `PlayState` into the editor menu. The portable piece is the **shortcut**, gated behind a pref so it cannot fire during normal play.
- **[P3] Note-colour screen — already ported, no action.** Cheese's `options/NotesColorSubState.hx` (716 lines) is a full note-colour editor: colour wheel, colour gradient, colour palette, hex entry, clipboard copy/paste and a live four-lane preview in both normal and pixel modes. Phoenix's `options/NotesSubState.hx` already has **all of it** — `colorWheel` (`:45`, loaded at `:175`), `colorGradient` + `colorGradientSelector` (`:161-166`), `colorPalette` (`:44`), `hexTypeLine`/`hexTypeNum` (`:35-36`), `copyButton`/`pasteButton` (`:39-40`, wired at `:153-159`), `onPixel` (`:32`) and `lime.system.Clipboard` (`:12`). This one is **done**; it is listed here only so the audit record shows it was checked and deliberately dropped, not overlooked.
- **[P3] Language sub-state with a `.lang` discovery flow.** Cheese's `options/LanguageSubState.hx` scans **mod folders** for `*.lang` files (`Mods.directoriesWithFile(Paths.getSharedPath(), 'data/')`), reads each file's first line as the human-readable language name, and builds a language list dynamically. Phoenix's §12.4 lists "Localization / translations" as a **[P3]** open item with no key system; Cheese's **`.lang` file + first-line display name** convention is a cheap, adoptable format — the *whole* localisation feature can be scoped as: (a) a `.lang` file format (first line = display name, then `key = value` pairs), (b) a scanner over the base mods folder + the active mod + every global mod, (c) a `getTranslationPhrase` / `setTranslationPhrase` script API (§34.1), and (d) a language picker sub-state. Cheese's `backend/Language.hx` (127 lines) is a small, self-contained reference for all four parts.

### 34.6 PLATFORM & SYSTEM-LEVEL FEATURES

- **[P2] Sticker transition system.** Cheese's `substates/StickerSubState.hx` implements a **sticker wipe** between states: stickers are chosen per mod from `sounds/stickersounds/keys/*.ogg` (each sticker has its own key sound), a `StickerTransitionSprite` slides in, plays its sound, and hands off to the target state. There are three sticker **modes** (`StickerSubState.stickerMode`, e.g. `'random'`), and the whole thing is scriptable: `switchStateWithStickers(state, mode)` / `switchStateWithStickersByName(name, mode)` exist in **both** Cheese's Lua and HScript APIs. Phoenix has **zero** sticker references in the whole tree. This is a high-impact, self-contained, very "FNF modding"-shaped feature: it is the transition most FNF mods actually want, it is data-driven (drop PNGs + OGGs in the mod), and it is fully script-driven.
- **[P2] Mod-overridable custom sound tray art.** Phoenix §12 claims "custom volume tray" as already present, and it *is* present: `objects/CustomSoundTray.hx` is a hand-drawn `FlxSoundTray` replacement with 11 bar images, a volume box, and the three distinct `Volup` / `Voldown` / `VolMAX` sounds — that part is already a port of Cheese's `backend/FunkinSoundTray.hx`, and it even has the `VolMAX` touch at `:145`.
  The gap is narrower and worth stating precisely: the **sounds** are mod-resolvable (`CustomSoundTray.hx:62-64` uses `Paths.resolveSoundAsset("sounds/soundtray/Volup", null, 'shared')`, so a mod can override them), but the **graphics are hardcoded to engine assets** — `Assets.getBitmapData('assets/soundtray/images/volumebox.png')` (`:25`), `.../bars_10.png` (`:34`) and `.../bars_$i.png` (`:47`) all read the engine's own `assets/` path directly and never consult `Paths.image(...)` / `Paths.mods(...)`. Cheese resolves the *same* images through a per-mod lookup (`backend/FunkinSoundTray.hx:_getImageData` checks the active mod, then every global mod, then the engine) — so a mod in Cheese can restyle the volume popup, and in Phoenix it cannot.
  Fix: route the tray graphics through `Paths.image('soundtray/volumebox', 'shared')` (or equivalent mod-aware resolver) so the existing `Paths.modFolders` override chain applies, and add the tray image/sound folder to the mod-override list. Small, but it is the difference between "the engine drew a tray" and "the mod drew a tray".
- **[P3] Window theme presets.** Cheese sets the window theme (`ClientPrefs.data.windowTheme`, e.g. `'PC Theme'`) from `Main.hx:530-597` via the `hxWindowColorMode` haxelib, on top of the per-mod `windowColor` (§34.5). Phoenix's `backend/WindowColorMode.hx` already provides `setDarkMode()` / `setLightMode()` and the border/title colour setters, but there is **no user-facing theme preset** — nothing in `options/` exposes "PC theme / dark / light" as a choice, and nothing reads a `windowTheme` pref. So the portable piece is a small **Options → Visuals** entry that offers the presets the backend already supports. Low priority, and it pairs with the per-mod colour above.
- **[P3] Advanced FPS counter with memory graphs.** Cheese's `debug/FPSCounter.hx` + `debug/StatsGraph.hx` draw a **live graph** of FPS, **GC memory** and **task memory**, with `gcMem`/`gcMemPeak`/`taskMem`/`taskMemPeak` read from the native layer, in three modes (simple / advanced / "isSimplier"), and it is drawn on a raw `openfl` `Sprite` (not a Flixel object) so it costs nothing.
  Phoenix §12.4 already lists "Advanced FPS counter — 1% lows, selectable/copyable diagnostics, and a true uncapped mode" as a **[P3]**. What Phoenix has today is a **text** FPS readout with a memory *number* (`debug/FPSCounter.hx:160-161` prints `MemoryUtil.formatMemory(memory)` and optionally the peak), plus a separate `debug/RamGraph.hx` (a compact gameplay-only RAM history panel, sampled twice a second). So the gap Cheese fills is narrower than "add an FPS counter": it is (a) a **graph of the FPS curve** rather than a single number, (b) a **separate GC-memory graph** vs Phoenix's task/RAM graph, and (c) the **peak-memory** readouts side by side with current. Phoenix's `debug/mem/GetTotalMemory` and `backend/MemoryLedger.hx` / `backend/MemoryProfiles.hx` are already positioned to feed all three. The raw-`openfl`-`Sprite` trick (draw the counter outside the Flixel display list) is also worth copying — it costs nothing per frame, which matters for the low-end target.
- **[P3] `ALSoftConfig` / ALSA config passthrough.** Cheese ships an `alsoft.txt` that Lime copies to `plugins/alsoft.ini` (Windows) or `plugins/alsoft.conf` (Linux) at build time, to configure the OpenAL audio backend. A small but real low-end-Android/Linux audio-compat lever that costs one file in the build config.
- **[P3] Audio-device-switch fix.** Cheese has `backend/AudioSwitchFix.hx` (56 lines) — a focused fix for audio output device switching, credited upstream to `yoshicrafter29`. Phoenix §12.4 already lists "Desktop audio-device hotplug" as a **[P3]** open item, so this is a concrete, small, upstream-attributed implementation to draw from rather than a from-scratch design. The `AudioSwitchFix` name and the actual fix need to be read before porting; do not assume the Phoenix hotplug item is already satisfied.
- **[P3] Multithreaded asset cache.** Cheese gates a **multithreaded** asset cache behind the `MULTITHREADED_LOADING` define (`backend/ThreadedCache.hx`, 83 lines). Phoenix has **no** `ThreadedCache` and no `MULTITHREADED` define anywhere in the tree — its loading is `backend/PhoenixPreloader.hx` plus `backend/Paths.clearUnusedMemory` / `backend/LowMemory`. Threading the cache is a real low-end-device win (§18), but it must be done carefully: Cheese's `ThreadedCache` was written against Psych's loader, and Phoenix's low-memory path (`docs/LOW_MEMORY_MODE.md`) is stricter than Psych's, so a naive port can regress the `NO_PRELOAD_ALL` / memory-profile behaviour.
- **[P3] Cursor loader.** `backend/CursorLoader.hx` (59 lines) — resolves and loads the software/system cursor, and Cheese exposes it in Options. Phoenix has **no** cursor-loader module at all (its cursor handling is scattered across `backend/MusicBeatState.hx`, `backend/MetaHorror.hx` and `backend/AndroidTVPolicy.hx`), so this is a genuine gap rather than a duplicate — but it is a small, low-impact one.

### 34.7 EXPLICITLY NOT PORTED (week / mod / song content)

Per the request, the following Cheese features are **content**, not engine features, and are deliberately **out of scope**:

- **All the per-week changes** in the Cheese README ("at least one change to every week"): the Dad Battle spotlight, the "Hey!" animations for BF/GF/Skid & Pump, Thunder's light flash and camera zoom, the Monster transition, Philly Nice's BF "Hey!", Blammed's colour flash, Week 4's hair physics, the Henchmen dying, the Christmas-tree/BF/GF bopping, GF's slower head bob on Winter Horrorland, the Thorns HUD hiding, and the spooky background girls on Thorns. These are **week/song-specific content**, hard-coded in Cheese's stage files, and Phoenix's §25.1-style approach (data-driven) is the right way to get equivalent behaviour if it is ever wanted — as **generic chart events** (§34.3), not as engine features.
- **The specific `defaultEvents` entries that only exist to serve that content**: `Dadbattle Spotlight`, `Philly Glow` ("No, i won't add it to other weeks."), `Kill Henchmen` ("For Mom's songs, don't use this please, i love them :("), `BG Freaks Expression`, `Trigger BG Ghouls`, `Set GF Speed` (a Fresh-specific hack), `Alt Idle Animation` in its Week-4 form, and `Hey!`. The **generic verbs** these ride on (`Play Animation`, `Screen Shake`, `Flash Camera`, `Set Property`, `Target Camera`) *are* in scope in §34.3.
- **Cheese's art and audio**: the `CheeseEngineLogo`, the `MainMenu/ModSelector/*` background/box/rayo atlases, the sticker PNG/OGG packs, the custom sound-tray bars, the note-splash/note-skin/hold-cover additions, the V Slice FPS counter skin, the editor-picker icons, and the `docs/img/*.png` screenshots. Phoenix must ship its own art for any of these it adopts.
- **Cheese's branding**: the `CHEESE_ENGINE` define, the `cheeseEngineVersion` string, the `versionShit` watermark, and the `TitleState`/`MainMenuState` splash art. Phoenix keeps its own identity.
- **Cheese's engine fork as a base.** Cheese is itself a fork of Psych Engine with 89 commits; porting *ideas* is fine, but merging its `source/` wholesale would import Psych's older architecture over Phoenix's refactored `play/`, `editors/charting/`, `psychlua/callbacks/` and `backend/` layout. Every item above is scoped as a **targeted port into Phoenix's structure**.

### 34.8 SUGGESTED ORDER

1. **§34.1 script API gaps** — highest payoff per unit of work, ~70 callbacks (the raw 77-entry delta minus the handful Phoenix already has under another name), mechanical, and immediately makes existing mods portable. Do it in one pass across all three languages, with the `ScriptSecurityScanner` policy applied to the reflection-flavoured ones (`callMethod`, `createInstance`, `getPropertyFromClass`) and to the state-navigation ones (`switchState`, `openSubState`).
2. **§34.2 Lua/Python scripted states** — closes the "modders want their own screen" gap and is the prerequisite for §0's "expose all screens to the modding API".
3. **§34.4 note-type discovery in the editor** — the cheapest item in the whole section and it unlocks a half-built feature: Phoenix already ships 11 custom note types and a runtime that can apply them, but the editor's dropdown only knows 8 hardcoded names, so **none of the 11 can be assigned from the editor**. One folder scan + one dropdown fix.
4. **§34.3 the missing chart-event verbs** — `Target Camera` / `Target Follow Pos` (camera locking), `Flash Camera`, `Play Sound`, `Video Player` and the four skin-swap events. The event *system* and its *editor UI* already exist in Phoenix, so these are handler-level additions. Unblocks §12.2's chart-format work and the CNE event importer.
5. **§34.5 TAB mod-mode switcher** — the first slice of §12.6's addon-priority work. (The mod-settings half of §34.5 is **§27.1.1's** item and is listed in §27.9's order, not here.)
6. **§34.6 stickers + mod-overridable sound tray** — high-impact, self-contained, data-driven.
7. **§34.4 editor toys / poser** — the one authoring tool here that is a genuine green-field build; do it after the items above, since it is the largest and the least blocked.
8. **§34.7** — nothing to do; this section exists so the exclusions are on record and are not re-litigated.

---

*Section 34 added by an audit of `whenkenas/Cheese-Engine` @ `368242a` (v1.61) against this tree. The API sets of both engines were extracted mechanically (300 Cheese vs 380 Phoenix callbacks) so the 77-entry delta is measured rather than guessed; every callback listed as "missing" was then re-verified absent here **by name**, and the handful that turned out to exist under a different name are called out inline rather than silently counted as gaps. Several candidate items were **dropped during verification** because Phoenix already has them — the note-colour screen (§34.5), decimal BPM and millisecond strum-time editing (§34.4), the chart-event editor UI and the `custom_events/` discovery mechanism (§34.3), and the custom sound tray (§34.6, narrowed to the mod-override gap) — and those are recorded inline as "already done" so the audit trail shows they were checked, not overlooked. Nothing in this section was built or run.*

## 35. JOALOR64 ENGINE (REWRITE) GAP REVIEW — 2026-11-21

Source: [Joalor64/Joalor64-Engine-Rewrite](https://github.com/Joalor64/Joalor64-Engine-Rewrite) at `d4ec110` (2025-09-04, *"I guess we're done."* — the project is archived, **143 `.hx` files / 1.8 MB of Haxe / 1,115 asset files**). Read in full against this tree at `443ae01`; nothing was built or run here. Sibling audit to §23 (Mario's Madness), §24/§26 (JS Engine forks) and §25 (Psych EK+), and the same rules apply: **every entry was checked against §0–26, and anything already listed is cross-referenced, not repeated.** The `_i will port WITH THE APPROPIATED CREDITS_` / default-on-with-a-toggle / Apache-2.0 rules at the top of this file apply to everything in this section.

**License:** `LICENSE` is **Apache 2.0**, same as ours — compatible, so everything below is portable. Credit `Joalor64GH` for the engine, plus the per-feature author named in each entry from its README credits (steve-studios, TheZoroForce240, MAJigsaw77, NexIsDumb, Endergreen12, Verwex, ActualMandM, TahirKarabekiroglu, crowplexus, Keoiki, skedgyedgy, bbpanzu, DiogoTVV, Leather128, God's Drunkest Driver).

**Method, and what "complete" means here.** The tree was inventoried file-by-file (143 files → 12 top-level packages), every option row in `meta/data/options/OptionsSubState.hx` was read, `meta/data/ClientPrefs.hx` was diffed key-by-key against `backend/ClientPrefs.hx` (**55 vs 216 statics**), all **245** Lua names registered in J64's `meta/data/scripts/FunkinLua.hx` were extracted and diffed against the **412** names Phoenix registers across `source/psychlua/**` (**45 J64-only names**), and every `.hx` under `modcharting/`, `meta/video/`, `meta/state/editors/` and `minigames/` was read. Of those 45: **24 are Artemis entry points** (§35.2.6, one system), **4 are false positives** (`runHaxeCode` / `addHaxeLibrary` / `openCustomSubstate` / `closeCustomSubstate` — Phoenix has all four), **1 is commented out** (`makeJsonI8LuaSprite`), **1 is dead** (`startMovie`, §35.11) and **15 are real**. That leaves the gap list below.

_Numbering note: this audit was written as §27 and renumbered as later engine reviews landed on `main` first — §28, then §32 (Mixtape Engine §27, FunkinAvi v2 §28, Funkin' Yarn §29, FNF: Doors §30, SeiunEngine §31) §34 (Super Plus Engine §32, candidate Haxelibs §33) and finally §35 (Cheese Engine §34). Cross-references inside this section (§35.1–§35.12) were updated with it._

**What this section is not.** Phoenix is ahead of J64 on most surfaces — ~70 shaders vs 8, crash handler + bundle, recovery boot, mod security/sandboxing, deep links, an Android/iOS layer, `AccuracyPresets`, FlxAnimate, Python + `hxluajit`, Modchart Lab, the freeplay filter, adaptive performance and an actual `docs/`. §35.10 lists what J64 has that we already ship in a newer form so nobody re-ports it; §35.11 lists what should stay out.

### 35.1. Verdict in one paragraph

J64's interesting material is in three places: **the per-mod options system** (`mods/<mod>/options/<Group>/*.json` plus `loadJsonOptions`/`getOptionSave`/`setOptionSave`), **the modchart file format** (`modchart.json` + `customMods/*.hx` custom modifiers with sub-values), and **a cluster of small content/screen features** (minigame hub, credits editor, save-data reset screen, toasts, background video). Everything else is either a Lua binding we can add in an afternoon, an option row we are missing, or something we already do better. Nothing here is a rewrite.

### 35.2. Lua / Python / HScript API gaps

All of these are J64-only names (verified absent from the 412 names Phoenix registers under `source/psychlua/`), and each should land in **all three** script languages — Phoenix registers Lua through `FunkinLua.registerFunction`, Python through `python.set` and HScript through `seedHScript()`, and `docs/` says to keep the three in sync (see the header comment in `psychlua/callbacks/WindowCallbacks.hx:10-20`).

- **[P1] `getGameplayChangerValue(tag)` — read a gameplay modifier from a script.** J64 `meta/data/scripts/FunkinLua.hx:2963` is a one-liner over `ClientPrefs.getGameplaySetting(tag, false)`. Phoenix already has both halves — the map (`backend/ClientPrefs.hx:407` `gameplaySettings`) and the getter (`:808` `getGameplaySetting`) — but nothing in `source/psychlua/` exposes either, so a script cannot read `scrollspeed`, `botplay`, `flip`, `onekey` or the 23 rows `states/helpers/GameplayChangersHelpers.hx` builds. Add `getGameplayChangerValue(tag)` plus `getGameplayChanger(tag)` (typed, non-bool default) and a `gameplaySettings` read-only table; a modchart that wants to react to Flip Mode currently has no way to know it is on. Also the missing half of §35.3.4: J64 scans a `gamechangers/` mod folder for extra changers (`GameplayChangersSubstate`), Phoenix's 23 rows are code-only. Effort: **S**.

- **[P1] `saveSettings()` — flush preferences from a script.** J64 `:2718` wraps `ClientPrefs.saveSettings()`. Phoenix has the method (`backend/ClientPrefs.hx:508`) but no binding, and it is the last step of every achievement/settings write in J64 (`giveAchievement` calls it, `:255`). Without it a script that writes prefs through `setProperty(ClientPrefs, …)` loses the change on quit. Pair it with the existing `SaveDataCallbacks` family (`psychlua/callbacks/SaveDataCallbacks.hx:12-40`) rather than a new file. Effort: **S**.

- **[P1] `giveAchievement(name)` + the per-achievement script scope.** J64 registers it at `:241` and **refuses to fire** unless the calling `FunkinLua` instance is in `PlayState.instance.achievementsArray` (`meta/state/PlayState.hx:300,1160,1177`), which is filled from `Achievements.getModAchievements()` and each achievement's JSON — so an achievement's own script is the only place that can unlock it, and the failure paths are specific ("This lua file is not a custom achievement lua."). Phoenix has the *API* already — `unlockAchievement`, `getAchievement`, `setAchievement`, `addAchievementScore`, `isAchievementUnlocked`, `achievementExists` at `backend/Achievements.hx:279-330` — but any song script can unlock anything, and there is no `achievementsArray` and no `mods/achievements/` folder convention. Port the scoping, not the functions: load `mods/<mod>/achievements/*.json` + its `.lua`, keep the `song` gate, and let `giveAchievement` work only from that script (with the three J64 error strings kept for compatibility). See §35.3.2 for the JSON fields this needs. Effort: **M**.

- **[P1] `getSoundPitch(tag)` / `setSoundPitch(tag, value, doPause)`.** J64 `:2330` / `:2336`. Phoenix `psychlua/callbacks/SoundCallbacks.hx` has **12** functions (play/stop/pause/resume/fades/volume/time) and no pitch — but the engine half is already patched in: `source/source-haxelib-patches/flixel/sound/FlxSound.hx:226-230` adds `#if FLX_PITCH public var pitch`, and J64 relies on the same patch. So this is two bindings and a `FLX_PITCH`-on check. Effort: **S**.

- **[P2] `backgroundVideo(video)` / `endBGVideo()` — see §35.7.** The bindings are meaningless without the feature; they are listed there.

- **[P2] `loadJsonOptions` / `getOptionSave` / `setOptionSave` — see §35.3.1.** Same: bindings on top of the per-mod options store, listed with it.

- **[P2] `GetFromTypedGroup(group, obj)` → `group.getFirstAvailable(obj)`.** J64 `:2945`. Phoenix has the rest of the group API (`getPropertyFromGroup:59`, `setPropertyFromGroup:84`, `removeFromGroup:110`, `getObjectOrder:145`, `setObjectOrder:159` in `psychlua/callbacks/PropertyCallbacks.hx`) but no pool getter, so a script that recycles its own sprites cannot reuse Flixel's recycling path. One line per language. Effort: **S**.

- **[P2] Parallax sprites (`makeParallaxSprite` / `fixateParallaxSprite` / `addParallaxSprite`).** J64 `:1697`, `:1704`, `:1838`, storing into `PlayState.instance.modchartParallax` and backed by **`flixel_5_3_1.ParallaxSprite`** from haxelib flixel. That type is the catch: Phoenix pins a *git fork* of flixel (`hmm.json` → `JS-Engine-things/flixel-JS-Engine` @ `baa4799`), so the class cannot simply be imported. Write a Phoenix `objects/ParallaxSprite.hx` with its own scroll math (`fixate(anchorX, anchorY, scroll1X/Y, scroll2X/Y, 'horizontal'|'vertical')`, two-layer depth) and expose the three calls; `objects/BGSprite.hx:42` already handles the plain `scrollFactor` case, so keep the API additive. Credit J64 + the flixel 5.3.1 author, and **do not** present it as modchart parity — it is a stage-decor API. Effort: **M**.

- **[P3] `borderlessWindow(which)`.** J64 `:2789` writes `lime.app.Application.current.window.borderless` directly. Phoenix deliberately models this as a real three-way option instead — `backend/WindowMode.hx` + `options/GraphicsSettingsSubState.hx:100-112` (`Windowed` / `Borderless` / `Fullscreen`, desktop-only, applies immediately) — plus `backend/Window.hx:31-58` `redrawFrame()` which toggles `borderless` and restores it so DWM repaints. Porting J64's raw bool would give scripts a second, contradictory source of truth. If a binding is wanted, expose `setWindowMode(mode)` / `getWindowMode()` **over `WindowMode`** and never the raw property. Effort: **S** (and lower value than the rest).

- **[P2] `onFocus` / `onFocusLost` script callbacks.** J64 fires both as Lua callbacks. Phoenix overrides them (`play/PlayState.hx:2258`, `:2266`) and does real work in them — adaptive performance focus, RPC, haptic cancel — but **never** calls `callOnScripts`, so no script can see a tab-out. Fire `onFocus`/`onFocusLost` from those two methods, after the existing bookkeeping, and mirror into Python/HScript. This is the last piece of the §21.2 "pause on controller disconnect" family and is needed by any mod that wants to pause on alt-tab. Effort: **S**.

- **[P3] `Artemis` integration — 22 entry points, one system.** §35.2.6.

- **[P3] Text-input prompt for mods.** J64 `meta/substate/PasswordPrompt.hx:11` (58 lines, `bbpanzu`) is a modal `FlxUIInputText` with a callback — useful for "enter a code" mods and for the §14 replay importer. Phoenix's `objects/Prompt.hx` is confirm-only (`requireHold`, two buttons) and the Lua API has **no** `getUserInput`. The patched `FlxInputText` already supports `passwordMode` (`source/source-haxelib-patches/flixel/addons/ui/FlxInputText.hx:100-104`), so this is a small substate plus one binding. Effort: **S**.

#### 35.2.6. Artemis (rhythm-game overlay) integration — [P3], but all-or-nothing

`meta/ArtemisIntegration.hx` (430 lines) + 24 Lua names. Artemis is a desktop overlay app; J64 writes to `http://localhost:9696/plugins/<guid>/…` after reading the URL from `%ProgramData%/Artemis/webserver.txt`, and pushes health, accent colours, stage/mod name, beat, combo, flashes, fades and custom flags/strings/numbers (API v1.2.1, credit **skedgyedgy**; `assets/artemis/fnf-vanilla.json` is the profile). Phoenix has **zero** hits for `Artemis`.

Take it or leave it whole — half an integration renders a permanently empty overlay. If taken: gate on `ClientPrefs.enableArtemis` (J64's `enableArtemis:Bool = true`, `ClientPrefs.hx:24`), desktop-only, every call a no-op when the server is absent, and **never** on the critical path of `update()` (J64 fires `sendBoyfriendHealth` per frame and blocks on a HTTP client — do it on a timer and drop failures). Cross-ref §11 (Android native) — this is the opposite corner of the engine and does not belong in the same milestone.

### 35.3. Options and settings gaps

J64's `OptionsSubState.hx` is one 1,500-line file holding five `BaseOptionsMenu` subclasses; Phoenix has ten separate sub-states plus `Accessibility` §16, so rows land in different places. Everything below was confirmed missing from `backend/ClientPrefs.hx` **and** from `source/options/*.hx`.

#### 35.3.1. Per-mod options (`mods/<mod>/options/<Group>/*.json`) — [P1], the biggest single gap

This is the feature `docs/Custom-Options.md` documents (credit **NexIsDumb** + upstream Psych PR #12307) and the one most likely to be *used* by other mods:

- The store is `ClientPrefs.modsOptsSaves:Map<String, Map<String, Dynamic>>` (J64 `meta/data/ClientPrefs.hx:370`), i.e. `mods/<mod>/options/<Group>/<OptionName>.json` → parsed, keyed by the JSON's own `variable`, defaults filled from `defaultValue` or `CoolUtil.getOptionDefVal(type, options)` (J64 `FunkinLua.hx:2653-2685`).
- Any `BaseOptionsMenu` subclass auto-appends the active mods' JSONs for **its own class name** as rows (J64 `meta/data/options/BaseOptionsMenu.hx:87-105`): the folder name must match `FlxStringUtil.getClassName(this, true)`, so `.../options/VisualsSubState/` adds rows to the visuals page. Each row is created as a plain `Option` and then every JSON field is `Reflect.setField`'d onto it — that is why `Option` needs no schema change.
- `Option.getValue`/`setValue` branch on a new `fromJson:Array<String>` field (J64 `meta/data/options/Option.hx:11,79-99`) to read/write the mod map instead of `Reflect.getProperty(ClientPrefs, variable)`.
- Scripts read and write the same store via `loadJsonOptions`, `getOptionSave(variable, isJson, modName)`, `setOptionSave(…)` (J64 `:2653/2686/2701`).
- The player-facing reset is `DeleteSavesSubState` (J64 `OptionsSubState.hx:330-480`, bound to the RESET key in the Options menu, `OptionsState.hx:184-186`) which lists every mod's saved options and can delete one or all.

Phoenix has **none** of it: `options/Option.hx` has no `fromJson`, `options/BaseOptionsMenu.hx` only reads its hardcoded `optionsArray`, and `states/ModsMenuState.hx:307-320` still has the Psych `ModSettingsSubState` button commented out with a `// not yet` (its `settings.json` loader is commented out too, `:934-953`). Do **not** revive that variant.

What a Phoenix port needs, in order: (1) `ClientPrefs.modOptions` next to the existing prefs, saved in its own `FlxSave` slot rather than inside `funkin` so a truncated prefs flush cannot eat mod data (same argument as `backend/FreeplayFilterStore`); (2) `Option.fromJson` + the two-branch `getValue`/`setValue`; (3) a loader in `options/BaseOptionsMenu.hx` that scans `mods/<mod>/options/<ClassName>/` for **enabled, platform-supported** mods only (`backend/Mods.getModDirectories` + `Mods.isSupportedOnThisPlatform`) — J64 does not check either, which is why a disabled mod's options can still show up there; (4) validation: reject a JSON with no `variable`, clamp against `minValue`/`maxValue`, and refuse `type` values Phoenix does not implement instead of silently defaulting to bool (J64 defaults everything unknown to `'bool'`); (5) the three script calls in all three languages; (6) a `Mod Options` page + reset page; (7) `docs/` page and a `tests/haxe/run-mod-options.sh` spec, per house rule. Effort: **L**. Cross-refs: §35.3.2 (the same JSON machinery serves achievements), §22.5 "Per-mod settings profiles" (player's *engine* prefs per mod — a different feature, do not merge), §13 (save split), §12.1 "custom options/controls scripts" (`:184`), §17 (mod tooling).

#### 35.3.2. Achievement JSON parity — [P2]

Phoenix's `Achievement` typedef (`backend/Achievements.hx:15-26`) is `name`/`description`/`hidden`/`maxScore`/`maxDecimals`/`mod`/`ID`, loaded from `mods/data/achievements.json` only (`:211-220`), with `"save"` required (`:249`) and a per-mod invalid-mod mark (`:265`). J64's schema (`docs/Custom-Achievements.md`, credit **TahirKarabekiroglu**) adds the things that make achievements *data* rather than code:

- `song` — the achievement only unlocks in that song (J64 `PlayState.hx:1155-1156` compares against `SONG.song`, skipping others; the same gate runs for inline code).
- `index` — explicit sort position (`-1`/null = append), which is what keeps a mod's achievements in a chosen order in the menu; Phoenix appends by load order.
- `week_nomiss` — declares "this week, no misses" as data (`PlayState.hx:1181` pushes `'<week>_nomiss'` into `achievementWeeks`) instead of the nine hardcoded `createAchievement('weekN_nomiss', …)` calls at `backend/Achievements.hx:32-39`.
- `lua_code` — inline script in the JSON, no `.lua` file (`PlayState.hx:1172-1178`).
- `global` — a single JSON that replaces *every* achievement (documented as "dangerous", and correctly so: it ignores all other fields).
- `clearAchievements` — wipe-then-load, for a total-conversion mod.

Take `song`, `index`, `lua_code` and `week_nomiss`; **refuse** `global` and `clearAchievements` from mod JSON (they let a mod delete the player's progress, which is exactly what §6 and the mod-invalid path exist to prevent) and offer "replace all" as an explicit, confirmed editor action instead. Effort: **M**.

#### 35.3.3. HUD / gameplay option rows — [P2–P3]

Each is a real missing row; each needs the default-on + toggle + colour-preset rule from the top of this file.

- **[P2] New score system (`weekendScore`).** J64 `OptionsSubState.hx:747` + `ClientPrefs.hx:41`, switching the HUD readout to `truncateFloat(smoothScore, 0)` (`PlayState.hx:2794`). Phoenix's score is Psych-style and multiplicative by polyphony (`play/helpers/PlayStateRating.hx:150` `daRating.score * polyphony`), and there is no smooth/settle animation to read, so this is **not** a cosmetic toggle: it needs a real v0.3-style scorer (per-note accuracy-weighted score with a settling counter) behind a `scoreSystem` option, default **Psych (current)**, with the results screen and `Highscore` recording which system produced a score (`§13` says records carry no engine version; they would need to). Do not add the row without the scorer. Effort: **M**. Cross-ref §14 (score trust) and §21.1 (results).

- **[P2] UI Skin (judgement + receptor skin set).** J64 `uiSkin` (`ClientPrefs.hx:33`, row `:636`) with `Default` / `Vanilla` / `Forever` / `Kade` / `Simplylove`, applied through a `switch` in `PlayState` (`:4804,4888`) and a pixel-stage special case (`:4829`). Phoenix split this into two stronger rows — `ratingType` (`ClientPrefs.hx:122`, `options/VisualsUISubState.hx:388`, eight sets in `assets/shared/images/ratings/`: default, Doki Doki+, FIRE IN THE HOLE, Kade, NMCW, TGT V4, VS Impostor, Yeahs) and `noteSkin` (`:66`) — so **do not port the combo**. What is missing is only the *receptor* half of a "skin set" (J64's UI skin swaps strum art together with judgements); if a curated "skin set" is ever wanted, it should be a preset over `ratingType` + `noteSkin` + `healthBarStyle` + `timeBarStyle`, not a fourth independent field.

- **[P2] Long health bar (`longBar`).** J64 `ClientPrefs.hx:37`, row `OptionsSubState.hx:652`, `PlayState.hx:1414-1415` picks `healthBarLong` when on. Phoenix has `healthBarStyle` (`VisualsUISubState.hx:320`) over `Vanilla` / `Dave Engine` / `Doki Doki+` (`play/PlayState.hx:1489-1505`) but **no art**: `assets/shared/images/` ships `healthBar.png` plus `DnBHealthBar.png` / `dokiHealthBar.png` / `editorHealthBar.png` and nothing long. Either draw a Phoenix `healthBarLong.png` (a 1:1 port of the PNG is art, not code — needs the artist credited) or make `healthBarStyle` a width-aware style table so a long variant is one entry. Effort: **S** + art.

- **[P3] Camera pan on sing + intensity (`cameraPanning`, `panIntensity`).** J64 `ClientPrefs.hx:45-46`, rows `OptionsSubState.hx:761-779`, and the effect is a per-`sing` offset in `PlayState.hx:2489-2492` scaled by `panIntensity` (0.01–10, `%vX`). Phoenix's camera only follows characters (`play/helpers/PlayStateCamera.hx:37-73` `moveCamera()` writes `camFollow` from the singer's midpoint) with `camZooms` (`ClientPrefs.hx:140`) and `cameraFxIntensity` (`:142`) as the only dials — a hit-direction kick does not exist. **This is the amendment to §25.2, not a duplicate of it:** §25.2 adds the EK+ `camMovement` switch that turns the *singer* pan off; this adds an *impact* pan that must be scaled by `reducedMotion` (§16), clamped by `Camera Effects Intensity`, and made to lose to a forced camera event (`play/helpers/PlayStateEvents.hx:344-347` writes `camFollow` + `isCameraOnForcedPos`). Per the porting rule at the top of this file it ships **on** at 1.0X (J64's own default) with both the toggle and the intensity dial, so a player who dislikes it turns it off rather than never seeing it; if it lands after a release and changes feel too much, the fallback is default-on at 0.5X rather than default-off. Effort: **S**.

- **[P3] Results screen toggle (`resultsScreen`).** J64 `ClientPrefs.hx:42`, row `:754`, checked at `PlayState.hx:4682,4748`. Phoenix always opens it (`play/helpers/PlayStatePlayback.hx:530` week, `:570` song). One bool, default on, plus a `Results` row in Gameplay. Cross-ref §21.1 (which owns the screen's *content*); this entry is only the skip. Effort: **S**.

- **[P2] Global colourblind filter row.** J64 `meta/Colorblind.hx` builds three `ColorMatrixFilter`s (`Deuteranopia`/`Protanopia`/`Tritanopia`) and applies them to `FlxG.game` from `ClientPrefs.colorBlindFilter` (`ClientPrefs.hx:35`, row `OptionsSubState.hx:1554-1560`). Phoenix has the *filter* (the same three matrices are presets in `backend/ScreenFilters.hx:53-54`) and a *note palette* (`options/AccessibilitySubState.hx:84-88` `noteColorblindPreset`), but the only way to apply the full-screen filter today is a script calling `addColorFilter('global', 'deuteranopia')` — there is no Options row. Add `screenColorFilter` (`None` + the named presets) to `AccessibilitySubState`, default `None`, and route it through `ScreenFilters` so the existing remove/clear registry stays the single owner. Cross-ref §16 (the accessibility category and its DONE note) and §0's filter API entry. Effort: **S**.

- **[P3] Freeplay song display style (`songDisplay`).** J64 `ClientPrefs.hx:39`, row `:1482`, values `Classic` / `Vertical` / `C-Shape` / `D-Shape` (`FreeplayState.hx:112-146`, `:336`). Phoenix's list items only lerp X/Y (`objects/Alphabet.hx:207-224` — `distancePerItem`, `changeX/changeY`), with no shape modes. A `freeplayLayout` option (Classic / Vertical / Curve) is a genuine QoL gap for large song lists, and it composes with the freeplay filter (§21.2 DONE) which already owns ordering. Effort: **M** (it moves the icons and the preview, not just the text).

- **[P3] Floating letters (`floatyLetters`).** J64 `ClientPrefs.hx:38`, row `:1474`, `onChange` sets `Alphabet.alphabet.shouldDisplace`. Phoenix `objects/Alphabet.hx` has no displacement pass. Cosmetic, one boolean + one per-char sine in `Alphabet.update`, must respect `reducedMotion`. Effort: **S**.

- **[P3] Simple main menu (`simpleMain`).** J64 `ClientPrefs.hx:36`, row `OptionsSubState.hx:1564`, + `meta/state/SimpleMainMenuState.hx` (217 lines): the same destinations as text-only `Alphabet` rows, no sprite atlas, no `menuBG`. Used from `MinigamesState:71` and `PauseSubState` to get back. This is a real low-end win (one atlas + one background fewer on the heaviest menu) and fits §18. Fold it into the main menu as a `mainMenuStyle` option (`Full` / `Simple`) rather than a second state, so the deep links, RPC, `PlatformIcon` and `MenuMusic` wiring (`states/MainMenuState.hx`) are not duplicated. Effort: **M**.

- **[P3] Donate screen.** J64 `meta/state/DonateScreenState.hx` (83 lines, from Chocolate Engine) — a blurb page with the support link, instead of the current behaviour where the `donate` row launches a browser directly (`states/MainMenuState.hx:411-415` → `CoolUtil.browserLoad(...repo…)`). A screen can hold several links, a QR and the credit be read *in game*, and it is mod-overridable. Low value; take it only if the donate row is being touched anyway.

#### 35.3.4. Gameplay changers from mods — [P2]

J64 scans a `gamechangers/` mod folder for extra `GameplayChangersSubstate` entries — `meta/substate/GameplayChangersSubstate.hx`, 560 lines, credit **Endergreen12**. Phoenix's 23 rows are code (`states/helpers/GameplayChangersHelpers.hx:15-165` `GameplayOption('Scroll Type', 'scrolltype')` … `GameplayOption('Troll Mode', 'thetrollingever')`) and there is no folder scan, so a mod cannot add a changer — it can only reach `gameplaySettings` from a script, which the player cannot see in the pause menu. Add a `mods/<mod>/gamechangers/*.json` loader (`name`, `tag`, `type`, `defaultValue`, `min/max`, `step`, optional `onChange` script call) that appends to the same `getOptions()` list, plus validation against the same clamps. Pairs with §35.3.1 (same `Option` machinery) and §35.2 (the read side, `getGameplayChangerValue`). Effort: **M**.

### 35.4. Game content

J64 ships no extra *songs* that Phoenix lacks: every chart folder in `assets/data/` except `amogus` and `compression` exists here (`dad-battle`, `test`, `blammed`, `2hot`, `darnell`, `lit-up`, `blazin` + weekend 1 via `assets/preload/weeks/weekend1.json`), and its "Week 8" (`assets/weeks/week8.json`) is a hidden pointer to the `Test` chart we already ship — no action (see §35.10). The content gaps are the **hub screens** around content.

- **[P3] Minigame hub (`states/MinigamesState`).** J64 `meta/state/MinigamesState.hx` (120 lines) is a standard `Alphabet` list over three entries (`:10-14`): two chart-based minigames that reuse the real `PlayState` — `amogus` and `compression`, plus `PlayState.inMini` (`:78-88`) so the pause/results/title paths know — and one standalone point-and-click (`minigames/KillBF.hx`, 77 lines + `minigames/FallingIcon.hx`, 24 lines, mouse-driven, `assets/music/minigame.ogg`). Phoenix has no minigame screen and no `inMini`; `states/ModState.hx` can be switched to by a script but there is no shared hub (§21.1 "Extras hub" wants one for mod screens). The right shape for us is the one §21.1 already describes — a hub that lists softcoded screens — with J64's three as the *example content*: `inMini` becomes a song-result routing flag ("don't write a highscore / don't advance the week"), and `KillBF` is small enough to port as a sample minigame with credit. Assets are vanilla-derived (`icons/bf`, `stages/stage/stageback`, `menuBGBlue`, `mgicons/slash`), so the art cost is ~one PNG set. Effort: **M**. Cross-ref §21.1 (Extras hub), §0.

- **[P3] Date-aware title text (`fridayText`).** J64 reads `data/fridayText.txt` instead of `data/introText.txt` on Fridays after 18:00 (`meta/state/TitleState.hx:317`). Phoenix reads `introText.txt` unconditionally and uses `funnyTips.txt` elsewhere; the title layout JSON is already shared (`assets/preload/images/gfDanceTitle.json` holds the same `titlex/titley/startx/starty/gfx/gfy/backgroundSprite/bpm` keys J64's `assets/data/titleStuff.json` does — **no port needed there**, see §35.10). One text file + one date check + a `holidayText` generalisation (Halloween/Christmas/Friday) so it is not a single hardcoded special case. Effort: **S**.

- **[P3] Two-line game name.** J64's `assets/data/gameName.txt` feeds a two-line animated title (`TitleState.hx:337,584-586`). Phoenix hardcodes one string. Fold into the same `holidayText`/title work rather than a separate entry.

### 35.5. Editors and modder tooling

- **[P2] Credits editor.** J64 `meta/state/editors/CreditsEditorState.hx` (**786 lines**) is a full editor: add credit / add title, per-entry name + icon + description + link + background colour, drag-to-reorder (`pushAtPos`, `moveSelection`), delete, and JSON/`credits.txt` save-load. It is reachable from `MasterEditorMenu` (case `'Credits Editor'`). Phoenix hardcodes `creditsEntries` inside `states/CreditsState.hx:72` and only *reads* an override file (`:309-310` `mods/data/credits.txt`), with icons loaded from `credits/<icon>` (`:139`) — so credits are the one content type with no editor, in an engine that ships week/character/menu-character/dialogue editors plus a modchart lab and note-splash debug screen (`editors/MasterEditorMenu.hx:114-130`). Port the editor on top of the existing `credits.txt` format (which becomes the save target), and add it to the master menu. Cross-ref §12.3 (editor parity list) and §12.4's "Lag credits" idea. Effort: **M**.

- **[P2] In-engine mod folder setup.** J64 `meta/state/editors/ModsSetupState.hx` (298 lines) is a form — folder, id, name, description, three RGB fields — that validates the folder name against `invalidFolderCharacters` and then creates the whole standard tree, writing a `-go-here.txt` placeholder in every folder and a filled `pack.json` (`:242-271`, `setupModFolder`). Reachable from `MasterEditorMenu` (case `'Mod Folder Setup'`). Phoenix ships `example_mods/modTemplate.zip` and §17 (`:339`) plans `tools/new-mod.py`, but **on a phone there is no shell to run it** and the mods menu can only *import* a `.zip` (`states/ModsMenuState.hx:735-770`). An in-engine version is the Android-friendly counterpart, not a duplicate of the CLI: cross-ref §17 (CLI, DONE-side) and §12.3. It must reuse `backend/ModPathPolicy.RESERVED_MOD_FOLDERS` (`ModPathPolicy.hx:41-44`) for validation instead of J64's own character blacklist, and must not create a folder whose name is reserved (`data`, `scripts`, `achievements`, …). Effort: **M**.

- **[P3] Visual modchart editor.** J64 `modcharting/ModchartEditorState.hx` (2,080 lines) edits playfields/modifiers/events on a running song and saves `modchart.json` (`:1985-1992`). Phoenix's Modchart Lab (§21.1, DONE) is the renderer-free half and explicitly **not** an in-chart editor. Do not port J64's editor: it is welded to J64's own renderer, and §21.1 already owns the Lab. What is worth taking is only the *file* half — see §35.6.

### 35.6. Modcharting gaps

Phoenix's native runtime (`source/modchart/`, 36 registered modifiers via `ModifierRegistry.register`, `:46-118`) is newer and better optimised than J64's `modcharting/` (13 files, `PlayfieldRenderer` + `ModTable` index tables + `SustainStrip`). **Do not replace it, and do not add a second modifier API** — the sections below are (a) a *file format* and (b) two argument-level compatibility gaps.

- **[P2] `modchart.json` per-song format.** J64's `ModchartJson` typedef is `{ modifiers:Array<Array<Dynamic>>, events:Array<Array<Dynamic>>, playfields:Int }` (`modcharting/ModchartFile.hx:31-35`), loaded from `Paths.json(folder + '/modchart')` (mod folder first, then base assets, `:104-118`), with the modifier tuple indexed by constants (`MOD_NAME 0`, `MOD_CLASS 1`, `MOD_TYPE 2`, `MOD_PF 3`, `MOD_LANE 4`) and events as set/ease with an optional repeat block (`EVENT_REPEAT*`, `:50-58`). Phoenix is script-only: a modchart exists only as `addModifier`/`modSet`/`modEase` calls (`psychlua/callbacks/ModchartCallbacks.hx:25-106`), and the Lab export is code (`modchart/lab/ModchartLabExport.hx:79`, Lua/Python only). Add a **loader** in the existing manager — `ModchartManager` already has `addPlayfield:18`, `addModifier:45`, `set:66`, `ease:71`, `add:76`, `setAdd:81`, `callback:86`, `repeater:91`, so the whole format maps onto calls that exist — plus a **writer** from the Lab (`ModchartLabExport` gains a third language, `JSON`). Reading a foreign `modchart.json` must be tolerant: unknown modifier names are logged and skipped, never fatal, and `MOD_TYPE` values map onto our per-player/per-lane percents rather than new fields. Credit **TheZoroForce240** (fnf-modcharting-tools) and J64. Effort: **M**.

- **[P2] Script-registered custom modifiers (`customMods/*.hx`).** J64 parses every `.hx` in `<song>/customMods/` into an hscript `Interp` (`CustomModifierScript`, `ModchartFile.hx:211-231`) and registers it under the file's base name (`:118-127`) so `modchart.json` can reference a modifier that does not exist in the engine. Phoenix's `ModifierRegistry` is `static` with no runtime registration path (`ModifierRegistry.hx:93-118`: `register` is `static`, `create`/`exists`/`names` are the only public API), so a mod can only use the 36 built-ins. Add `ModifierRegistry.registerRuntime(name, instance)` returning an unregister handle, fed by a Phoenix `modifiers/` script folder (Lua/Python/HScript — hscript is *not* the only option here, unlike J64) with a documented surface: `render(pos, params)`, `visuals(visuals, params)`, `shouldRun(params)`, `setPercent/getPercent`, matching `modchart/Modifier.hx:14-88`. Guard it: a scripted modifier that throws must be disabled for the rest of the song, not retried per frame, and it must be excluded from `maxNotes`-style perf accounting only if it is measured first (§18). Effort: **L** (this is the real scripting-parity item).

- **[P3] Sub-values (`setSubMod`) and modifier-level targeting (`setModTargetLane` / `setModPlayfield` / `removePlayfield`).** J64's `Modifier` carries `tag`, `type` (`ALL`/`PLAYERONLY`/`OPPONENTONLY`/`LANESPECIFIC`), `playfield:Int`, `targetLane:Int`, `subValues:Map<String, ModifierSubValue>` and a static `beat` (`modcharting/Modifier.hx:29-53`), with the four Lua calls at `ModchartFuncs.hx:50-63`. Phoenix's `Modifier` (`modchart/Modifier.hx`) has `playfield` and helpers (`getPercentLane` already sums `name` + `name+lane`, `:38-41`), and `ModchartManager` has `addPlayfield:18` but **no removal** and no re-target. So: `setModTargetLane` is a **compat shim** over the lane-suffixed percents we already support (do not add a second lane mechanism); `setModPlayfield`/`removePlayfield` are genuine small additions (`ModchartManager.playfield(i)` exists at `:25`); sub-values are a genuine gap only for modifiers with more than one knob — expose them as extra named percents on the existing `PercentStore` (`modchart/PercentStore.hx`) instead of a parallel `subValues` map. Effort: **S–M**.

### 35.7. Video

Phoenix has `objects/VideoSprite.hx` (hxvlc-backed, `:13`) and `play/helpers/PlayStateCutscenes.startVideo` (`:21`), which sets `inCutscene`/`canPause` (`:24-25`) and swaps in a full-screen cutscene. J64 has three things on top:

- **[P2] Background video (`backgroundVideo` / `endBGVideo`).** J64 `:2221`/`:2226`, backed by `meta/video/BackgroundVideo.hx` + `WebmHandler.hx` + `GlobalVideo.hx` (a per-index registry of players so several can coexist). This plays a **looping webm behind the stage while the song runs** — not a cutscene, no `inCutscene`, no pause. That is a real gap: the extension point is `PlayStateCutscenes` (same file, add `startBackgroundVideo`/`stopBackgroundVideo`) with `forMidSong: true` semantics, `loop: true`, and the sprite routed to `camGame` behind `stage`'s layers so notes/HUD stay on top. Must be off-switchable per stage (`StageData.StageFile` already has Phoenix-only fields), must respect `lowQuality` (§22.6 adaptive quality), and must be skipped entirely when `VIDEOS_ALLOWED`/hxvlc is absent. Credit J64 + **KookerFoxYT** (borrowed code, per its README). Effort: **M**.

- **[P3] Video as a substate / overlay (`VideoSubState`, `VideoState`).** J64's `meta/video/VideoSubState.hx` (138 lines) and `VideoState.hx` (133) are standalone players usable outside `PlayState` — a video cutscene in a menu, a mod intro, a credits reel, with `end()` and an `onComplete`. Phoenix's only player wraps a `PlayState` cutscene, so a mod cannot show a video from a menu without switching states. Small wrapper over `objects/VideoSprite` + a `psychlua` `startVideoState(file, callback)`; it should reuse `LoadingState`'s video path if one exists rather than a fourth player. Effort: **S**. Cross-ref §4 (video/audio) and §12.8's "do not remove video support".

- **Cutscene handler, MP4/WebM decode, hxvlc:** already parity — see §35.10.

### 35.8. In-game notification (toast) API — [P3]

J64's `backend/ToastCore.hx` (211 lines, credit **MAJigsaw77**) is a stackable toast queue: `Main.toast.create(title, colour, description)` (`Main.hx:31,138-139`), enter/display/leave timings (`ENTER_TIME 0.5` / `DISPLAY_TIME 3.0` / `LEAVE_TIME 0.5`), `onFinish`, reflow on state switch and window resize, documented in `docs/ToastCore.md`. Phoenix has **no** in-game toast — only OS-level notifications on Android/iOS (`backend/StdFire.hx:111` → `AndroidStdFire.showToast`, `IOSStdFire.showToast`) — and in-game feedback is either a `Prompt` (blocking), an `objects/AchievementPopup`, or a `trace`. A non-blocking queue is the right home for "mod loaded", "screenshot saved", "settings backed up", "controller disconnected" and script-driven mod messages, and it is the missing middle between a console line and a modal. Port it as a Phoenix object (do not copy `ToastCore`'s `Sprite`/`FlxTimer` split) with an `addToast(title, description, colour, duration)` API in all three languages, a `toasts` option (Full / Compact / Off) per the default-on rule, and a hard cap on simultaneous toasts. Effort: **M**. Credit Majigsaw77 + J64.

### 35.9. Save data

- **[P2] A save-data reset screen.** J64 `meta/data/options/SaveDataState.hx` (136 lines) offers exactly four rows — `Reset High Scores`, `Reset Week Progress`, `Reset Achievement Data`, `Reset ALL Data` — each behind a `Prompt` with an irreversible warning, clearing `Highscore.songScores/songRating/weekScores` (`:110-118`), `StoryMenuState.weekCompleted` (`:120-124`) and the achievement maps (`:126-135`) then `FlxG.save.flush()`. Phoenix has **nothing**: `grep` for a reset/wipe UI across `source/options/` and `source/states/` returns no match, and the nearest code is `psychlua/callbacks/SaveDataCallbacks.hx` (script key-values, unrelated) and the per-song `ResetScoreSubState` (`states/substates/ResetScoreSubState.hx:20`, opened from freeplay/story only). This is a support tool as much as a feature: "my save is weird" currently means "delete the folder by hand". Add `states/substates/ResetSaveSubState` reachable from Misc + `phoenix://` (deep links are already a Phoenix surface) with four scoped actions, each behind `Prompt(..., requireHold: true)` (`objects/Prompt.hx:39-56`), writing through the same stores the game reads (`backend/Highscore`, `backend/WeekData`, `backend/Achievements`) and **not** through a raw `FlxG.save` wipe, so per-store extra files (`FreeplayFilterStore`, `freeplay_filter`) are handled deliberately. Cross-ref §13 — which already owns "Export/import a save bundle" (`:277`) and notes that `backend/SettingsBackup` carries **preferences only**; a reset is the complement of an export, so it belongs in §13's scope even though §13 does not list it. Effort: **S**.

- **Per-mod options saves reset:** part of §35.3.1 (J64's `DeleteSavesSubState`), not a separate entry.

### 35.10. Checked, already shipped in Phoenix — **no action**

Verified one by one; listed so nobody re-ports them. (J64's own README advertises many of these as features, which is why they need a "we have it" line.)

- **Results screen.** Phoenix `states/ResultsState.hx:16`, opened from `play/helpers/PlayStatePlayback.hx:530,570`; §21.1 owns the content work. Only the *toggle* is missing (§35.3.3).
- **Window mode / borderless.** Real three-way `backend/WindowMode.hx` + `options/GraphicsSettingsSubState.hx:100-112`, better than J64's bool; plus `backend/Window.hx` (DWM dark mode, `matchSystemTheme`, `redrawFrame`) which J64 calls from `Main.hx:75` via `meta/data/windows/WindowsAPI.hx`.
- **Latin accents.** `objects/Alphabet.hx:414-419`; J64 needed `meta/data/alphabet/AlphabetOld.hx` as a fallback because it has **two** alphabet implementations.
- **Uncapped BPM.** The song-BPM stepper is capped at `999999` (`editors/charting/ChartingUISections.hx:134`) and the section-BPM stepper likewise (`:462`), against J64's `1..9999` (`ChartingState.hx:457`); `backend/CoolUtil.hx:314-360` recomputes `stepCrochet` from whatever BPM a chart declares with no clamp.
- **Noteskins.** `ClientPrefs.noteSkin` (`backend/ClientPrefs.hx:62`), row `options/VisualsUISubState.hx:64-70`, four skins shipped in `assets/shared/images/noteskins/` + `list.txt`; J64 ships two and its own row says *"NOT ACTUALLY IMPLEMENTED FOR SOME REASON!!"* (`OptionsSubState.hx:644-650`).
- **Pitch patch.** `source/source-haxelib-patches/flixel/sound/FlxSound.hx:226-230` already adds `pitch`; only the bindings are missing (§35.2).
- **Video sprite + cutscenes.** §35.7.
- **Achievements JSON loader.** `backend/Achievements.hx:211-220` (per-mod `data/achievements.json`, `hidden`, `maxScore`/`maxDecimals`); §35.3.2 lists the fields we lack.
- **`getPropertyAdvanced` family, `luaSpriteMakeGraphic`, `setObjectCamera`, `noteTweenAlpha`.** All registered in `source/psychlua/` (`PropertyCallbacks`, `SpriteCallbacks`); a suggestion to "port them" would be a no-op.
- **Mods menu parity.** Enable/disable-all (`states/ModsMenuState.hx:71` `curSelectedButton (-1 = Enable/Disable All, -2 = Reload)`, buttons `:286-350`, `ModItem:910`, `mustRestart:998`), zip import (`:735-830` via `mobile.files.MobileFilePicker` + `ModZipInstaller`), Mod Info (`:721`). J64's only extra is the GameBanana downloader, already tracked at §0 line 60.
- **Auto Pause, Safe Frames, No Miss Judgement, No Reset, Pause Music, FPS counter, Anti-Aliasing, Shaders, Resolution, Widescreen.** All present (`options/GameplaySettingsSubState.hx:90,325,111,118`, `VisualsUISubState.hx:568,507`, `GraphicsSettingsSubState.hx:42,51,89,26`) — the earlier "J64-only option" list was wrong about these.
- **`Display Milliseconds`.** Functionally already here: `ClientPrefs.showMS` (`:313`) + `options/OptimizationSubState.hx:107` + `play/objects/MSText.hx:72-86` show the exact `±N MS` per hit. J64's separate `precision` text adds nothing.
- **Hitsound Volume / Note Offset / hit windows.** `options/GameplaySettingsSubState.hx:160`, `options/NoteOffsetState.hx`, and `AccuracyPresets` (§22.4) supersede J64's four raw window rows.
- **Screen Resolution + Scale Mode.** `resolution` (`GraphicsSettingsSubState.hx:89`, 13 presets 16x9→3840x2160) + `widescreen` (`:26`) + `windowMode`; J64's list is `1280x720`/`1280x960`/`FULLSCREEN` (`OptionsSubState.hx:1503-1532`).
- **`scoreTxtType` (Default/Psych/Kade/Simple).** Phoenix `scoreStyle` (`ClientPrefs.hx:112`) has **ten** styles (`options/VisualsUISubState.hx:304-307`).
- **Gameplay changer count.** Phoenix has 23 `GameplayOption` rows (`states/helpers/GameplayChangersHelpers.hx`) against J64's core set, plus `bothsides`, `opponentdrain`, `onlySicks`, `jacks`, `randomspeed*`.
- **`ModchartLab` vs `ModchartEditorState`.** §21.1 DONE; §35.5/§35.6 explain what is *not* covered.
