- **`gameName`/title layout JSON.** Phoenix reads the same key set from `assets/preload/images/gfDanceTitle.json` (`states/TitleState.hx:131-136`) — J64's `assets/data/titleStuff.json` is byte-for-byte the same schema.
- **Week metadata.** `backend/WeekData.hx` already carries `freeplayColor` (`:17`), `storyColor` (`:39`), `startUnlocked`, `hiddenUntilUnlocked`, `hideStoryMode`, and per-week `difficulties` (`:22`, consumed at `states/StoryMenuState.hx:485-492`) — i.e. everything `week8.json`/`weekend1.json` use, and `secret.json` covers the hidden-week pattern. J64's "Week 8" is a pointer to the `Test` chart we ship; no content to port.
- **`GameDimensions`, `PlatformUtil`, `CoolSystemStuff.getUsername`, `PlayerSettings`, `CustomFadeTransition`, `AttachedSprite`/`AttachedText`, `CheckboxThingie`, `MenuItem`, `FlxUIDropDownMenuCustom`, `BGSprite`, `BackgroundDancer`/`BackgroundGirls`/`TankmenBG`, the `Bar`/`FlxBar` HUD, `animateatlas/*`, `FlxDrawQuadsItem` (`#if FLX_DRAW_QUADS`), `PsychAnimationController` (we do the same job globally through `FlxG.animationTimeScale`, `play/PlayState.hx:641,1964,3310`), `objects/shaders/{WiggleEffect,RainShader,BuildingShader,ColorSwap,PhillyGlow}` and the weekend `ABot` props.** Every one has a Phoenix counterpart in a newer form (`ScreenLayout`, `PlatformSupport`, `backend/CoolUtil.hx:121`, `backend/PlayerSettings.hx`, `shaders/CustomFadeTransition.hx`, `objects/*`, `stages/objects/*`, `source-haxelib-patches/flixel/graphics/tile/FlxDrawQuadsItem.hx`, `backend/animation/PsychAnimationController.hx`, `stages/PhillyBlazin.hx:32,93`). Its `objects/shaders/` (8 files) is a subset of our ~70.

### 35.11. Checked and deliberately **not** taken

- **`startMovie(flashFile, sound)`.** Registered at `FunkinLua.hx:2202` but **no `startMovie` method exists in J64's `PlayState`** (`grep -rn "startMovie"` across its `source/` finds only the registration), so the call throws. Dead binding — do not list it as a feature gap, and do not implement a Flash player.
- **`makeJsonI8LuaSprite`.** Commented out in J64's source. JSONI8 *sprite* support exists (`psychlua/FunkinLua.hx:629`, `PythonScript.hx:701` `case 'aseprite' | 'jsoni8'`), the Lua maker does not.
- **`ButtplugUtils` (391 lines) + its three `PlayState` call sites.** A device-control HTTP bridge to `localhost:6969`, initialised unconditionally and unasked in `Main.hx:78-79` (i.e. before the player has opted into anything), with three call sites inside `PlayState`. Keep out: it needs explicit opt-in and a device/config UI to be defensible, has no mobile story, and is the first thing a platform reviewer would see. If ever wanted it is a mod-side library, never engine code.
- **`nimu/DumbKidLol.hx`, `meta/state/YourMomState.hx`, `editors/SexEditorState.hx`, `meta/state/BruhState.hx`, `meta/data/options/SocialCreditState.hx`.** Joke/griefing code; one file is a targeted harassment of a named person. Nothing to port.
- **`EpicState`** — a list linking to the author's other projects; it is a mod's credits page, not engine content.
- **`GameExitState`** — a Yes/No exit screen. Phoenix already exits through `openfl.system.System.exit(0)` where it means to, and a "quit confirmation" belongs with the platform/mobile pass (§11), not ported as a state.
- **`screenResTemp`/`screenScaleModeTemp` "apply on ACCEPT, revert on CANCEL" pattern.** Our `resolution`/`widescreen` apply immediately and are reversible from the same menu; adding temp fields would be a regression.
- **`ClientPrefs.shitWindow` + the four raw window ints as separate rows.** `AccuracyPresets` (§22.4) owns timing windows with bounded min/max refresh.
- **Its `NoteMovement` static `keyCount`/`playerKeyCount`.** That is the subset of §25.1's multikey work; Phoenix's `modchart/PlayField.keyCount` (`:20`) is already fed from `playerStrums.length` (`play/helpers/PlayStateModchart.hx:60`). Follow §25.1, do not add a second lane-count source.
- **`SustainStrip` (`FlxStrip`-based sustain geometry).** J64 needed a custom strip because its sustains are not per-sprite; ours are (`play/helpers/PlayStateModchart.hx:248` already iterates `notes` + `sustainNotes` and passes `isTapArrow`), and §12.5's skew-capable renderer is the place a strip would justify itself. Do not port it standalone.
- **`ReplayState`/`ReplaySelectState` (199 + 185 lines).** Its replay is a key-event clip replayed inside a `PlayState` subclass; §14 specifies a real deterministic replay format for Phoenix. Read it for ideas only — do **not** port the class, or we get a second replay format. (J64's `saveReplay` pref is likewise blocked on §14.)
- **`CustomOptions` doc pointing at upstream Psych PR #12307** — the *doc* there is marked outdated; take the behaviour (verified against its source), not the doc.

### 35.12. Suggested order

Cheapest first, each shippable alone; every one needs a `tests/haxe/run-*.sh` spec and a `docs/` page per house rule.

1. **The Lua binding batch** (§35.2): `getGameplayChangerValue`, `saveSettings`, `getSoundPitch`/`setSoundPitch`, `GetFromTypedGroup`, `onFocus`/`onFocusLost`, `setWindowMode`/`getWindowMode`. Six small additions, all three languages, no new systems.
2. **Save-data reset** (§35.9) + **colourblind filter row** (§35.3.3) + **results toggle** — three small, high-support-value rows.
3. **Per-mod options** (§35.3.1) — the biggest modder payoff in this section; it also unlocks §35.3.2 (achievement JSON) and §35.3.4 (modded gameplay changers) because they share the `Option`-from-JSON path.
4. **`modchart.json` loader + writer** (§35.6) — maps onto `ModchartManager` calls that already exist.
5. **Background video** (§35.7) and **toasts** (§35.8) — independent, both purely additive.
6. **Credits editor** (§35.5) and **in-engine mod folder setup** — the two editor gaps; the second is what makes modding possible on a phone.
7. **Minigame/extras hub** (§35.4) — wait for §21.1's Extras hub rather than inventing a second one.
8. **Script-registered custom modifiers** (§35.6) — the only **L** in the section, and the one that should be designed against §12.1's script manager and §17's script safety first.
9. **Artemis** (§35.2.6) — last, and only whole.

---

## 36. FUNKIN.AVI REUSE REVIEW — WHAT IT HAS THAT PHOENIX DOESN'T (2026-11-21)

**Numbering note:** this review has followed a moving `main` — it started as §27 and is now **§36**, because other audit sections kept landing while it was in review (§27 Mixtape, §28 FunkinAVI-V2, §29 Funkin' Yarn, §30 FNF: Doors, §31 Seiun, §32 Super Plus, §33 Candidate Haxelibs, §34 Cheese Engine, §35 Joalor64). All internal references are `§36.x`. **§28 is not a duplicate of this section:** it audits the sibling repo [FunkinAVI-V2-Source](https://github.com/ThatOneSillyGuyDon/FunkinAVI-V2-Source) (a different, later tree whose licence is still a placeholder) and focuses on modchart/modifier *plumbing* — custom modifier plugins, `modchart.json`, `SustainStrip`, `ALSOFT_CONF`, spectrum bars, song cards. This section audits the released **Funkin.AVI** mod (Apache 2.0) and focuses on the **scripting API, options, events and editor/modder surface**. Read both. The genuine overlaps are cross-referenced rather than merged: §36.5 script-scope ↔ §28.1 editor script host; §36.4 unlocks ↔ §28.2 unlock conditions; §36.5 `composer`/`charter` ↔ §32.10 `song_meta.json`; §36.1 memory bindings → §32.8.

Source: [ThatOneSillyGuyDon/Funkin.AVI](https://github.com/ThatOneSillyGuyDon/Funkin.AVI) at `e59e220` ("oh my god funkin.avi v3 CONFIRMED to release TOMORROW", 2026-08-14), read in full against this tree at `443ae01` and never built or run here, same sibling-audit style as §23–§26. **Apache 2.0, same as ours, so license-compatible — port with credits** to the Funkin.AVI team, and per the rule at the top of this file every port lands **on by default with a toggle**.

What it actually is: a heavily modded **Psych 0.5.2h-era** fork (its own `Project.xml` still says `packageName="com.shadowmario.psychengine"`), 176 `.hx` files (148 excluding their vendored `flixel/`, `lime/`, `openfl/`, `animateatlas/`, `gamejolt/` and `vlc/` copies), of which one (`source/PlayState.hx`) is 443 KB / 14,263 lines of monolithic gameplay, plus copies of `flixel/`, `lime/`, `openfl/`, `animateatlas/`, `gamejolt/`, `vlc/` and a `systools/` tree. It is a *mod*, not an engine: nearly everything specific to its songs, weeks, characters, episodes, menu characters and shaders-for-one-song is **deliberately excluded here** per the request that started this section, and it is listed in §36.7 so nobody re-audits it.

**Method.** Every `.hx` in their `source/` (excluding the vendored libs) was read; their 167 Lua globals (`Lua_helper.add_callback`) and 30 script hooks were diffed mechanically against Phoenix's 362 unique Lua globals (604 registrations across hosts) and 35 hooks; `ClientPrefs` (67 fields), the option menus, `PauseSubState`, `ChartingState`, `GameplayChangersSubstate`, `Shaders.hx`, `ModsMenuState`, `Highscore` and the `systools/` tree were compared field by field. Anything already in §0–§26 is cross-referenced, not repeated. Line numbers are their tree unless the path is `source/`-relative to ours.

### 36.1. Scripting API — the Lua surface they have and we don't

Our value diff is deliberately small, and that is the headline: **Phoenix's Lua API is a strict superset of Funkin.AVI's except for the 19 names below**: 15 are the slot-numbered effect batch (14 `addEffect*` + `die`), one (`noteTweenScale`) exists here under `noteTweenScaleX`/`noteTweenScaleY`, and only three (`openBrowser`, `loadCredits`, `clearUnusedMemory`) are real gaps. Everything else their 167 globals can reach, ours can reach too — usually under a more specific name. There are **no missing script hooks**: a mechanical diff of their 30 `callOnLuas` event names against our 35 leaves only the two `goodNoteHit`/`opponentNoteHit` spellings, and those reach scripts here under exactly those names through a ternary (`play/helpers/PlayStateNotes.hx:456`, `:625`), so they simply don't appear in a literal grep. We are ahead by seven hooks (`onCreate`, `onGhostTap`, `onResumeCountdownStart`, `onResumeCountdownTick`, `onSectionHit`, `onSpawnNote`, `onUpdateScore`), and they ship no Python or HScript host at all.

- **[P3] `openBrowser(link)`.** Their `FunkinLua.hx:830` callback wraps their `CoolUtil.browserLoad` (`CoolUtil.hx:201`); ours has the identical helper (`backend/CoolUtil.hx:544` `browserLoad`, used by `states/CreditsState.hx:222`, `states/OutdatedState.hx:150`, `backend/CrashHandler.hx:500`) but **nothing in `source/psychlua/` registers it**, so a mod cannot open its GameBanana/GameJolt page or a tutorial link. Register `openBrowser` (and the alias `openUrl`) next to `changeCursor` in `psychlua/callbacks/MiscCallbacks.hx`, returning `false` on `#if web`/mobile where the launch is a no-op, and route it through the same allowlist check `ModSecurity` already applies to file paths so a script cannot open arbitrary `file://`/`javascript:` URLs. Effort: **S**.
- **[P3] `loadCredits(?music, ?forceMenuMusic)`.** `FunkinLua.hx:271` switches to `CreditsState` and starts either the given track or `freakyMenu`, so a mod can roll its own credits from a song script. There is **no script-callable state switch to an engine screen at all** here: `psychlua/StateScripts.switchState` (`:198`) and `openSubState` (`:212`) can only reach `data/states/<name>.hx` hosts (`states/ModState`, `states/substates/ModSubState`), so `loadCredits` has to be built as the first entry of a small, explicit "open a built-in screen" table (`CreditsState`, `AchievementsMenuState`, `StatsState`, `OptionsState`, `ModsMenuState`, `FreeplayState`, each behind its existing `#if` and a name allowlist) rather than as one hardcoded function. Their extra `?music` argument is worth keeping: `CreditsState` picks its own track today. Effort: **S–M**.
- **[P3] `clearUnusedMemory()` / `clearStoredMemory()` (the *binding* only).** `FunkinLua.hx:304` exposes `Paths.clearUnusedMemory()` and returns `true`. We have the exact helper (`backend/Paths.hx`, plus `backend/LowMemory` policies and the `Options -> Optimization -> Free Memory Now` row at `options/OptimizationSubState.hx:49`) and **no script binding**, so a TV-sized mod chart cannot free an old stage between songs without a reload. Register `clearUnusedMemory` and the narrower `clearStoredMemory` in `MiscCallbacks.hx`, and make them respect the active `MemoryProfile` (a script must not undercut `LOW_MEMORY_MODE`'s preload decisions) and return the freed-byte count from `backend.MemoryLedger` so a script can log what it saved. Effort: **S**. Note the memory *system* itself is already reviewed and declared covered in **§32.8** (`AssetCache` + `MemoryLedger` + `MemoryProfiles` + `LowMemory` + `PoolStats` + `Paths.clearUnusedMemory`); the only thing this entry adds is the missing *script name*, so it is a few lines next to the existing callbacks, not new machinery.
- **Already named differently: `die(camera)`.** Their `die` (`FunkinLua.hx:1865`) is "remove every shader from this camera". Ours is `clearShadersFromCamera` / `clearEffects` (`psychlua/callbacks/EffectCallbacks.hx:76`, `:228`) plus the named-filter registry (`clearFilters`, `psychlua/callbacks/FilterCallbacks.hx:156`), so nothing is missing — but a Psych 0.5-era mod calling `die` gets a nil-call error. Both this and `addEffect1..14` belong in `psychlua/callbacks/DeprecatedCallbacks.hx`, which already carries thirteen psych-0.6-era aliases (`objectPlayAnimation`, `luaSpriteMakeGraphic`, `musicFadeIn`, …, `:15`-`:148`) and is exactly the right home; see the shader entry below for the slot table.
- **Covered, do not port: `noteTweenScale`.** Their version tweens `scale.x`/`scale.y` together (`FunkinLua.hx:568`). Ours has `noteTweenScaleX`/`noteTweenScaleY` (`psychlua/callbacks/TweenCallbacks.hx:288`, `:302`) and all three hosts get it (`psychlua/pystdlib/PyTweenLib.hx:190`). A two-value wrapper is a one-line compat alias, not a feature.
- **Checked and already better:** their `setPropertyAdvanced`/`getPropertyAdvanced` dotted-class helpers are **commented out in their own source** (`FunkinLua.hx:2197`-era `/*…*/`), so `setPropertyFromClass` + dotted paths (`psychlua/callbacks/PropertyCallbacks.hx:129`) already covers the shape they abandoned. Their `Lua_helper` sets only 76 globals; ours sets 93 and publishes `curDecBeat`/`curDecStep`, `songPath`, `difficultyPath`, `curStage`, `modFolder`, `curSong` and the `Function_Stop`/`Function_Continue` pair they have too. They expose `io` (`FunkinLua.hx:116`) — **do not copy that**; §6's sandbox and `ModSecurity.luaBootstrap` deliberately decide IO per mod.

### 36.2. Rendering, effects and shaders

Phoenix already ships 40+ effect pairs (`source/shaders/`), the six OpenFL filters, the chainable Flixel effects and a named warm-up registry (§0, §23.2, `docs/SHADERS.md`). What Funkin.AVI adds on top is not a new shader list — it is **two shapes we don't have**: a slot-numbered effect API and stage/song code that attaches effects itself instead of asking a script.

- **[P2] `VhsShader` (noise + tape wobble).** The one effect in their `Shaders.hx` (`VhsEffect` `:378`, `VhsShader` `:407`) that is genuinely a *different* look from everything we own. Their `VCRDistortionShader` is the shadertoy VCR chain we already have (`shaders/VCRDistortionShader.hx`), `VCRFuzz` (§23.2) is a steady simplex wobble, `OldTV`/`TVStatic` are static-and-roll, `VCRBorder` is a frame — but **none of them does VHS's two signature tricks**: the *head-switching noise band* that travels up the frame (`noi`/`stripes()` driven by `iTime/2.`) and the *per-scanline horizontal tearing with a sliding window* (`look.x += sin(look.y*10. + iTime)/50. * onOff(…) * window`), plus `screenDistort()`'s barrel pre-warp. Their implementation is two texture fetches and its two live uniforms are `noisePercent` and `intensityVHS`, which is phone-affordable; drop their unused `sampler2D noiseTexture` (declared, never sampled, so every driver still pays to bind it). Port as `shaders.VHSEffect`/`VHSShader` on `backend.FlxFixedShader`, register it in `ShaderWarmupRegistry` under `vhs`, expose `addVHSEffect(camera, noisePercent, intensity)` from `psychlua/callbacks/EffectCallbacks.hx` (one registration covers Lua/Python/HScript), and **fix what we know is broken**: their `rand()` uses `mod(dt,3.14)` with a comment about `highp` crashing, so it is not a hash at all on some GPUs — replace it with the same float hash §23.2 used for `TVStatic` (no `uvec`, no `texture()`), and pre-multiply by `openfl_Alpha` like the other ports. Add a `VhsShader` parameter table to `docs/SHADERS.md` and an `example_mods/scripts/RetroShadersExample.lua` entry. Spec: extend `tests/haxe/run-retro-shaders.sh` (it generates its stub from the `uniform` declarations, so it catches an uninitialised uniform for free). Effort: **S–M**. Credits: shader by its original author as noted in their header (the `VhsShader` body is the well-known VHS shadertoy); port with attribution.
- **[P2] Slot-numbered drop-in effects (`addEffect1`…`addEffect14`).** Their whole shader API is fourteen positional functions with hardcoded presets, and their `PlayState.addShaderToCamera(camera, effect)` is called straight from stage code (`PlayState.hx:1015-1042`). We already have **every** one of those effects — the slot table maps 1:1 onto existing registrations — so the gap is compatibility, not capability: `addEffect1` chromatic = `addChromaticAbberationEffect` (`psychlua/callbacks/EffectCallbacks.hx:47`), `2` scanline = `addScanlineEffect`, `3` grain = `addGrainEffect`, `4` tiltshift = `addTiltshiftEffect`, `5` VCR = `addVCREffect`, `6` glitch = `addGlitchEffect`, `7` pulse = `addPulseEffect` (ours additionally warms it at load, `play/helpers/PlayStateShaders.hx:72`), `8` distortBG = `addDistortionEffect` (`:96`, same `DistortBGEffect`), `9` invert = `addInvertEffect`, `10`/`11` greyscale = `addGreyscaleEffect` (they really do register the same `GreyscaleEffect` twice), `12` 3D = `add3DEffect` (same `ThreeDEffect`, same four arguments), `13` bloom = `addBloomEffect` — **note their argument order is inverted** (`intensity, blurSize` becomes `bloomSize/512., intensity`), so an alias must keep *their* order and flip it, or old scripts get a 1/512th-scale bloom — and `14` VHS = the effect in the previous entry. Their `die` = `clearShadersFromCamera`. Two things to port, and the second is the real win:
  - **Legacy slot aliases** in `psychlua/callbacks/DeprecatedCallbacks.hx`: `addEffect1..14` mapping **by role** onto the existing `addChromaticAbberationEffect`, `addScanlineEffect`, `addGrainEffect`, `addTiltshiftEffect`, `addVCREffect`, `addGlitchEffect`, `addPulseEffect`, `addDistortionEffect`, `addInvertEffect`, `addGreyscaleEffect` (`10`/`11` both), `add3DEffect`, `addBloomEffect`, `addVHSEffect` (from the entry above), plus `die` → `clearShadersFromCamera`. Keep their argument order so existing 0.5-era scripts run unchanged, emit the usual deprecation trace pointing at the modern name, and honour `ClientPrefs.shaders` exactly as the modern callbacks do. Effort: **S**.
  - **Declarative effect attachment in the stage file.** Extend `StageFile` (`data/StageData.hx:28`) from `"shaders": ["rain"]` to `"effects": [{"name":"chromatic","camera":"hud","params":{"offset":0.003}}, …]` and have `PlayStateShaders.warmUp()` *attach* what it warms, so a stage JSON is enough to ship a filtered stage with no Haxe class and no script. That closes a real gap: their stages are Haxe-only, so this would actually be a step *past* Funkin.AVI rather than a port, and it fits the untaken CNE "declarative stage node graph" idea in §12.2. Params resolve against each effect's existing public setters (`setChrome`, `setBlur`, `setStrength`, …), unknown cameras/effects fail loudly at load with the file name, and the same list feeds `ShaderWarmup` so nothing compiles on the first bop. Effort: **M**.
- **[P3] `BlendModeEffect` + `OverlayShader` (lighten-blend tint).** `source/BlendModeEffect.hx` (30 lines) plus `source/OverlayShader.hx` implement exactly one blend: `blendLighten`, a Photoshop-style *lighten* with an opacity, driven by a single `uBlendColor` vec4. We have `setBlendMode` in Lua (`psychlua/callbacks/SpriteCallbacks.hx:389`) but that only exposes OpenFL's fixed `BlendMode` enum, which has no "lighten with an alpha knob", and our `RGBPalette`/`ColorSwap` (`objects/ColorSwap.hx`) are hue/sat/brt shifts, not blends. Port it as `shaders/BlendModeShader` + a `setSpriteBlendColor(tag, color, ?opacity)`/`setSpriteBlendMode(tag, 'lighten'|'darken'|'overlay'|'screen')` pair; their single-mode version is small enough to ship the other three common modes while we're in there (each is a 10-line GLSL change). Respect `ClientPrefs.shaders`, and keep the sprite's own alpha composited (their shader **overwrites** `gl_FragColor` with the blended RGB and loses `base.a`, which makes a tinted sprite square). Effort: **S**. Credits: their header credits the shadertoy source; port with attribution.
- **[P3] Per-stage/song shader attachment is the real gap; `IndieCrossShaderShit.hx` is not.** Their `FXHandler.UpdateColors` is a 700-line hardcoded colour-matrix switch for one mod's colourblind mode; our `backend/ScreenFilters` presets (`invert`, `greyscale`, `sepia`, `gameboy`, `nightvision`, `sepiacold`, three dichromacy simulations, identity) plus `backend/Accessibility.colorblindPreset` already cover the *feature*, and §0's "in-engine Options toggle for colour matrices" is the remaining half. **Do not port their file.**
- **[P3] `WiggleEffect` and the `Effect` base class.** Their `WiggleEffect` (`source/WiggleEffect.hx`, enum `DREAMY`/`WAVY`/`HEAT_WAVE_HORIZONTAL`/`HEAT_WAVE_VERTICAL`/`FLAG`) is the stock HaxeFlixel demo one; we ship `shaders/WiggleEffect.hx` + `WiggleEffectLua.hx` and expose `addWiggleEffect`. Checked: our `shaders/WiggleEffect.hx` already carries **all five** of their members *plus* `HEAT_WAVE_BOTH` (`:6-13`), so there is nothing to port here at all — the file is listed only to close it. Their `Effect` base class (`source/Shaders.hx:1730`, a one-method `setValue` reflection shim) is superseded by `shaders/Effect.hx` + `backend/FlxFixedShader` and is not a port either. Effort: **XS**.

### 36.3. Gameplay & HUD options

Their option set is a Psych 0.5.2 set with a few genuinely extra rows. Everything below was checked against `backend/ClientPrefs.hx` (216 fields) and the option menus; the ones marked *not in our tree at all* have no `ClientPrefs` field, no row, and no read site.

- **[P2] Judgement counter panel + `hideJudgement` pref (Visuals and UI).** Their `PlayState.hx:2531` builds a live `judgementCounter:FlxText` (`:412`) behind `ClientPrefs.hideJudgement` (`ClientPrefs.hx:39`, option "Hide Judgement Counter" at `options/GameplaySettingsSubState.hx:90`), showing `Marvs / Sicks / Goods / Bads / Shits` from counters they bump inline (`marvelouses++` at `:7799`, `:7828`, `:7858`), with **two layouts** switched by a `hudStyle` local (`'Psych'` = centred text, `'Demolition'` = a second box). **We have no such widget and no `hideJudgement` pref**: `perfects`/`sicks`/`goods`/`bads` are tracked on `PlayState` (`:314-315`) and read by `states/ResultsState.hx:122-123`, but in-song the only per-judgement feedback is the transient `Popup`/judgement text, and `ClientPrefs` has `hideHud` and nothing else in that area. This is one `FlxText` on `camHUD` over counters that already exist, so it is cheap; it also fills the "judgement breakdown on the HUD" half of the Mic'd Up ranking idea (§0) and pairs with §22.5's per-profile visuals. Rows to respect: `hideHud` (their counter follows it, `:2553`), the new `hideJudgement`, `UI Scale`, `reducedMotion`; ship **one** layout (the `'Psych'` text) rather than porting `hudStyle`, and add a Lua setter pair (`setJudgementCounterVisible`, `getJudgementCounts`) so a modchart can drive it. Effort: **S**.
- **[P2] Per-judgement note splashes.** Their hit path splits one splash into three by rating: `spawnNoteSplashOnNoteSick` (`PlayState.hx:8842`), `...Marvelous` (`:8875`), `...Shit` (`:8903`), gated by `if (daRating == 'marvelous' && !note.noteSplashDisabled)` / `'sick'` / `'good'` / `'bad'` (their `marvelouses` pref, `ClientPrefs.hx:36`, is literally documented in their option text as "marvelouses rating will appear on the Judgment counter and will show when hit", `options/GameplaySettingsSubState.hx:153`, so it is a **splash + counter** gate, not a hit-window change — see the counter entry above). Ours is one effect for every hit: `play/helpers/PlayStateNoteHelpers.hx:207` `spawnNoteSplashOnNote(state, isDad, note)` picks the strum and calls `spawnNoteSplash` at `:214` with no rating argument at all, and the two call sites (`PlayStateNotes.hx:421`, `:592`) pass only a side flag. The data model is already there and unused for this purpose: `Note.noteSplashData` (`objects/Note.hx:58`) carries a per-note `texture`/RGB/alpha bundle, and `objects/NoteSplash.hx` `setupNoteSplash` *does* read `note.noteSplashData.texture` — so per-judgement splashes are mostly a matter of adding a `splashSkins` map (`sick`/`marvelous`/`good`/`bad` → texture) to the splash config, passing the rating down into `spawnNoteSplash`, and resolving the texture there when the note has no explicit `noteSplashData.texture`. Then a mod can ship `noteSplashes-sick`/`-marvelous` and an options row per tier (`splashType` already works this way, `options/VisualsUISubState.hx:75-89`). Costs one parameter and one lookup on a hot path, so **cache the resolved name per pool slot** rather than doing a `Paths.fileExists` per hit. Effort: **S–M**.
- **[P3] One `marvelouses` pref covering both tiers above.** Their single `ClientPrefs.marvelouses` (`ClientPrefs.hx:36`, default **on**) drives *both* the 5-tier vs 4-tier judgement counter and whether the marvelous splash branch runs, so the counters and the splash work above should share **one** pref rather than inventing two. **Do not** copy the name as a hit-window switch: our `perfectWindow` (`ClientPrefs.hx:444`) is the top window of a single ladder defined by `backend/AccuracyPresets` (§22.4 bounds it), and a "marvelous tier" here is a *presentation* choice — whether the top tier is labelled Marvelous in the counter, the judgement text and Results — not a new timing window. Implement as a label/presentation flag with that scope and the two entries above get it for free. Effort: **S**.
- **Found while checking, not a port: their "Auto-Restart".** Their `ClientPrefs.restart` (`ClientPrefs.hx:66`, option "Auto-Restart" at `options/GameplaySettingsSubState.hx:59`) does not retry the song: all four read sites (`PlayState.hx:8585`, `:8594`, `:8621`, `:8630`) are inside a **`/* … */` block** (`:8575-8598`) for their joke `'Error Note'`, and the next thing they did was *add a ninth life to the error screen* rather than restart. Even if it shipped, its action is `TitleState.restartGame()` (`TitleState.hx:425`), which is `Sys.command`-re-launching the exe, **not** replaying the song. So there is nothing to port, and our real auto-retry entry (§22.4, `[P3]` `autoRetry` → `doDeathCheck()` at `play/PlayState.hx:2723`) already describes the right shape — cross-referenced, and this time not duplicated.
- **[P1] Missing generic chart events.** Diffed their `ChartingState.eventStuff` (`source/editors/ChartingState.hx:91-120`, the list their editor offers) and `triggerEventNote` against our `play/helpers/PlayStateEvents.hx` (37 `case` labels) and our own `eventStuff` (`editors/ChartingState.hx:92`). Everything they have that is *generic* rather than song-specific — and that we do **not** handle — is: **`Lyrics`** (two-values-per-section subtitle lines; the closest thing we have is §23.3's planned `Add Subtitle`, so implement them together, one engine-side lyric track that both events feed), **`Screen Fade`**, **`Flash Screen`** / **`Flash Background`** (their `Screen Fade` normally ramps camera `fade` — note their `Flash Screen` is *`FlxG.camera.flash`*, which we cannot simply copy: `FlxCamera.flash` needs `ScreenFilters`-style handling on our multi-camera setup), **`Cinematic Bars`** (top/bottom letterbox on `camOther`), **`Alter HUD Transparency`** (a real gap: we have `hideHud` and per-object alpha but no event that dims the HUD for a cutscene), **`Set Strum Visibility`**, **`Alter Camera Zoom`** / **`Alter Camera Bouncing`** (we have `Camera Bopping` on/off, not a *ramp*), **`Do Health Tween`** (we have instant `Set Property` health and `Tween Camera Zoom`, not a health ramp), **`Fade Character`** (their version is a crude ±0.05 alpha nudge, `ChartingState.hx:105` — implement as a proper 0–1 tween with duration), and **`Scroll Type`**, which is the event form of the `Scroll Type` **gameplay-changers** option we already ship (`states/helpers/GameplayChangersHelpers.hx:19`, consumed at `play/helpers/PlayStateChartLoader.hx:36`) so the plumbing exists and only needs an event wrapper. `Tween Song Lenght` maps to our existing `Fake Song Length`; `Bozos going mimir mode`, `Spotlights`, `Relapse Shoot`, `BG Freaks Expression` and `Trigger BG Ghouls` are song/stage-specific and stay excluded (§36.7). Each handler needs an `eventStuff` entry in `editors/ChartingState.hx:92` (the dropdown is built from that array, so the description text is part of the work), an `onEvent` pass-through that **must not** be swallowed, and a `Reset`/`Cancel` path so a mid-song event can be undone — that last part is what makes `Cinematic Bars`/`Screen Fade`/`Alter HUD Transparency` worth doing together rather than as nine one-offs. Effort: **M–L**.
- **[P2] Master "Chart events" switch (`eventsEnabled`, bool, default on, Gameplay).** Their `ClientPrefs.events` (`ClientPrefs.hx:21`) is checked at the top of *every* event branch (`PlayState.hx:6493`, `:6500`, `:7216`) so a player can disable the whole chart-event track while keeping charts intact — which is not the same as the per-event `onEvent` script hook, and is the first thing to reach for when a stage event is what is breaking a chart on a weak device. **We have no such pref**: `source/play/helpers/PlayStateEvents.hx` runs every event unconditionally, and `ClientPrefs` has no `events` field. Add `eventsEnabled` (Gameplay → "Chart Events", default **on**) checked once in `PlayStateEvents.triggerEvent` before the `switch`, so custom events (`onEvent`) still fire — the flag must gate the **built-in** handlers only, or every mod event dies with it. Related: their per-mechanic twin `ClientPrefs.mechanics` (`PlayState.hx:1783+`) gates their *song* mechanics; ours are per-note-type data (`backend/NoteTypesConfig`, §23.6), so **do not** port that one. Effort: **S**.
- **[P2] Player-facing cursor style (`cursorStyle`, string over a mod-scannable list, default `Default`, Visuals and UI).** Their row is a **hardcoded five-entry list** — `new Option('Cursor Style:', 'What Texture Should The Cursor Be?', 'cursor', 'string', 'Default', ['Default', 'Hand', 'Mickey', 'Silhouette', 'The Eye'])` (`options/VisualsUISubState.hx:55-61`) — loaded as `FlxG.mouse.load("assets/images/mouse/" + ClientPrefs.cursor + ".png")` in three places (`BetterPreload.hx:399`, `TitleState.hx:176`, and the `onChangeCursorSkin` handler at `:226`). So the *feature* is "choose an OS cursor image", and their *mechanism* is a hardcoded list of one mod's art — we should take the feature and not the list. We already have the loading half: `changeCursor(path, visible, loadDefault, scale, xOffset, yOffset)` (`psychlua/callbacks/MiscCallbacks.hx:67`) does `FlxG.mouse.load(Paths.image(path).bitmap, …)` with a graceful default fallback, and a `pointingCursorStyle` pref exists for the *link-hover* sprite only (`ClientPrefs.hx:201`, exposed only in the Super Secret Debug Menu at `options/SuperSecretDebugMenu.hx:72`). What is missing is a player-facing row and a folder: add `ClientPrefs.cursorStyle`, scan `images/cursors/*.png` plus a `list.txt` exactly like `splashType` does (`options/VisualsUISubState.hx:75-89`), always insert `Default` first, persist it, and apply on boot and on change through one helper shared by the option row, `TitleState` and any state that installs a cursor. Keep `pointingCursorStyle` separate — that is the hover swap, not the base cursor. Effort: **S**.
- **[P3] Anti-mash toggle (`antiMash`, bool, default on, Gameplay).** Their key handler sets `canMiss = false` while **any** hittable player note exists on *any* lane (`PlayState.hx:8166-8172`), so mashing through a dense section never produces a `noteMissPress`; it is exposed as "Enable Anti Mash" with the description "IDK WHAT THIS DOES" (`options/GameplaySettingsSubState.hx:104-106`). Our nearest pref is **`ezSpam`** ("More Spammable Inputs", `backend/ClientPrefs.hx:39`, option at `options/GameplaySettingsSubState.hx:130-134`, read at `play/helpers/PlayStateInput.hx:74` as `plrInputNotes.length > 2 && ClientPrefs.ezSpam`), which is the *same* player intent reached from the opposite end: it lets an extra press consume another pending note instead of eating a miss. Adding a second, `canMiss`-shaped switch would let a player contradict themselves (both on), so the cheapest correct move is to **widen `ezSpam`** — it already exists, is already default **off**, and already covers the case their flag is trying to — and to say so in its tooltip. If a separate row is ever wanted, make the two mutually exclusive in the UI and note in the entry that it is a convenience alias, not new behaviour. Effort: **S** (or **XS** for the tooltip-only version).
- **Found while checking, not a port: their "Live Difficulty" row is dead code.** `options/GameplaySettingsSubState.hx:82` has the whole option wrapped in `/* … */`, so their build never reads it and it has no read site in their `source/`. If an always-on difficulty label is ever wanted it belongs as a third text line in the judgement-counter panel above, not as a pref — so no entry.
- **[P3] Song/artist credit block in the pause screen (`showSongCredits`).** Their `PauseSubState` builds `composerCredit`/`charterCredit` texts from the chart (`PauseSubState.hx:144` reads `PlayState.SONG.composer`; the texts tween in at `:232-237`), and `composer`/`charter` are `SwagSong` **and** `Song` fields (`Song.hx:18-19`, `:40-41`). We have `objects/CreditsPopUp` for the *start-of-song* card (`songCredit`, `songCreditBarPath`, `songCreditIcon` in `data/Song.hx`) and **no** pause-screen composer/charter block, and `SwagSong` has **no `composer`/`charter` fields at all** — which also means our Chart Editor cannot write them (see §36.5). Add the two optional `SwagSong` fields (read-tolerantly, exactly like `onLoadJson` backfills the other later-added strings — `data/Song.hx:70-77` loops `['specialAudioName', 'specialEventsName', 'windowName', 'arrowSkin', 'splashSkin', 'songCredit', 'songCreditBarPath', 'songCreditIcon', …]` and writes `''` for anything missing, which is the pattern `composer`/`charter` must join), show them in the pause screen behind a row defaulting to on, and let a mod's `pack.json` supply defaults so a chart without them isn't blank. Effort: **S**. Credits: field names and layout idea from Funkin.AVI.
- **Checked, already covered — do not port:** their "Show Outdated Version" (we have `UpdateChecker` + `states/OutdatedState`), "Lane Underlay"/"Lane Underlay Alpha" (DONE, §21.3), "Note Size" (`ClientPrefs.men`, their `Note.hx:460`/`StrumNote.hx:115` scale by it — we have `noteScale`/lane-spacing as §22.3, whose entry names the same five hardcoded `0.7` sites), "Persistent Cached Data" (`imagesPersist` — theirs is commented out in `GraphicsSettingsSubState.hx:107-115` and ours tracks + disposes through `backend/Paths`/`MemoryLedger`, which is strictly better), "Play Instrumental In Freeplay" (we have a real `MusicPlayer` preview + `instPlaying`, §22.5), "Screen Resolution"/"Fullscreen" (`resolution` + `backend.WindowMode`, §22.2), "Low Quality"/"Anti-Aliasing"/"Framerate"/"Shaders"/"Optimize Shaders" (`lowQuality`, `globalAntialiasing`, `framerate`, `shaders`, §21.3/§22.2), "Controller Mode" (`controllerMode` + §15), "Ghost Tapping"/"Disable Reset Button"/"Hitsound Volume" (`ghostTapping`, `noReset`, `hitsoundVolume`), "Rating Offset"/"Safe Frames"/the four hit windows (bounded, §22.4 `AccuracyPresets` — theirs lets `safeFrames` go to 99999999, which we deliberately do not), "Hide HUD"/"Score Text Zoom"/"Long Health Bar"/"Health Bar Transparency"/"FlxBar alpha" (`hideHud`, `scoreZoom`, `healthBarStyle`, `healthBarAlpha`), "Camera Zooms" (`camZooms`) and "Camera Movement" (`camMovement` — **already an open entry in §25.2**, `[P3]` line 682, so this is a cross-ref not a second entry), "Winning Icons" (icon-frame logic in `PlayState.hx:2570-2600`), "Center Menu" (our menus are layout-driven through `backend.ScreenLayout`), "FPS Counter" (`debug/FPSCounter`), "Debug mode" (`SuperSecretDebugMenu`).

### 36.4. Screens, menus & data

Their `ExtrasState` / `EpisodesState` / `LegacyState` / `VoidState` / `FreeplayCategoryState` are **one mod's song lists**, copied five times — excluded. What is *structural* about that copy-paste is real, though, and it maps onto entries we already have open:

- **[P2] Freeplay categories (nested freeplay pages).** Their `EpicSelectorWOOO` + `FreeplayCategoryState` + `FreeplaySelector` / `FreeplaySelectorBETA` implement a category picker in front of Freeplay: a `freeplayCats` array (`EpicSelectorWOOO.hx:95` → `['Legacy', 'Episodes', 'Extras', 'Covers']`), a lock text when a category is empty (`noCovers`, `:175`), and then a normal Freeplay state per category. **We have one flat Freeplay** whose only grouping is `backend.WeekData` weeks plus the per-song filter in `backend/FreeplayFilter.hx` (`FreeplayFilterState` = query / sort / mod / favourites, `:94-117` → `states/substates/FreeplayFilterSubState`), so a 300-song mod list is one scroll. This is the "**categorised / nested freeplay**" half of §12.2's `meta.json` work and §22.5's auto-preview: add a `categories` list (name, icon, colour, song selector, optional unlock condition) that Freeplay reads — default **off** (a single "All songs" page) so nothing changes for existing mods — and implement the picker as a `FreeplayFilterState` *field* (a category-key exactly like `mod`/`sort` above) plus one more row in `FreeplayFilterSubState`, not as five copy-pasted states. Effort: **M**.
- **[P3] Per-song unlock / progression states (`songUnlock`).** **Overlaps §28.2's "Per-song unlock conditions"** — that entry proposes the same metadata-driven `{week, song, achievement, deaths}` shape from the AVI-V2 tree; take one implementation, and prefer §28.2's declarative form over the string-per-song model below.  Their `FPClientPrefs` (`source/FPClientPrefs.hx`) keeps a string per song per episode — `'beaten'` / `'unlocked'` / `'completed'` — and both the Freeplay list and the locked-icon swap read it (`ExtrasState.hx:66-90`, and the category list in `EpicSelectorWOOO.hx:95`). Ours stops at **week-level** locking (`FreeplayStateHelpers.weekIsLocked`, `backend/WeekData.hiddenUntilUnlocked`), so a mod cannot gate an individual song behind another song. Generalise into `backend/Hiscore`-adjacent data (`songUnlockState` per mod+song+difficulty) with three states, a `pack.json`/week-JSON declaration of what unlocks what, and a `ClientPrefs.hideLockedSongs` row; keep it **off by default** because it changes what Freeplay lists. Ties into §13 (save data) and §36.5's freeplay editor. Effort: **M**.
- **Already tracked elsewhere: an "Extras" hub fed by mod metadata.** Their `ExtrasState` is a Freeplay clone for bonus songs and their main menu reaches it through a category picker; our planned equivalent is **§21.1 `[P2]` Extras hub (`states/ExtrasState`, `pack.json` `menus`)**, which is the more general shape (mod-declared screens, not one mod's song list), so there is nothing to port on top of it — see the cross-reference there rather than a second entry here.
- **Checked, nothing to port: the achievements menu.** Their `achievementsStuff` 4-tuple (`source/Achievements.hx:13-31`) renders as a scrolling list with a description pane and a locked placeholder; **we already have all of it and more** — `states/AchievementsMenuState.hx` has `descText`/`progressTxt` panes (`:28`, `:158`), a real locked-art swap (`achievements/lockedachievement`, `:126`), and it *hides* hidden achievements until unlocked (`:86-88`) rather than showing a "Locked" row. Our model also carries `maxScore`/`maxDecimals`/`mod`/`ID` (`backend/Achievements.hx:12-27`). Only their achievement *list* is content and excluded (§36.7).
- **Checked, already covered — do not port:** their `LanguageState` + `options/spanish/*` + `SpanishTitleState` + `PauseSpanishState` + `CreditsSpanishState` (a **hardcoded two-language** build: `ClientPrefs.language == "Spanish"` string compares sprinkled through `PlayState.hx:2656`, `:5352`, `:8636`, `:13925`). We have **no** localisation system, which is §12.4's "Localization / translations" — their approach is exactly the anti-pattern that entry should avoid (copy-pasted state files per language), so it is a data point *against*, not a port. Their `DisclaimerState` / `ShadersState` / `WednesdayInfedilityWarnScreen` / `NoticeState` first-run warnings are covered by our `states/FlashingState` + `states/StartupState` + `showPhotosensitivityWarning` (§21.4); their `LegacyState`/`VoidState`/`FunnyDVDState`/`CustomFPMenuTestState`/`MemeGameOverState`/`TestCredits` are joke or per-song screens; `DumbKidLol.hx` imports eight non-existent modules and is dead code.

### 36.5. Editors & modder tooling

Their editor is a Psych 0.5.2-era `ChartingState` with the usual 0.5 additions; ours is a split `editors/charting/*` and is ahead on undo/redo, save/load, waveform, tooltips and the Modchart Lab. Four things they do that we don't:

- **[P2] Chart meta fields `composer` / `charter`.** **Overlaps §32.10's `song_meta.json`**, which already proposes a per-song `composer` field and a Meta tab in the editor — if that lands, this becomes "expose the same two fields in the *chart* JSON too (and read them in the pause screen)" rather than a separate metadata system. Their chart format carries both (`Song.hx:18-19` on `SwagSong`, `:40-41` on `Song`) and the pause screen prints them ("By: " + `PlayState.SONG.composer`, `PauseSubState.hx:144`). Our `SwagSong` (`data/Song.hx:34-36`) has only the three `songCredit*` fields a popup needs, so a chart written elsewhere **loses its author data the first time it is saved here** — the encoder simply does not have the keys. This is now a small job because the plumbing is already in place for the neighbouring fields: `onLoadJson` backfills later-added strings from a list (`:70-77`), the editor already has `creditInputText` wired to `_song.songCredit` (`editors/ChartingState.hx:1262`), and `ChartingState`'s new-chart template (`:343-345`) lists the defaults. Add both to that list, that template, and one text field each beside the existing credit inputs; then surface them in `PauseSubState` behind a `showSongCredits` row (default on) and let `pack.json` supply per-mod fallbacks so older charts are not blank. Do **not** overload `songCredit` for this — that one is the start-of-song popup card and has a bar/icon layout of its own. Effort: **S**.
- **[P2] Restore from the editor's own autosaves (we write them, nothing reads them).** Their `ChartingState.hx:474` has a **Load Autosave** button that restores the last autosave directly from the save slot (`Song.parseJSONshit(FlxG.save.data.autosave)`), i.e. a crash mid-edit has an in-editor way back. Ours is half-built in a *better* place and then unplugged: `editors/charting/ChartingSaveLoad.saveLevel` writes a **timestamped** copy to `backups/<song>_<date>.json` on every autosave and prunes to the newest 5 (`ChartingSaveLoad.hx:192-225`), the timer and indicator exist (`editors/ChartingState.hx:562`, `:578`), `ClientPrefs.autosaveCharts`/`autosaveTime` exist (`:395-396`) — and **nothing in `source/` ever reads `backups/`** (verified: the string appears only in `ChartingSaveLoad.hx` itself, plus unrelated `VLog`/`SettingsBackup` rotation). The only recovery button is `ChartingUISections.hx:82` **"Load Backup From File"**, which calls `ChartingSaveLoad.promptBackup` (`:31`) → `MobileFilePicker.openFile` → `Song.parseJSON` → `FlxG.resetState()`: it loads *whatever JSON the user picks* and **restarts the editor with it**, which is both the wrong affordance (the user has to find `backups/` by hand) and destructive (no diff, no undo). Fix: a `Restore Autosave` submenu that lists `backups/` newest-first with mtime and note count, loads the pick into the existing undo stack (`ChartingSaveLoad.saveUndo`, `:62`) instead of `resetState`, and refuses silently if the chart on disk is newer. Their single-slot version is the *worse* design — port the affordance, not the mechanism. Effort: **S–M**.
- **[P1] Script scope flags (`inChartEditor` / `inEditor` / `inPlayState`) and note-type scripts in the chart editor.** **Overlaps §28.1's "A script host inside the chart editor"** — that entry reaches the same `EditorLua` from the modchart/modifier side and proposes the same `inChartEditor` fix; do them as one change and treat this entry as the *notetype* half and that one as the *modchart* half. Two halves, one fix. **(a) The flags are dead.** Their `FunkinLua.hx:95` sets `inChartEditor` to `false` just like ours, but their `editors.EditorLua` overrides it to **`true`** (`:66`) for its own host; ours is **hardcoded `false` in all three hosts and nothing ever sets it true** (`psychlua/FunkinLua.hx:200`, `psychlua/HScript.hx:335`, `psychlua/PythonScript.hx:177`) and therefore tells a script nothing — every mod that guards editor-unsafe behaviour with `if inChartEditor then return end` is silently running it in the editor, which is exactly the class of bug that produces "opening the chart editor crashed". **(b) The editor never runs note-type scripts at all.** Their playtest host `editors/EditorPlayState` spawns each custom note type's Lua through a stripped-down `EditorLua` (`editors/EditorLua.hx`: about ten callbacks — `getProperty`/`setProperty`/`…FromGroup`/`removeFromGroup`/`getColorFromHex`/`setGraphicSize`/`scaleObject`/`updateHitbox` — `set('inChartEditor', true)` at `:66`, and one `call('onCreate', [])` at `:185`, created per note type at `EditorPlayState.hx:114-121` and then **stopped after 0.1 s**, which is why the note type's properties stick but its per-frame behaviour does not), so the editor shows what a note type actually *does*. Ours: `editors/EditorPlayState.hx:31` is a standalone `MusicBeatState` with **zero** references to `FunkinLua`/`custom_notetypes`/`NoteTypesConfig`, `editors/charting/ChartingUISections.hx:1015-1035` only *lists* `custom_notetypes/*.lua` filenames for the note-type dropdown, and `backend/NoteTypesConfig` reads the `.txt` property side only. So a note type's colour/RGB/glow are previewed and its behaviour is not. **Do it as one change:** add a `PlayState.scriptScope`-style field set by each host (chart editor playtest → `'editor'`, normal play → `'play'`), wire `inChartEditor`/`inEditor`/`inPlayState` off it in all three hosts (Python and HScript included, or the flags stay half-dead), and give `EditorPlayState` a **read-only** script host that only registers the note-type-relevant callbacks (`setProperty`, `getProperty`, note/stum setters, `onCreatePost`, `goodNoteHit`, `noteMiss`) with `luaTrace` suppressed and file writes refused. Gate it behind a `ClientPrefs.chartEditorScripts` row (default **on** per the repo rule, with a real off switch) because it costs editor load time, and keep normal `PlayState` behaviour untouched. This is the highest-value item in the section because it makes an existing feature (note types) previewable *and* removes the flag misreporting that affects every script. Effort: **M**.
- **Checked, nothing to port: the chart editor's copy/swap buttons.** They have "Swap section", "Clear section" and a last-section recall; we have all three plus more (`editors/charting/ChartingUISections.hx:577` "Swap section", `:545` "Clear", `:588` "Copy last section", `:680`/`:704` "Clear Left/Right Side" for duet charts), and `:86` already has a separate "Load Events" button. Their `eventStuff`/`section` UI work adds nothing here — cross-referenced only so the read is closed.
- **Checked, already covered:** their `MasterEditorMenu` entries (Character / Week / Menu Character / Dialogue Portrait / Dialogue / Chart) all exist here (`editors/MasterEditorMenu.hx` + `states/…`), and we add Modchart Lab. Their `NoteSkinState` is commented out in their options (`options/OptionsState.hx:31`) — we ship a full `Note Skins` page.

### 36.6. Platform, mod storage & services

- **[P2] System dialogs + native menu bar for scripts, un-gated.** Their `systools/Dialogs.hx` gives any script `message`/`confirm`/`saveFile`/`openFile`/`folder` (`:37`–`:87`), and `systools/win/Menus.hx` exposes a real **native menu bar** (`addItem`, `addDivider`, `addSubmenu`, `show`, `showPopup`, 82 lines against `user32`). Ours exist but are **locked behind `#if META_HORROR`, off by default** (`MetaHorrorCallbacks.hx:13` registers `getClipboardText`/`setClipboardText`, `:181` registers `openFile`; `backend/MetaHorror.getClipboardText` returns `""` unless `-DMETA_HORROR`, and `project.hxp:510` ships it `false`), so a normal mod cannot copy a chart export or show a native "Save as…". Split the **non-intrusive** half out of the horror gate into a `backend/SystemShell` module: clipboard read/write (behind a per-mod permission in `ModSecurity`, logged like every other allowlist hit), `openFile`/`saveFile`/`openFolder` on top of the existing `mobile/files/MobileFilePicker` (`:57`–`:190`), `confirm`→`Prompt`/`Dialog.showYesNoWindows`, and the window menu bar — the last one is the only genuinely new native code in this whole section, and our `backend/native/include/phoenix_window.h` already owns the HWND, so it slots in beside `phoenixWindowMessageBox`. Everything stays `#if desktop`, degrades to `false` elsewhere, and the META_HORROR-only behaviours (wallpaper swap, taskbar hiding, window alpha) stay exactly where they are. Effort: **M** (menu bar), **S** each for dialogs/clipboard.
- **[P3] Per-mod save-data container.** Their `ClientPrefs.mechanics`/`FPClientPrefs` and their whole episode-lock model are per-mod *data* living in the **engine's** `FlxG.save` (`FlxG.save.data.huntedLock`, `…malfunctionLock`, one field per song) — exactly the save-bloat pattern §13 is trying to kill. Our scripts already have named save containers (`initSaveData`/`getDataFromSave`/`flushSaveData`, `psychlua/callbacks/SaveDataCallbacks.hx`). What is missing is **the same container for engine-side per-mod state** (unlock tables, per-mod counters) so a 40-song mod does not add 40 fields to `funkin`. Port the *container*, not their usage: one `mods/<mod>/save.json`-shaped `FlxSave` per mod, owned by `backend/Mods`, with the migration path in §13. Effort: **M**.
- **[P3] Mod `.zip` import parity.** Their `ModsMenuState.installMod()` is **commented out dead code** (`ModsMenuState.hx:653-700`, the whole `_file`/`FileReference` block); ours is real and reachable from two buttons (`states/ModsMenuState.hx:748` `importModFromZip` → `MobileFilePicker.openFile` → `mobile/files/ModZipInstaller.inspect` (`:170`) → conflict `Prompt` → `importZip` (`:207`)). **Nothing to port** — note it only so nobody "ports" their dead version.
- **[P3] Native process / file utilities.** Their `systools/FileUtils.getTempFolder`, `win/Tools.replaceExeIcon` + `createProcess`, `win/Display.setMode`/`isModeSupported`/`getScreenSize`, `win/Tray` (an SW-HX tray icon, effectively dead since `swhx` is not in their `Project.xml`) and `Registry` (`setValue`/`getValue`/`deleteKey`) are exposed. Ours: window/display handling is `backend/WindowMode` + `backend/ScreenDensity` + `backend/MonitorRefreshRate` (**better**), notifications/tray are `backend/Windows.sendWindowsNotification` and `backend/MetaHorror.hideTaskbar`/`setWallpaper`, and there is no `Registry` or `createProcess`. Only `createProcess` is worth a row: `phoenix://` + `MetaHorror.openFile` already shell out (`xdg-open`/`open`, `backend/MetaHorror.hx:217-219`), so a general `openPath(path)` for scripts covers the use case. **Skip `Registry`** — writing to `HKCU`/`HKLM` from a Lua script is a security problem, not a feature.
- **Checked, already covered — do not port:** their `Discord.hx` custom RPC fork (we have `backend/DiscordClient` + `changePresence`), `TentoolsStuff.hx` (a vendored GameJolt API for **leaderboards/trophies**; §0 already tracks the GameBanana/GameJolt browser idea and `backend/Achievements` + `Highscore` cover the local half — a real `GameJolt` connector belongs in that §0 entry, not here), `CppAPI`/`WindowsData`/`PlatformUtil` (RAM query, dark mode, window alpha, layered-window colour key — superseded by `backend/Native`/`NativeHardware`, `backend/WindowColorMode`, `backend/TransparentWindow`, `docs/TRANSPARENT_WINDOW.md`), `MemoryCounter` (we have `debug/Memory` + `RamGraph` + `MemoryLedger` + `docs/ram-graph.md`), `MP4Handler`/`vlc/` (we use `hxvlc` + `objects/VideoSprite` + `startVideo`, which is cross-platform; theirs needs the bundled `libvlc` DLLs and is Windows-only), `CustomFadeTransition` (we have `shaders/CustomFadeTransition` + `CrossFade` + `FlxTransWindow`), `modPrevention/No.hx` (their "you pirated our mod" screen — an anti-feature we will not ship), `Snd.hx`/`SndTV.hx` (a `faxe` audio wrapper declared `if='switch'` in their `Project.xml:116`, i.e. dead on every target they ship; `FlxSound` + `backend.VolumeCurve` + `PlayStatePlayback` cover it), their patched `flixel/system/FlxSound.hx`, `flixel/animation/FlxAnimationController.hx`, `flixel/system/ui/FlxSoundTray.hx`, `lime/_internal/backend/native/NativeAudioSource.hx` and `flixel/addons/display/FlxRuntimeShader.hx` (all vendored copies of the same 2021 libs our haxelib tracking already patches — see `source/source-haxelib-patches/`; porting the files would fork the audio/animation backends for nothing).

### 36.7. Deliberately excluded — mod content, not engine

Listed so this audit is closed and does not get re-run: every song, week, episode, difficulty name, character, icon, menu character, dialogue set, stage and cutscene (`assets/week*`, their 28k-line `PlayState.hx` stage/cutscene/`case 'WaltStage'` blocks, `BackgroundDancer`/`BackgroundGirls`/`TankmenBG`/`PhillyGlow`, `Philly`/`School`/`SchoolEvil` hooks, `IndieCrossShaderShit`), every per-song event (`Relapse Shoot`, `Speedy`, `Spotlights`, `Lyrics`, `BG Freaks Expression`, `Trigger BG Ghouls`, `Do Health Tween`, `Tween Song Lenght`, `Alter Camera Bouncing`, `Flash Screen`, `Fade Character`, `Screen Fade`, `Cinematic Bars`, `Set Strum Visibility`, `Alter HUD Transparency`, `Scroll Type` with `Undyne`/`Left`/`Right` — the *generic* ones we already have are `Set Property`, `Screen Shake`, `Change Character`, `Play Animation`, `Alt Idle Animation`, `Camera Follow Pos`, `Add Camera Zoom`, `Tween Camera Zoom`, `Set Camera Zoom`, `Camera Bopping`, `Camera Twist`, `Change Scroll Speed`, `Set GF Speed`, `Change Note Multiplier`, `Fake Song Length`, `Change Song Name`, `Popup`, `Credits Popup`, `Windows Notification`, `Hey!`, `Philly Glow`, `Dadbattle Spotlight`, `Nothing`, `Rainbow Eyesore`), their `ModsMenuState`/`GameplayChangersSubstate`/`PauseSubState` song-name `switch` blocks, the `gitarooPause`/meme states, and their `week*.json` / `characters/*.json` / `stages/*.json` assets. If a *generic* shape can be extracted from one of those (e.g. a "Scroll Type" lock event, a lyric/subtitle line — §23.3 already tracks `Add Subtitle`), it goes in the relevant section above with credits, never as a port of the song-specific code.

### 36.8. Suggested order (biggest payoff first)

1. **Script scope flags + note-type scripts in the editor** (§36.5, `[P1]`) — the only item that fixes an existing *bug* (`inChartEditor` is a lie in all three script hosts) while also making note types previewable. Half a day's work in the hosts, the rest in `EditorPlayState`.
2. **Missing generic chart events** (§36.3, `[P1]`) — lyrics, fades, cinematic bars, strum visibility, health tween. Nine handlers plus one shared "undo a mid-song event" path, and the only other item here that adds capability rather than compatibility.
3. **`eventsEnabled` chart-events switch** (§36.3) — one pref and one guard, the fastest "this chart is breaking my device" escape hatch we lack; independent of the events above, so it can land in the same PR.
4. **Judgement counter panel + the shared `marvelouses` presentation pref** (§36.3) — the only visible HUD the player is missing, reuses counters that already exist, `S`.
5. **`openBrowser` / `loadCredits` / `clearUnusedMemory`** (§36.1) — three script globals that unblock normal mod workflow; two are `S`, `loadCredits` wants the small "open a built-in screen" allowlist.
6. **`VHS` effect + the `addEffect1..14` / `die` compat aliases** (§36.2) — the only real shader gap, plus legacy-script compatibility in one pass; the bloom argument-order trap is called out on the entry.
7. **Declarative stage effects** (§36.2) — the biggest engineering item here and the one that moves *past* Funkin.AVI instead of matching it; do it after the VHS/alias work proves the parameter plumbing.
8. **Per-judgement splashes** (§36.3) and **chart meta `composer`/`charter`** (§36.5) — two small data-plumbing jobs that both become trivial once the counter work lands.
9. **System dialogs + native menu bar out of `META_HORROR`** (§36.6) — small, but it is a permission-model change, so pair it with a `ModSecurity` review.
10. Then the rest: real autosave restore (§36.5), freeplay categories and per-song unlocks (§36.4), and whatever is still left in §36.3.

Effort is quoted per entry (XS–M); **nothing in this section needs new art, new audio or a new dependency**, which is why it is a port list and not a rewrite. Both sides are Apache-2.0, so every entry is license-clean; each one lands with a credits line to Funkin.AVI (`ThatOneSillyGuyDon` and the names in the mod's own `CreditsState`) and, for a shader, to that shader's original author as noted in their header. Per the rules at the top of this file every port ships **enabled by default with a toggle** — where an entry above names a default it is because their pref has one worth matching, and where it does not, the default is still on and the toggle is part of the work, not an optional extra. Findings that turned out to be their dead code (`restart`, the "Live Difficulty" row, `installMod`, `Tray`/`Registry`, the `Effect` reflection shim) are recorded as **not a port** rather than silently dropped, so the next audit does not re-derive them.

## 37. KATHY ENGINE PARITY — WHAT KATHY HAS THAT PHOENIX DOES NOT (2026-11-21)

**Source:** [kittycathy332/FNF-KathyEngine](https://github.com/kittycathy332/FNF-KathyEngine) at `d9dbbc9` (the commit the request links to; branch cloned 2026-11-21). License: **Apache 2.0**, same as ours, so everything below is legal to port **with credit to kittycathy332 / the Kathy team and the upstreams it credits** (NovaFlare, Slushi/OS Engine, Psych Engine Mobile team, Leather Engine for the LE shims, dopadream for EK+, HaxeFlixel for `HitGraph`).

> **Cross-reference, read this first.** Five other fork reviews landed on `main` while this one was open (§28 FunkinAVI V2, §29 Yarn, §30 Doors, §31 SeiunEngine, §32 Super Plus), and **§31/§32 already carry a lot of the same gaps in more detail** — they audited forks that overlap Kathy's "Psych 1.0 mobile kitchen sink" territory. Where that happens, the entry below is **kept to a one-liner that points at the fuller entry** instead of re-specifying the same feature: mobile/touch bindings → §32.1.1; reflection API → §32.1 (and §27.1.2); `getVar`/`setVar` → §32.1; window geometry/multi-window → §32.1.1; i18n → §32.7; ModSettings → §31.1 (also §27.1.1); V-Slice import → §31.4; replays → §31.3 and §14; hold-cover art → §32.9; key viewer → §32.6. What is **unique to Kathy** and specified in full here: Hold Cover (the feature, not the art), `HitGraph`, the HUD widget pack, splash overflow policy, the letter-rank results extras, the three sound trays, the 45-field FPS counter, the FNF/legacy splash modes, and Kathy's own regressions in §37.9.

**Verdict, one paragraph.** Kathy is a Psych 1.0-era mobile fork with a very different centre of gravity from ours: it is *much* smaller than Phoenix (325 vs 563 `.hx`) yet ships whole subsystems we have none of. The four that matter are **Hold Cover** (a visible gameplay-render feature, absent here entirely), **`HitGraph` + a HUD widget pack** (KeyViewer/RatingCounter/NoteTimer/in-song MusicPlayer), **the splash overflow policy and the three selectable sound trays** (small, but the kind of thing players notice daily), and **letter ranks + `Sick+`/`perfect` tier insertion on the results screen**. Everything else Kathy has either landed on `main` in the meantime (Stage Editor, i18n, replays, reflection API, mobile bindings — now spec'd in §31/§32 and cross-referenced here rather than re-specified) or is not worth porting (§37.9). Roughly 60% of the first draft of this section turned out to be duplicate work once `main` caught up; what survives is the list above plus a handful of cheap QoL APIs (`getFPS`/`getFrameCount`, note inspection, the explicit legacy-class alias table) and two real bugs (`getPropertyAdvanced` still commented out; `LuaUtils.resolveClassCompat` guessing packages by prefix).

**Method.** Both trees were cloned and read side by side; nothing was built or run. The comparison is mechanical first, then read:

- file inventory (both trees at the time of writing; the Phoenix side moved a little when #358 landed mid-audit — 548 → **563** `.hx`): Kathy ships **325** `.hx` files, Phoenix **548**; **262** Kathy files have no path-identical counterpart here and **485** Phoenix files have none there (reorganisation noise — Kathy moved `Note`/`PlayState`/`Paths` into `objects.`/`states.`/`backend.` too, so a path diff alone proves nothing).
- Lua API surface: every `add_callback(lua, "…")` / `Convert.addCallback(...)` / `registerFunction("…")` name was extracted from both trees. Kathy registers **381** distinct names, Phoenix **380** at the time (re-measured on `main` after #358: **390**). **166** of Kathy's names still have no same-named counterpart here, and after re-checking every one against the current tree **all 166 appear nowhere at all** as quoted strings — the earlier "14 are renames" figure was wrong and has been dropped. Those 166 are the real API gaps, and every one of them is sorted into a family in §37.1.
- script callbacks: grepping quoted `'on…'`/`"on…"` names gives **52** hooks in Kathy vs **44** here (41 was the count before #358); **16** are Kathy-only and §37.7 lists them. Phoenix is ahead on 8 of its own (`onClose`, `onStart`, `onStop`, `onRestart`, `onAndroidEvent`, `onModchartLabSection`, the two resume-countdown hooks), so diff both ways before adding.
- options: `ClientPrefs` — Kathy's `@:structInit SaveVariables` carries **307** fields, Phoenix's flat class **215** at the time of the audit (**218** after #358); **270** Kathy field names have no same-named counterpart here. That number is inflated: a large share are pure renames (§37.9 lists the confirmed ones), and a second share is Kathy's ~45-field FPS-counter family. The real gaps are the ones named individually below.
- every claim below was then re-checked by grep against this tree at `443ae01`; where a feature turned out to be already present, it is in §37.9 instead of being repeated.

Priority tags follow the file's convention (`P0` blocker … `P3` nice-to-have), and every entry obeys the house rules at the top: **on by default where it is a feature, always with a toggle**, **credits shipped with the port**, and **no Psych/JS compatibility regressions** (§12.8). **No `P0` is assigned anywhere in this section on purpose:** a `P0` here means "the build or a shipped feature is broken", and nothing Kathy has falls in that class — Phoenix builds and ships without any of it. `P1` therefore reads as "high modder payoff / closes a visible feature gap", not "blocker". Distribution: 25 `P1`, 24 `P2`, 10 `P3`, plus the 8 bullets in §37.9 that are explicitly *not* to be done. The count went down from 26/26/12 in the first draft of this section because five entries were merged into the fuller specs that landed in §31/§32 while this PR was open (see the cross-reference note above) — they are still tracked, just once each.

### 37.1. Scripting APIs Kathy has and Phoenix does not

All of these are *new global functions* for Lua (and the same set should be added to Python `pystdlib/` and HScript `interp.variables` in the same PR, per the "same registration object for all three languages" pattern Phoenix already uses for filters, chainable effects and MetaHorror).

- **[P1] The whole mobile/touch script API (`mobile/psychlua/Functions.hx`, 73 functions) — **spec'd in §32.1.1, do not duplicate the design.**** The finding worth recording here is the size: Kathy alone registers **73** functions in five families (device probes, pad control, touch probes with `*Object`/`*ObjectComplex` overlap variants, extra buttons, shell — `toast`, clipboard, orientation, keep-screen-on), and `grep -rn '"isMobile"\|"addVirtualPad"\|"toast"' source/` is still **0** across Lua, Python and HScript, so §32.1.1's `MobileCallbacks.hx` should be sized for this list, not the 48 SPE registers. Much of the capability already exists in `android/platform/*` and `mobile/*` — exposure, not new engine work.
- **[P1] `getVar` / `setVar` — script-local variables — **spec'd in §32.1** (script-local variable store).** Kathy's variant is worth noting for one difference: it stores into `PlayState.variables`, the same map we already use for sprites and tags (`psychlua/CustomSubstate.hx:84`), so a Kathy script can round-trip a value through `getVar` and also see it as an object tag. §32.1 proposes a per-script `Map` instead, which is the safer design — but the tag-map behaviour is what existing Kathy mods expect, so decide it deliberately rather than by accident. Effort: S.
- **[P1] Reflection API — **spec'd in §32.1**, with the sandboxed-allowlist design already worked out there.** Kathy's `psychlua/ReflectionFunctions.hx` is 329 lines and adds one thing §32.1 does not mention: a `"##PSYCHLUA_STRINGTOOBJ"` sentinel that lets Lua hold a real Haxe reference across a `setProperty` round-trip, so `addInstance` results can be passed back as arguments. Take that sentinel with the rest. Phoenix has 11 functions in `PropertyCallbacks.hx` and no `callMethod`/`createInstance`/`instanceArg` at all — two independent forks (Kathy, SPE) now ship the same gap, which settles that it is real.
- **[P1] Achievement-scoring functions in Python and HScript.** Lua already has all six (`Achievements.addLuaCallbacks`, `backend/Achievements.hx:277`); `addPythonCallbacks` (`:329`) registers the same set — verified with `grep -oE 'Convert.addCallback\(lua, "…"'` → `getAchievementScore setAchievementScore addAchievementScore unlockAchievement isAchievementUnlocked achievementExists`, and the HScript preset only exposes the `Achievements` **class** (`HScript.hx:604`), not the flat functions. So the gap is **parity, not the API**: a Python/HScript script doing `addAchievementScore('kills', 1)` has to reach through `Achievements.addScore` by hand. Effort: S.
- **[P1] Note inspection API: `getNoteCount`, `getTotalNoteCount`, `getNoteProperty`, `setNoteProperty`, `getNoteData`, `getNoteRating`, `getAliveNotes`, `destroyNote`, `isPlayerNote`.** Kathy's `psychlua/NoteFunctions.hx` (141 lines) is a whole module Phoenix has no equivalent for; here `grep -rn '"getNoteData"' source/` is empty. `getAliveNotes` returns the live `unspawnNotes` subset (useful for "delete the next 4 notes" gimmicks), `destroyNote` removes one by reference, `getNoteRating` reads the judgement a note produced. Effort: M (needs a stable note handle; do **not** hand Lua raw `Note` objects — use an integer id + lookup so a script cannot leak a destroyed sprite, the way `HitSoundPool`/`onSpawnNote` already work).
- **[P1] `getPlayingCharacterAnim(character)` and `characterExists(name)`.** The first is a one-liner over `Character.animation.curAnim.name` for `dad`/`gf`/`bf`; the second checks `characters/<name>.xml|.txt` through `Paths`. Both are cheap modder QoL functions used constantly in Kathy scripts (`ExtraFunctions.hx:430-465`). Effort: S.
- **[P1] `getFPS()` and `getFrameCount()`.** Phoenix has `FlxG.drawFramerate` and `debug.FrameProfiler`, but no script-visible FPS or frame counter; Kathy's `ExtraFunctions.frameCount` is bumped from `PlayState.update` (`ExtraFunctions.hx:20`). Needed for the modchart "animate at N fps" idiom and for adaptive-performance scripts. Effort: S.
- **[P1] String/colour/math helper set: `getColorFromString`, `getColorFromName`, `hueShift`, `lerp`, `clamp`, `distance`, `angleBetween`, `makeFlxPoint` — **half of it is in §32.13's free-win list already** (`getColorFromString`/`getColorFromName`).** The part nobody has spec'd is the geometry half (`hueShift`, `lerp`, `clamp`, `distance`, `angleBetween`, `makeFlxPoint`, 40 dependency-free lines in Kathy's `ExtraFunctions.hx:280-380`); Phoenix only ships `getColorFromHex` (`GameCallbacks.hx:116`). Fold them into the same PR. Effort: S.
- **[P1] Sound pitch: `getSoundPitch` / `setSoundPitch(tag, value, ?doPause)` — **already in §32.1 and §32.13's free wins.** One detail to carry over: gate on `FLX_PITCH` per target — Kathy's `#else` branch traces "not supported on this platform", which is why its mods behave differently on HTML5 than on native. Effort: S.
- **[P1] Window geometry (`getWindowX/Y`, `getWindowWidth/Height`, `setWindowPosition`, `setWindowTitle`) — **spec'd in §32.1.1** alongside the much larger `winTween*` set.** Kathy's version is the small subset (eight calls) and it is worth noting that Phoenix already exposes `screenWidth`/`screenHeight` as script *globals* only (`FunkinLua.hx:230`) — so this is callable-vs-global, not new capability. Do not re-add `setWindowTransparent`/`setWindowAlpha`/click-through, which we already ship (`WindowCallbacks.hx`).
- **[P2] `updateScoreText()` — **already in §32.1 and §32.13.** Only worth a line here because the cause is different: our `setScore` calls the refresh internally, so scripts that write `songScore` **as a field** see stale text while `setScore('…')` users do not. Same fix either way. Effort: S.
- **[P2] `loadMultipleFrames(tag, imagesArray)`.** Loads an array of image paths into one animated sprite. Phoenix has `loadFrames` and `loadGraphic` (`PropertyCallbacks.hx`) but not the array form. Effort: S.
- **[P2] `isRunning(scriptFile)`, `getLuaCompatVersion()`, `getFileTranslation()`/`getTranslationPhrase()`.** These four are **not** in §31/§32: Kathy can ask whether a named script is currently running (`ScriptCallbacks.hx` has `getRunningScripts` but no name test), can report its own Lua-compat version so a mod can branch on engine age, and exposes the two i18n getters to Lua. The last two ship with §32.7's `Language`, not before. Effort: S.
- **[P2] `eraseSaveData(name)`.** `SaveDataCallbacks.hx` has `initSaveData`/`flushSaveData`/`get-`/`setDataFromSave` but no erase; Kathy's `FlxSave.erase()` wrapper is four lines. Effort: S.
- **[P2] Explicit legacy-class alias table — **same feature as §32.8's `backend/ClassResolver.hx` entry; build one table, not two.**** Our side of the problem is worth stating precisely because it is worse than it looks: `LuaUtils.resolveClassCompat` (`psychlua/LuaUtils.hx:20-34`) tries `Type.resolveClass(name)` then prepends each of **16** package prefixes and returns the first hit. That is slower and, for a short name that lives in two packages, can resolve the *wrong* class — Kathy's `psychlua/LegacyClassAlias.hx` (168 lines) avoids it with an explicit `Map` that also covers non-moves (`'Boyfriend' => 'objects.Character'`). Requirement for whoever takes §32.8: add a `tests/haxe/` spec asserting every short name a 0.6.3 script can use resolves to the intended class, and keep prefix-guessing only as the fallback for unlisted names.
- **[P3] `getPropertyAdvanced` is commented out.** It sits disabled next to the working `setPropertyAdvanced` (`psychlua/callbacks/TweenCallbacks.hx:335-350` vs `:351`), so a script can set a deep property path but not read one — an asymmetry Kathy's `ReflectionFunctions.hx` does not have. Re-enable or delete the dead block. Effort: S.
- **[P3] `launchExternalExe(path, ?args, ?waitForExit, ?windowMode, ?activateMainWindow)`.** Windows-only, `backend.Native` already has a `Process` path (`FFmpegLocator`/`PlayStateRender` spawn `ffmpeg`), but exposing arbitrary process launching to mods is a **security decision, not a feature**: if ported, it must be behind `ModSecurity` + an explicit opt-in pref (`allowModExternalExe`, default **off**), never a silent global. Effort: S to write, M to review. Related §37.4 (`requestAdminPrivilege`).

### 37.2. Localisation — **spec'd in §32.7; only the Kathy-specific data is recorded here**

`main` landed §32.7 (`atox07` Super Plus) with the full design: `backend.Language` + `getPhrase` + a picker + twelve locale files. §31 also has a P2 `language` entry. **Do not write a third spec.** What Kathy adds to that picture, and what a porter should take from it:

- **Scale, not just presence.** Kathy's `assets/languages/en_us.json` has **577** keys plus **859** option keys in a separate `options/en_us.json`, and editor string packs per editor under `assets/languages/editors/{stage,character,week,dialogue,dialogue_character,hold_cover,notesplash,menu_character}_editor/`. That is roughly 1,436 engine strings + 8 editor packs — a realistic size estimate for §32.7's "b)" pass, which SPE's 476-key `EnUS` understates by ~3×.
- **Six per-area sub-tables + live reload.** `backend.Language.OptionsLanguage` etc., with `reload()` firing `addCallback`/`removeCallback` listeners so an **already-open** options menu re-labels itself when the language changes. That live-reload behaviour is the piece §32.7 does not mention and it is what makes the picker feel finished; add the listener pair to `Language` from day one.
- **`LanguageBasic` as the Psych-compat side.** A `.lang` flat `key: "value"` loader for mod-supplied text, kept *alongside* the JSON engine packs so existing Psych mods keep working. §32.7's "merged-mod-override path" is the same idea; Kathy's split (engine = JSON, mods = `.lang`) is the version that does not break Psych.
- **`Option.valueLocalizations`.** Enum-valued dropdowns are translated too (`'BotPlay' => Language.get('render_input_botplay')`) rather than only the row label — easy to forget, and it is visible in every translated options screen.
- **Per-language fonts.** Kathy swaps fonts on load (`uitab_font`, `charting_font_size`, `checkbox_font_size`, and a CJK-coverage pixel font) so translated labels do not tofu. §32.7 does not say what happens to the font; it needs an answer before the CJK locale files land.

Verified on the rebased tree, so nobody re-derives it: `grep -rn "Language.get" source/` = 0, and the only hit for "language" in `source/` is `modchart/lab/LabLanguage.hx`, which is a two-value enum (`Lua; Python;`) naming the *scripting language* the Modchart Lab exports to — not a translation system.

### 37.3. Spine skeletal-animation runtime (large, load-bearing in Kathy)

Kathy bundles `spine-haxe` + `source/spine/flixel/{SkeletonSprite,NoCullSkeletonSprite}.hx` + `source/spineflixel/SpineViewerTextureLoader.hx`, and builds **two editors and a story mode on top of it**:

- `states/editors/SpineViewerState.hx` (2006 lines) — load a Spine skeleton, browse animations/skins, scrub a timeline, export.
- `states/StoryPlayerState.hx` + `states/StorySpineTools.hx` — a Blue-Archive-style ADV story player driven by Spine characters, with its own gradient UI, typewriter and per-line camera moves.
- **35** Lua functions: `makeSpineSprite`, `addSpineSprite`, `removeSpineSprite`, `spineSetPosition/Scale/Angle/Alpha/Color/Flip/Visible/Layer/Size`, `spineSetAnimation`, `spineAddAnimation`, `spineSetAnimationSpeed`, `spineSetMix`, `spineSetBonePosition`, `spineGetBonePosition`, `spineGetAttachmentPosition`, `spineSetBoundingBox`, `spineComputeFullBounds`, `spineSetAntialiasing`, `spineSendToBack`/`spineBringToFront`/`spineSetLayer`/`spineGetLayer`/`spineMoveLayer`/`spineSwapLayers`, `spineClearTrack(s)`, `spineGetAnimations`, `spineGetInfo`, `spineSetCamera`, `spineScreenCenter`, `spineSpriteExists` — **zero** of which exist here (`grep -ril spine source/` = 0).

Phoenix already ships **FlxAnimate** (`source/flxanimate/PsychFlxAnimate.hx`, `loadAnimateAtlas`, `makeFlxAnimateSprite`, `addAnimationBySymbol`) which is the free-format alternative; Spine is a commercial-format alternative that a lot of modern FNF mods use.

- **[P2] Spine runtime + Lua surface.** Add `spine-haxe` and a `backend.SpineUtils` wrapper mirroring the 35-function set, in Lua **and** Python/HScript. Keep FlxAnimate untouched; the two coexist. Gate the whole feature behind a compile flag (`SPINE_ALLOWED`) like `VIDEOS_ALLOWED`, because it is a build-size hit (`SIZE.md` is explicit that binary size is a Phoenix constraint). Effort: L, mostly dependency wiring + re-implementing the API against Spine-Haxe's API rather than Kathy's exact wrappers.
- **[P3] Spine viewer editor.** Rides on the above and can reuse `editors/StageEditorState`'s pan/zoom/undo/property-panel plumbing, which has landed (§37.5); do not build a second editor frame for it.
- **[P3] Advanced-dialogue story player.** A separate product decision (the results-screen extras in §37.7 and the dialogue-editor parity in §37.5 are the more pressing gaps). If wanted, port as a *new state*, not a replacement for `DialogueBoxPsych` — Psych dialogue must keep working.

### 37.4. Native/window surface Kathy has and Phoenix does not

Phoenix has a *deep* window layer already (`backend/WindowMode` — Windowed/Borderless/Fullscreen, `TransparentWindow`, `FlxTransWindow`, `GDIEffects`, `NativeHardware`, `MetaHorror`'s 43 window functions, `deeplink/`). The genuine gaps:

- **[P1] Extra native windows — **same feature as §32.1.1's multi-window entry (`createWindow`/`closeWindow`/`addSpriteToWindow`/`setWindowView`); build one Win32 layer, not two.**** What Kathy contributes to that spec: a **close-request callback** (`setCloseCallback`/`isClosingRequested`, so a script can veto or animate a close), a **timed main-window fade** (`fadeOutWindow(ms)`), and window enumeration (`getWindowCount`, `getAllWindowIds`, `isWindowActive`, `getWindowPosition`) which SPE's list does not have. Our side is still bare: `source/backend/Native.hx` is 14 system-info functions and `grep -n "createWindow\|fadeOutWindow" source/backend/Native.hx` = 0. Effort: L, and §32.1.1's warning applies — port the capability, never Kathy's inline-C++ file (§37.9).
- **[P2] Window opacity fade / drag-to-move helpers.** Phoenix has `setWindowAlpha` via `TransparentWindow`; it lacks `startWindowDrag` (drag from an arbitrary in-game region) and the timed alpha fade. Useful for borderless pseudo-titlebars (§37.6) and for overlay mods. Effort: S.
- **[P2] Admin/privilege handling.** `isAdmin()` + `requestAdminPrivilege()` (a `ShellExecute runas` relaunch). Not in any of §28–32, and it is the reason a mod cannot write into a `Program Files` install.
