If ported it must be **explicitly opt-in** (default-off pref + a prompt), and note Kathy's own default is off too. Effort: S, but the review cost is the real cost — arbitrary elevation from a mod is a trust decision.
- **[P3] Platform JNI helper classes — mostly NOT a gap, one real one.** Kathy's `android/{Tools,Permissions,Settings,callback,widget,os,content}` + `mobile/backend/*` bundle is **smaller** than our `source/android/platform/` (`AndroidIntents`, `AndroidNotification`, `AndroidSystem` with `getBatteryInfo`/`acquireWakeLock`/`isAndroidTV`/`hasTouchscreen`/`moveTaskToBack`, plus Haptics/Media/Lifecycle/StdFire). Do **not** port that tree. The single real gap: **runtime permission requests from a script** — we have `AndroidPermissionPolicy`/`AndroidPermissionPolicySpec` for *policy* but no request call, and Kathy can ask for a permission and read the result. Toasts are already covered by §32.1.1's binding layer (`AndroidStdFire.showToast` exists and is just unreachable). Effort: S.

### 37.5. Editors and menus Kathy has and Phoenix does not

Phoenix's editor set is `CharacterEditorState`, `ChartingState` (2417 lines), `DialogueCharacterEditorState`, `DialogueEditorState`, `ModchartLabState`, `NoteSplashDebugState`, `WeekEditorState`, `MasterEditorMenu`. Kathy adds **four editors** (Stage, Spine viewer, Hold Cover, Note Splash) plus a version router and a Freeplay category editor:

- **[P1] Stage Editor — *now landed, so this is a scope diff, not a port*.** A stage editor **shipped on `main` while this audit was open** (`editors/StageEditorState.hx` 1674 lines + `data/StageEditorCore.hx` 886 + `data/StageUndoStack.hx`, `PlayStateStageObjects` spawning the JSON; §12.3 is marked DONE, docs in `docs/STAGE_EDITOR.md`). What Kathy's 3087-line `StageEditorState` still has beyond it: `_editorMeta` per-stage metadata block, `PreloadListSubState` (preload a chosen asset list before entering), and its live character/pose preview. Compare the two before writing any code — the honest entry is "**diff the editors**", not "build a stage editor". Do **not** re-implement what shipped.
- **[P1] Charting router (pick which editor generation opens) — *the legacy editors themselves are a non-port* (§37.9); only the router is new.** `ChartingRouter.hx` lets the player choose `1.0.4-Kathy`, `1.0.4-Official`, `0.7.3` or `0.6.3` at the editor door, because old charts and old habits do not survive a new editor. §31.4 and §32.2 both ask for **importers** (V-Slice, CNE, osu!, Psych 1.0 layout) but neither asks for *keeping the old editor around*, and that is the one thing Kathy users actually rely on. Cheap version: keep today's `ChartingState` reachable behind a pref (a related toggle already appears in §31.2) and open imports in it. Effort: S once a second editor exists, pointless before that.
- **[P1] V-Slice chart import/export — spec'd twice already: §31.4 (`CneExport`/`VSlice`) and §32.10 (psych_v2 + `VSlice` import). Build one importer.** Kathy's contribution is the reference implementation plus two facts worth carrying over: its `states/editors/content/VSlice.hx` converts **both ways** (Psych to V-Slice too, via `PsychJsonPrinter` 220), and it is 490 lines, which is a realistic size for the job and beats the M-to-L guess in §32.10. Its `VSliceFreeplayState` is **not** worth taking (§32.12 agrees).
- **[P1] Dialogue editors: the four behaviours Kathy has.** Both editors exist here and are *larger* than Kathy's (`DialogueCharacterEditorState` 1009 lines vs 759; `DialogueEditorState` 549 vs 546), so this is a behaviour diff, not a file diff: Kathy's add **undo/redo with unsaved-change prompts, folder browsing for portraits, translation keys, and a live preview pane**. Port those four onto the existing editors. Effort: M.
- **[P2] Hold Cover editor and Note Splash editor.** Kathy has `states/editors/HoldCoverEditorState.hx` (659) and `NoteSplashEditorState.hx` (1029); we have `NoteSplashDebugState.hx` (615, debug-only) and no hold-cover editor at all (`grep -ril HoldCover source/` = 0). **Sequencing matters:** the Hold Cover *feature* is §37.6's P1 entry and has to exist first, because an editor for something we do not render is a trap. Once it does, Kathy's 659-line editor is the reference. For the note-splash half, §32.2 (editors) and §32.9 (art) already cover the gap; do not re-spec it.
- **[P2] Week Editor parity.** Present here (633 lines vs Kathy's 796), and **do not believe a schema diff**: Phoenix already reads and applies per-week `difficulties` (`states/StoryMenuState.hx:484-508` splits the comma list into `CoolUtil.difficulties`, plus auto-appends `jshard`/`Noob`/`Expert`/`Insane` when charts exist), `WeekData.difficulties` exists (`WeekData.hx:44`) and `WeekEditorState` writes it (`:251`). The only real gaps are the `hideStoryMode`/`hideFreeplay` toggles (the base-game flags exist in `WeekData`, but the editor is where they are missing) and, separately, difficulty state living in three places (`CoolUtil.difficulties`, `WeekData.difficulties`, `CoolUtil.currentDifficulty`) — see the `backend.Difficulty` entry below.
- **[P2] Freeplay categories / tabs with an in-engine editor — partly covered by §31.2's data-driven freeplay cards; the tab bar itself has no other entry.** Kathy's build: `backend/FreeplayCategory.hx`, `backend/ui/FreeplayTabBar.hx` (676 lines, hand-drawn skewed tabs), `FreeplayCategoryEditor.hx`, `FreeplaySkewButton.hx`, reading `mods/freeplay_categories.json` plus per-mod `freeplay_categories.json` with folder/week/song/exclude rules and per-category colours. Ours (`FreeplayFilter`/`FreeplayFilterStore` + `FreeplayFilterSubState`) does search/favourites/sort and **no** categories (`grep -ril freeplayCategor source/` = 0). Reuse the existing filter page rather than shipping a second one; adopt Kathy's `docs/freeplay_categories_template.json` as the format. Effort: L.
- **[P2] Main Editor menu restructure.** `MasterEditorMenu.hx` here is 228 lines vs Kathy's 186 and only lists the editors that exist — the Stage Editor has since landed and should already be in it (verify first); once the Hold Cover editor lands this needs a grouped layout with tabs. Effort: S after the editors exist.
- **[P3] `FirstLaunchState` / `ModsImport` / `OptionsIOState` — three user-facing UIs we have backends for but no screen for.** Kathy's first-run wizard (636 lines), zip mod importer (434) and settings import/export UI (703) are front-ends over things we already own: `backend/SettingsBackup` + `SettingsBackupCodec`, `mobile/files/ModZipInstaller`, and the zip flow `states/ModsMenuState.hx` does inline. Related but not the same: §31.3's `BootState` (one animated boot screen) and §13's save-bundle export. The settings import/export UI is the one with no other entry anywhere in the file — build that on `SettingsBackup` first. Effort: M total, three small PRs.
- **[P3] `options/KeybindManager` — §31.3 already specs gamepad rebinding + persistence; do that first.** Kathy's version is a *matrix* screen (per-device columns, conflict detection, reset-per-device) rather than a list. That is a layout decision on the same data, not a separate feature.
- **[P3] Command-line launch state.** `states/CommandLineLaunchState.hx` parses args into "open this chart / play this song / import this zip". Phoenix's `LaunchTarget`/`LaunchState`/`deeplink` cover the URI half; CLI args are unhandled.

### 37.6. Gameplay, HUD and game-content gaps

- **[P1] Hold Cover (sustain masks) — the single biggest feature in this section, and the prerequisite for three entries in §34.** First, the correction that matters: **we do not have it.** §34.1 asserts "Phoenix ships hold covers but does not let scripts mutate them", and that is wrong — `grep -ril "holdCover" source/` = 0, and what we ship per side is `PlayState.opponentStrums`-driven `holdSplash*` **splashes**, a different thing. Three separate forks (Kathy, Cheese §34, SPE §32.9) ship hold-cover art or APIs, so it is worth fixing the record here. Kathy renders a scrolling cover sprite over each sustain (`objects/NoteHoldCover.hx`) with per-skin art (`holdCoverSkin`), alpha, an opponent toggle and per-side colouring — add `objects/HoldCover` + `ClientPrefs.holdCovers`/`holdCoverSkin`/`opponentHoldCovers`, spawn/despawn from `PlayStateNotes`, **on by default** with art shipped, toggle in Options -> Visuals, and each colour configurable per the house rule. Once it exists, §34.1 (script API), §34.4 (editor) and the four skin-swap chart events in §34.3 become buildable.
- **[P1] HUD widget pack: `RatingCounter`, `NoteTimer`, in-song `MusicPlayer` — `KeyViewer` is already §32.6, keep them in one menu.** §32.6 specs `showKeyViewer`/`keyViewerColor`/`keyViewerOffset` + a 691-line `objects/KeyViewer.hx` (pressure bars, KPS, live bind labels) — do **not** write a second key viewer. The three that exist nowhere else in the file: **`RatingCounter`** (`ratCounter` + animation, a running judgement tally counter), **`NoteTimer`** (`noteTimer` + display/layer/stepped, a per-note ms readout), and the **in-song `MusicPlayer`** (a track browser that plays references over gameplay — note §32.12 dismissed SPE's `objects/MusicPlayer`, but that is Freeplay's *preview* player, a different feature; Kathy's plays inside `PlayState`). Each is one object + one pref family; build them as the "HUD widget pack" menu in §37.8 rather than seven separate menus. Effort: S each.
- **[P1] Judge windows / rating pipeline variants — overlaps §32.3's `useFlawlessRating` entry; build on that shape.** Kathy's `backend/Rating.hx` makes ratings **data** (`name`, `image`, `hitWindow`, `ratingMod`, `score`, `noteSplash`, `hits`) via `loadDefault()`, plus a `perfect` tier gated on `rmPerfect`, an optional `sickPlus` tier, and `fallbackPerfectToSick`/`fallbackEXPerfectToSick`. Phoenix (`play/helpers/PlayStateRating.hx` + `AccuracyPresets`) has presets and a 5-tier ladder but no tier *insertion*, no `rmPerfect`, and no fallbacks. §32.3 wants the same `Rating.hx` shape for Flawless; **one implementation**, with `perfect`/`sickPlus` as data rows rather than hardcoded tiers. Effort: M.
- **[P1] Hitsound object pool.** `backend/HitSoundPool.hx` recycles a fixed ring of `FlxSound`s (`hitSoundPoolEnabled`, `hitSoundPoolSize`, default size 50) so a 30-NPS chart does not allocate 30 sources/s. Phoenix allocates per hit and `grep -ril hitSoundPool source/` = 0. Directly on the engine's low-end identity (§8/§18). Effort: S — probably the cheapest win in this whole section.
- **[P1] "Leather"-style rating fall animation set.** `ratingFallStyle` (`Leather`/`Legacy`/`Kathy`/`Kathy(Legacy)`/`Camellia`), `ratbounce`, `exratbounce`, `camelliaScaleMode`, `biggerInfoText` — a whole family of popup animation modes with dependent-option visibility (`HudSettingsSubState.updateBounceOptionsVisibility`). Phoenix has `ratingPopups`/`comboPopups`/`scoreStyle` but a **single** popup behaviour (`grep -ril ratingFallStyle source/` = 0). Port as one `ratingFallStyle` string option whose default reproduces today's look, plus the dependent toggles with proper `disabled`/`requirement` handling (Kathy's `HudSettingsSubState` does this with `onOptionsBuilt` + a per-frame `update()` visibility refresh — copy the pattern, not the per-frame cost; `BaseOptionsMenu` here can do it on change only).
- **[P1] Timing-accuracy styles: `msTimingStyle`, `msTimingOffsetMode`, `showModeLabelInMsTxt`, `softJudgmentEdge`, `preciseHit`, `accuracyMode`, `sustainAccuracy`, `sustainTailFix`.** Phoenix shows the ms offset and has Early & Late (`docs/EARLY_LATE.md`) but not these presentation/judgement modifiers (`grep -ril preciseHit\|sustainAccuracy source/` = 0). Each is a small, independent `Option` + a branch in `PlayStateRating`. Effort: S each.
- **[P2] Lane covers.** `laneCoverAlphaP1/P2` + `laneCoverByStrumAlpha` — a dark strip over each side's lanes, alpha per player. Phoenix has the **lane underlay** (`docs/LANE_UNDERLAY.md`, `LaneUnderlayMath`) which covers the "something behind the lanes" need but not the "hide the approaching notes on the other side" need. Distinct feature; add as a second pref or document that underlay is not the same thing.
- **[P2] Hold-tail leniency / instant-miss options — `sustainsAsOneNote`/`guitarHeroSustains` is already §31.2, do not add it twice.** The genuinely unlisted ones: `holdTailLeniency` + `holdTailLeniencyMs`, `holdReleaseInstantMiss`, `holdTailJudge`, `holdScoreBonus`, `instantResolveExpired`, `singleHoldNoteAnimation`, `forceHoldAnimations`, `kadeHoldVisual`, `kadeScoring`, `healthOverflow`/`healthOverflowDrain`, `breakComboOnBad`/`breakComboOnShit`, `botplayPerfectTiming`/`botplayStyle`, `holdNoteBehind`, `autoResync`, `syncSpeedMultiplier`, `fixedTimestep` — **18** toggles with no Phoenix field (`grep -ril "holdTailLeniency\|kadeScoring\|healthOverflow\|breakComboOnBad" source/` = 0), all small branches in `PlayStateNotes`/`PlayStateRating`. Kathy's `JudgmentSettingsSubState` (400 lines, 30 rows) is the right unit: one "Judgment & Holds" options category. Effort: M for the set.
- **[P2] `objects/Bar` + `HealthIcon` variants — `healthbarstyle` is §32.9 and `iconsOnHB` is §31.2; the rest is unlisted.** Already here and **not** a gap: `healthBarStyle` (Leather/Impostor/Dave/Kade/TGT/DD+/JS/Vanilla presets, `options/VisualsUISubState.hx:307`), `iconStyle`, `iconBounceType`, `comboStacking`, `scoreStyle`, `healthBarAlpha`, `uiScalePercent`. Genuinely missing: `dynamicHealthIcons` (PNG+XML atlas icons with a `default` frame prefix), `threeIcons` (win/lose/normal strip), `dynamicColors` (per-character colour extraction), `comboNumDisplay` (Psych/Default/OG-Funkin number sets), `beatScale`, `biggerInfoText`, `iconbopNormalize`. Effort: S each.
- **[P2] Results/loading presentation toggles: `showResultScreen`, `replayTextStyle`, `loadingScreen`/`loadingStyle`, `legacyMainMenu`, `legacyHUD`, `showcaseStyle`.** `showResultScreen` is the one with a behaviour behind it: Phoenix **always** switches to `ResultsState` at the end of a song (`play/helpers/PlayStatePlayback.hx:570`, and `:530` for a campaign) and Kathy lets a mod or the player skip straight back to Freeplay. The rest are skins. `splashMode` is a related startup choice (§37.9 has the Phoenix equivalent).
- **[P2] Song/difficulty plumbing: a real `backend.Difficulty` module.** Kathy has `backend/Difficulty.hx` owning `list`, `defaultList`, `getFilePath`, `loadFromWeek`, `resetList`, `copyFrom`, `getString`; Phoenix spreads the same state across `CoolUtil.difficulties` / `CoolUtil.defaultDifficulties(Full)` / `CoolUtil.currentDifficulty`, `WeekData.difficulties` and `StoryMenuState`'s inline comma-splitting (`:484-537`). Consolidating removes a whole class of "which array is the truth" bugs and is a prerequisite for the Week Editor work above. `SongMetadata.hx` and `states/StoryData`/`StoryPlayerState` ride on §37.3's story player, so they are not separate entries. Effort: M.
- **[P3] Splash limits and single-anim modes — `splashAlpha` itself is already §30/§31.2, this is the *policy* around it.** Phoenix has `maxSplashLimit` and `splashType` (the skin); missing is the **overflow policy** (`splashLimitMode`: drop the new splash vs recycle the oldest — a real gameplay-feel difference on dense charts) plus `forceSingleSplashAnim`/`singleNoteSplashAnim` (one animation for every lane). Effort: S.

### 37.7. Results, replays and session tooling

- **[P1] Rich results screen extras: letter rank, per-judgement colouring, `accuracyMode`, F9 replay save.** Phoenix's `states/ResultsState.hx` is a clean modern rewrite (metric cards + judgement panel + hit histogram), so the *screen* is not a gap — §21.1 is DONE and satisfies the one-line improvement Kathy has. What is missing from it: a letter rank (`Ratings.GenerateLetterRank(accuracy)` → a `Highscore.get-best-rank`-style store; Phoenix's `backend/Highscore.hx` has `saveScore`/`saveWeekScore`/`getScore`/`getRating` and **no** rank or combo-total slot), per-line coloured judgement text, a `Sick+` line, weighted-vs-raw accuracy, and F9 replay save from the screen. Note Kathy's own `ResultsScreen` calls `Highscore.saveCombo` **twice** in the same method (`:647`, `:669`) and references a `Ratings` class that does not exist in its tree (`grep -rln "class Ratings" source/` = 0) — **do not copy the file**; implement the five behaviours on top of `ResultsData`.
- **[P1] Deterministic replay: record, picker, playback, render source — §31.3 and §14 hold the design; the picker and the render-source hookup are Kathy-only and are the two missing pieces.** Kathy's loop is: `replayData` (`{key, noteTime, late}`), `replayHeldKeys`, `replayNoteDelays`, a `NON_NOTE_KEY_OFFSET` scheme, `substates/ReplayPickerSubState.hx` (342 lines: sort by time/accuracy, detail panel with judgement breakdown, rate, version), `saveReplay()` writing `mods/<mod>/replay/<song>-<date>.replay.json` with `replayVersion: "2.0"` + chart mtime + tz offset, and the renderer consuming a replay as its input source (`renderInputSource = 'Replay'`). §14 plans recording and playback but **without** a picker, a file format, or a render hookup — those three are what to lift, and §14's warning stands: record *resolved* hit events, not raw keys, because Kathy's key model breaks on rebind. Take its file shape, not its input model.
- **[P2] `HitGraph` — a live hit-offset graph HUD.** §22.4 already specs the cheap version (`hitErrorBar`, a strip of the last ~15 offsets, `TODO.md:514`) and this is the same data drawn as a graph — **build §22.4 first and reuse its offset buffer**; if the graph is then wanted, Kathy's `backend/HitGraph.hx` (a `StatsGraph` port, credited in its header) adds a scrolling trace with min/max/avg labels, `showInput` from `FlxG.save.data.inputShow`, a configurable axis range and colour. Worth knowing before choosing: Kathy's graph is the thing modders screenshot, the error bar is the thing players tune with. `grep -ril HitGraph source/` = 0.
- **[P2] Death/game-over/input script hooks.** Kathy exposes `onDeath`, `onCustomGameOver`, `onEventPushed`, `onEndCountdown`, `onUp`, `onClick`, `onButtonPress(Pre)`, `onButtonRelease(Pre)`, `onKeyPressPre`, `onKeyReleasePre`, `onRawKeyPress`, `onRawKeyRelease`, `onDraw`, `onDrawPost` (+`onDraw`'s `onDrawPost` pair). Phoenix's hook set (`psychlua/callbacks/*` plus the `callOnLuas('on…')` sites in `play/`) is missing 16 of the 52 Kathy has — re-grepped on `main`: all sixteen appear **zero** times in `source/`, so they are true absent hooks, not renames. The input ones (`onKeyPressPre`/`onRawKeyPress`) are needed for input remappers, `onDraw`/`onDrawPost` for modchart overlays, `onDeath`/`onCustomGameOver` for custom game-over flows, and `onEventPushed` for event-debug tooling. Phoenix is *ahead* on 8 of its own (`onClose`, `onStart`, `onStop`, `onRestart`, `onAndroidEvent`, `onModchartLabSection` + the two resume-countdown hooks) — diff both ways before adding. Effort: M for all sixteen; each is one `callOnLuas` site plus a row in `docs/`.
- **[P2] Per-script state: `getGlobalFromScript`, `setGlobalFromScript`, `callScript`, `loopTheSong` — **already covered here** (`ScriptCallbacks.hx`), listed only so a reader does not re-add them. `getVar`/`setVar` is the actual gap (§32.1).
- **[P2] Audio-level API: `initAnalyzer`, `initPlayerAnalyzer`, `initOpponentAnalyzer`, `getAudioLevels`, `getPlayerAudioLevels`, `getOpponentAudioLevels`.** Kathy exposes a `SpectralAnalyzer` per vocal track with N bars (`PlayState:592-651`). Phoenix ships `funkin.vis` + `grig.audio` and already drives a `SpectralAnalyzer` in `stages/objects/ABotSpeaker.hx`, but only there and hardcoded; `grep -rn '"getAudioLevels"' source/` = 0. **This is the script-visible half of §28.2's menu spectrum bars** — that entry builds `objects/SpectrumBars` on the same analyzer, so expose the levels as an API in the same PR and let mods (and future HUD widgets) consume them. Cheap: one `backend.AudioLevels` holding two analyzers, six Lua functions. Ideal for modchart visualisers.

### 37.8. Options-surface diff (the short version)

Row counts, from `grep -c "new Option("` on each file. **Kathy:** `GraphicsPerformance(31)`, `HudSettings(52)`, `Judgment(30)`, `SystemWindow(28)`, `NotesVisual(24)`, `MobileTouch(22)`, `Render(14)`, `CompatibilityScripts(13)`, `HealthIcons(11)`, `Audio(10)`, `Tools(7)`, `DebugPanels(3)`, `ModSettings(1)` — **~246** rows, plus hand-built (non-`Option`) screens: `KathyFPSCounterSettingsState` (1297 lines), `SimpleInfoDisplaySettingsState` (1137), `NotesColorSubState` (895), `OptionsState` (898), `NoteOffsetState` (765), `OptionsIOState` (703). **Phoenix:** `VisualsUISubState(81)`, `Gameplay(37)`, `Accessibility(18)`, `Optimization(20)`, `Graphics(12)`, `Misc(12)`, `SuperSecretDebugMenu(11)`, `GameRenderer(10)` — **205** rows after #358 plus `Option.hx(1)` and `BaseOptionsMenu(3)` internals. (Per-file counts re-verified with `grep -c "new Option(" source/options/*.hx`; `Controls`, `Notes`, `Audio`, `NoteOffset` and `OptionsState` are hand-built screens with zero `Option` rows, same as Kathy's hand-built ones.)

The counts are close, so the difference is **organisation and a few whole families**, not volume:

- **[P1] Judgment settings menu** (§37.6's 18 unlisted toggles — Kathy's `JudgmentSettingsSubState` is 30 rows in 400 lines, which is the right size for the category). This is the one menu that is a genuine grouping gap: today those toggles would land scattered across Gameplay, Optimization and Visuals.
- **[P1] HUD settings menu** — one menu for Hold Cover, `RatingCounter`, `NoteTimer`, the in-song `MusicPlayer`, §22.4's `hitErrorBar`, the `HitGraph` and §32.6's `KeyViewer`/key overlay. Seven features, one screen; building seven screens is how the options list became unreadable in the first place.
- **[P2] Notes-visual menu** (lane covers, note scale/spacing from §22.3) — Phoenix's notes options are split across `NotesSubState` + `VisualsUISubState` + `GraphicSettingsSubState`, so this is a reorganisation, not new rows.
- **[P2] Debug-panels menu** + configurable FPS counter — **mostly covered already, see the count below.** Kathy's FPS counter is the outlier in this whole comparison:
- **[P2] "Tools & calibration" menu** — **the interactive calibration itself is §31.3's `LatencyState`; do not build a second one.** What Kathy adds on top of that design is the *wording-level* spec: `ratingoffset_cal_*` and `keyviewer_cal_*` key families (~20 keys in `assets/languages/options/en_us.json`) spell out beat, "tap N times", suggested-vs-current, a warm-up phase, early/late counts and apply/reset. Use those as the copy for §31.3's screen — and note a second calibration screen is needed for the key viewer once §32.6 lands.
- **[P3] One row that is a whole feature: `soundTrayStyle`.** Kathy ships **three** selectable trays (`FunkinSoundTray` 180 lines, `DaveEngineSoundTray` 155, `KathySoundTray` 421 — the Blue-Archive one) behind a `soundTrayStyle` row in Options → System/Window (`SystemWindowSettingsSubState.hx:233`). Phoenix has exactly one `objects/CustomSoundTray` (193 lines) and no row for it (`grep -rn soundTrayStyle source/` = 0). Cheap, highly visible, and the Dave/Funkin variants are close to skins Phoenix could draw itself.

### 37.9. Checked and deliberately NOT ported (so nobody re-adds them)

> **Overlap with §34 (Cheese).** Cheese carries the same Hold Cover feature and the same three tray-era prefs, and §34 already has entries for the hold-cover *property API* (`getHoldCoverProperty`/`setHoldCoverProperty`, §34.1), the hold-cover *editor* (§34.4) and mod-overridable **sound-tray art** (§34.5). None of those can be built before the feature itself exists, so **§37.6's Hold Cover entry is the prerequisite for all three** — when it lands, the §34 entries become live and should be implemented with it. Section 32.9 lists SPE's `holdCovers/` art directory, which is the third fork in a row to ship the art for a feature we do not have.

> **Landed on `main` after this audit was first written (#358) — verified against the merge base, do not re-enter any of it:** Stage Editor (`editors/StageEditorState` + `data/StageEditorCore` + `StageUndoStack`, so §37.5's entry is now a *scope diff*, not a build), device benchmark (`backend/Benchmark`/`BenchmarkChart`, `BenchmarkResultsState`, Options -> Optimization), first-run performance presets (`backend/FirstRunPerformance`), per-mod "why did it break" reports (`backend/ModHealth`), photo mode (`PhotoModeSubState` + `PhotoModeMath`), extra vocal stems (`data/ExtraVocals`, `getExtraVocalNames`/`get-setExtraVocal Volume`/`Pitch`), 25 more `GdiCallbacks` functions, and 54 new option rows. None of it was in Kathy's scope as a gap — it just makes our side of the comparison newer than the numbers below.

- **Do not copy Kathy's `Native.hx`.** It is 1143 lines of inline C++ with Chinese comments, hardcoded `"Extra Window #%d"` titles and a `WM_PAINT` that draws literal `"Extra Window"` text. Take the *capability list* (§37.4), write it against Phoenix's `backend/WindowBackend`/`NativeHardware`, Windows-gated, no inline C++ in a `.hx` once a native module exists.
- **Do not port the legacy chart editors.** `OldChartingState063`/`OldChartingState073`/`vanilla104/` are ~9k lines of duplicated editor to serve "mods that only run in the old editor". The V-Slice importer (§37.5) gets the actual value.
- **Do not port Kathy's on-screen event-debug text as a default.** Its `EventHandler` pushes up to 8 `FlxText`s into the playfield (`eventDebugGroup`, `maxEventTexts = 8`) whenever the `eventDebug` pref is on. Phoenix has **no** on-screen event list at all (`grep -rn "eventDebug" source/` = 0), so this is a genuine small gap — but ship it as an opt-in debug overlay in `debug/`, never inline in the playfield, and never on by default.
- **Do not take Kathy's `ClientPrefs` shape.** It is a `@:structInit SaveVariables` class with **307** fields and an empty `ClientPrefs` class body — no reflection-based save loop, no `saveBlackList`/`loadBlackList`, no `SettingsBackup`-compatible allowlist. Phoenix's flat class + reflection save (`ClientPrefs.hx`) is what makes settings export/import and `PlatformSupport.onlyOn()` work. Port **fields**, never the structure.
- **Rename-only mismatches — no action.** `antialiasing`→`globalAntialiasing`, `popUpRating`→`ratingPopups`, `ghostTappingMode`→`ghostTapping`, `cacheResourcesOnReload`→`resourceCachingOnReload`, `ratingsPos`→`ratingOffset`(partly), `showFPS`+`fpsRework`+`fpsLayer`→`showFPS`+`framerate`, `noteSplashOpacity`→`splashType`, `hudZoom`/`hudSize`→`hudZoomStyle`/`uiScalePercent`, `systemCursor`→(N/A), `stageQuality`→`quality`, `lowQuality`→`quality`+`LowMemory`. These are the same setting under a different name; do not add duplicates.
- **Already present under other names, verified:** `wideScreen`→`widescreen`; `borderless`→`WindowMode.BORDERLESS`; `dynamicWindowTitle`→`useCustomWindowTitle`/`windowTitle*`; `disableNetworking`→ (Phoenix has `ModSecurity` + no networking calls at all); `gcOnResume`→`enableGC`; `framerate`→`framerate`; `watermarkType`→`watermarkStyle`; `storageType`/`getStoragePath`→`mobile/StorageUtil` (present, no Lua name); `splashMode` (`Kathy`/`Flixel`/`Flixel+`/`None` startup splash, `Main.hx:392`)→`ClientPrefs.disableSplash` + `Main.hx:52 skipSplash` (two states instead of four — the `Flixel+` enhanced windmill screen and `EnhancedFlixelState` are the only missing piece). Still genuinely missing: `showResultScreen` (§37.6) and `language` (§37.2).
- **Kathy regressions — do not inherit.** Its `ResultsScreen` calls `saveCombo` twice and references a non-existent `Ratings` class; `ModsImport` hardcodes `"全局"` (Chinese) into an English UI; `FreeplayCategory` merges per-mod and global files with "first wins for names, mod wins on save", which silently rewrites a global file from inside a mod folder; `Native.hx` installs a global `WndProc` hook on first use and never removes it (`resetWindowHandle` only clears a static). Any port must be re-implemented against Phoenix's ownership model.
- **Not a gap, despite the name:** `backend/Network.hx` (Kathy) is a `disableNetworking` interceptor for `lime.net.HTTPRequest` + `openUrl`; Phoenix simply does not make network calls outside Discord RPC and the update checker, so there is nothing to intercept. If §19's auto-updater lands, add the interceptor **then**.

### 37.10. Suggested order (highest payoff first)

Numbered by *Kathy-unique* value only — anything already spec'd in §31/§32 should ship inside that spec, not here.

1. **Hold Cover** (M) — the one feature in this whole section that a player can see in the first second of a song. Ship art + `ClientPrefs` + `PlayStateNotes` spawn/despawn + one colour option per side.
2. **`HitSoundPool`** (S) — cheapest win here and squarely on the low-end identity (§8/§18); do it in the same PR series as Hold Cover because both touch hitsound/sustain spawn paths.
3. **The two real bugs** (S each): re-enable `getPropertyAdvanced` (`TweenCallbacks.hx:335`) and replace `LuaUtils.resolveClassCompat`'s 16-prefix guessing with an explicit alias table (coordinate with §32.8 so there is one table, not two).
4. **Scripting QoL batch** (S each): `getFPS`/`getFrameCount`, note inspection (`getNoteData`/`getAliveNotes`/`destroyNote`/`getNoteRating` with an integer handle), achievement functions in Python/HScript, `isRunning`/`getLuaCompatVersion`.
5. **HUD widget pack** (S each: `RatingCounter`, `NoteTimer`, in-song `MusicPlayer`) into the single §37.8 menu — after §32.6's `KeyViewer` lands, so it is one menu and one migration.
6. **Judgment & Holds options category** (M) — the 18 unlisted toggles + `Rating` tier data from §37.6, folded into §32.3's `Rating.hx` shape.
7. **Results extras** (M): letter rank (a real `Highscore` rank slot), `Sick+`/`perfect` line, per-judgement colouring — on top of `ResultsData`, never by copying Kathy's `ResultsScreen`.
8. **Splash overflow policy + sound trays** (S) — `splashLimitMode` and `soundTrayStyle` with the three trays as selectable skins.
9. **Freeplay categories/tabs** (L), **Spine runtime** (L, build-size decision) and **extra native windows** (L, Windows-only, inside §32.1.1) as the long tail.
10. Everything in §37.9: **leave it.**

Ship each numbered item as its own PR with a `tests/haxe/run-*.sh` spec for the pure-logic half and a `docs/` page where it is a subsystem, per house rules. Every ported feature credits **kittycathy332 / FNF-KathyEngine** and the upstream Kathy credits, and keeps a toggle so a vanilla-Psych mod sees today's behaviour.

---

## 38. VS ROB: REBOOTED — ENGINE REUSE REVIEW (`Joalor64/VS-Rob-Rebooted`, 2026-11-21)

**Source:** <https://github.com/Joalor64/VS-Rob-Rebooted> — 100 commits, HEAD `a5bf678` (pushed 2025-06-24), 80 Haxe files + assets. It is a **mod**, not an engine: `Project.xml` still says `version="0.2.7"`, `MainMenuState.psychEngineVersion = '0.5.2h'`, and the README credits "Psych Engine Team (0.5.2h)". So "what the engine has" = **Psych Engine 0.5.2h + Joalor64's own additions on top**.

**Method:** cloned the repo, then diffed its `source/` against a clone of Psych Engine tag `0.5.2h` (`diff -rq` + per-file diffs) to separate *upstream Psych* from *Joalor64's changes*, then checked every Joalor64 change against this tree (grep + read). Also extracted and diffed the Lua callback table, the Lua `set()` globals, the `callOnLuas`/`setOnLuas` hook tables and the HScript `setVariable` table against Phoenix's `psychlua/` registration. Nothing was built or run.

**License — read this before porting anything.** The repo `LICENSE` is **not Apache 2.0** and not an OSI licence: "If you want to modify the code and assets yourself or if you want to make a mod with my engine, OPEN-SOURCE YOUR PROJECT because if you don't, I will happily ask you to take down the project. AND you have to send me $300000000 worth of bitcoin. >:) / Okay the last one was a joke. But still, please open-source your mod. :) / Also, credit isn't necessary, but would be nice. / Copyright 2021-2025 Joalor64 / Upstream (ShadowMario/FNF-PsychEngine): Copyright 2021-2025 ShadowMario". It is a custom "please open-source" notice with no grant of patent/expression rights and no compatibility statement with Apache 2.0. The **code is derived from Apache-2.0 Psych 0.5.2h**, so anything that already exists upstream is fine to take from *Psych* directly (with Psych's attribution); the **small set of genuinely Joalor64-authored additions below should be re-implemented from their behaviour, not copied**, and attributed. That satisfies both the licence caution at the top of this file and the "port WITH THE APPROPRIATE CREDITS" rule.

**Headline:** Phoenix already contains ~95% of this repo. Every Lua shader callback, every gameplay changer, the options, HScript, achievements, Discord, dark mode, prompt, loading screen, crash handler, GameMode, mod packaging and the whole Psych 0.5.2h feature set are present — most of them in a larger form. The honest gap list is **three real features and a handful of small compatibility/comfort items**.

### 38.1. Real gaps worth carrying forward

- **[P2] Global colour-blind screen filter option.** Joalor64's `source/Colorblind.hx` (a `ColorMatrixFilter` applied to `FlxG.game` through `FlxG.game.setFilters`, gated on `ClientPrefs.shaders`) plus `ClientPrefs.colorBlindFilter` (`None`/`Deuteranopia`/`Protanopia`/`Tritanopia`) and an Options → Visuals and UI → "Colorblind Filter:" row (`options/OptionsSubState.hx:984`). Phoenix has the *matrices* already — `backend/ScreenFilters.hx:417-420` ships `protanopia`/`deuteranopia`/`tritanopia` and `psychlua/callbacks/FilterCallbacks.hx:119` exposes `addColorFilter(target, name, preset)` for cameras/`'global'` — and it has a *note-palette* preset (`ClientPrefs.noteColorblindPreset`, `options/VisualsUISubState.hx:241`). What is missing is the **in-engine Options row that applies one of those matrices to the whole screen**. Port: add `ClientPrefs.colorBlindFilter`, a Visuals and UI string option (same `Accessibility.COLORBLIND_PRESETS` list), and apply it through the existing `ScreenFilters`/`FlxG.game` path so Lua's `addColorFilter('global', …)` and the pref do not fight (one clears the other). This is the concrete instance of the still-open "in-engine Options menu toggle" for screen filters in §0. Default **None**, `shaders` gate, effort **S**. Credits: Joalor64; the file itself says "some code from Forever Engine, some from Flixel Demos".

- **[P2] Camera panning on sing.** Joalor64 adds `ClientPrefs.cameraPanning` + `panIntensity` and `PlayState.camPanRoutine()` (`source/PlayState.hx:1244-1268`), called from all four sing paths (`:3197`, `:3201`, `:3450`, `:3539`). On a sing animation it nudges a `moveCamTo` vector by `±40 * panIntensity * 240 / fps` in the sing direction (`:1260-1263`), but only when the singing character owns the section (`mustHitSection`, `:1252-1258`); `moveCamTo` is added to `camFollowPos` and decays back to 0 (`:2029-2040`). Phoenix has camera *follow* (`play/helpers/PlayStateCamera.hx:38,61-67`) and `cameraFxIntensity` for shakes/zooms, but **no per-sing directional nudge**. Do not confuse this with §25.2's `camMovement` toggle, which is about the *section target*; this is an additive impulse on top of whatever the camera is doing, so the two are independent prefs. Port: `cameraPanning` (default **on**, per the porting rule) + `panIntensity` slider (0.01–10, `%vX`), applied in the sing handlers and cleared on section change; must not fight `isCameraOnForcedPos` (`play/helpers/PlayStateEvents.hx:344-347`). Effort **S–M**. Credits: Joalor64.

- **[P3] Song-start banner with composer credits.** Joalor64's `PlayState` builds a HUD panel at song start — `bgThing` / `beforeSongText` ("Now Playing...") / `songText` (song name) / `whoDuntIt` ("Composer - …") at `source/PlayState.hx:773-800`, reads `credits/<song>.txt` for the composer line (`:775-778`), slides in at countdown start (`:1311-1319`) and slides out after ~3.7 s via `hideSongBanner()` (`:1469`). The code comment says "Credits to Unholywanderer04" (`:224`). Phoenix has no equivalent: its watermarks are commit/freshness, bot and platform only (`ClientPrefs.showCommitWatermark`, `botWatermark`, `platformWatermark`, `watermarkStyle`). Port as an optional HUD element (default on, toggle in Visuals and UI), reading `assets/credits/<song>.txt` with the mod-folder fallback (`Paths.mods(...)`), and keep it off `camOther`/on `camHUD` as in the original. Effort **S**. Credits: Unholywanderer04 (original), Joalor64 (integration).

### 38.2. Small / compatibility gaps

- **[P3] Combo-sprite position offset.** Joalor64's `ClientPrefs.comboOffset` has **6** entries; `[4]`/`[5]` shift the combo sprite itself (`source/PlayState.hx:3026-3027`) and `options/NoteOffsetState.hx` got two extra binding slots for it. Phoenix has **4** (`backend/ClientPrefs.hx:449`) — rating X/Y and combo-number X/Y — and `NoteOffsetState` has no combo sprite to drag. Effort **S**; only worth it if the combo popup position needs independent tuning from the digits.

- **[P3] `ALSOFT_CONF` audio-config handling.** Joalor64 ships `alsoft.ini` (renamed `alsoft.conf` off-Windows) and sets `ALSOFT_CONF` in `Main.__init__` (`source/Main.hx`) — Windows: next to the exe, mac: `Resources/alsoft.conf`, Linux: next to the exe — so OpenAL Soft picks up a shipped config. Phoenix has no `alsoft` file or env handling at all. Effort **S**. Worth it only if we ever ship an ALSOFT config (HRTF, resampler, buffer sizes) for low-end audio; otherwise skip.

- **[P3] HScript compatibility aliases.** Joalor64's `FunkinHScript` (Psych 0.5.2h) seeds `trace`, `importScript`, `stopScript`, `createThread`, `createSpriteGroup`, `createTypedGroup`, and `add`/`remove`/`insert`/`members`/`state` bound to `FlxG.state` (`source/FunkinHScript.hx:39,66,70,77,109,146-158`). Phoenix's HScript is a bigger, differently-shaped API — it has `debugPrint` (`psychlua/HScript.hx:720`), `addScript`/`addHScript`/`removeHScript`, `getRunningScripts`, `isRunning`, `callOnScripts`, `addHaxeLibrary`, `state`, `game` — but **not** `trace`, `importScript`, `stopScript`, `createThread` or the `FlxG.state` group helpers. For Psych-0.5.2h-era HScript mods, add thin aliases (`trace` → `debugPrint`; `importScript` → `addHScript` returning its locals; `stopScript` → `Function_Stop`/destroy; `createThread` → `sys.thread.Thread` on `sys`, plain call elsewhere; group helpers → `FlxG.state`). Effort **S**. Credits: MAJigsaw77 (Psych's base HScript code, per VS-Rob's own credits list).

- **[P3] Health-percentage text.** Joalor64 draws `healthTxt` = "Health: N%" above the health bar (`source/PlayState.hx:816-821`). Phoenix has the health bar and icon frames but no percentage label. Effort **S**; fold into the existing `healthBarStyle`/`timeBarStyle` family if added.

- **[P3] "Currently Playing: `<song>` | `<version>`" HUD line.** Joalor64's `versionTxt` (`source/PlayState.hx:840-843`). Phoenix has the commit/freshness watermark (`debug/CommitWatermark.hx`) but no song+version line. Effort **S**; could be another `watermarkStyle`.

- **[P3] `Alphabet` menu layout shapes.** Joalor64's `Alphabet` has `itemType` = `Classic` / `Vertical` / `C-Shape` / `D-Shape` (`source/Alphabet.hx:27,377`) — per-menu layout curves for the selector list. Phoenix's `Alphabet` is a different implementation (`objects/Alphabet.hx`, with `alphabetStyle`/`alphabetFont`/`reloadAll`) and has no layout shapes. Effort **M**; only worth it if a mod needs a non-linear menu, otherwise skip.

- **[P3] Letter-grade rating names.** Joalor64 hardcodes `ratingStuff` = `F-, F, D, C, B, A-, A, A+, S-, S, S+, SS-, SS, SS+, X-, X, X+, P` (`source/PlayState.hx:70-89`). Phoenix's `ratingStuff` is **quote-driven** (`data/ratingQuotes/<rateNameStuff>.txt`, `ClientPrefs.rateNameStuff`, `play/PlayState.hx:1035-1072`), so this is achievable today by dropping in a quotes file — no engine work. A built-in "Letter Grades" preset would still be a nice preset row. Cross-ref §0's "micd up ranking thing (S, A, etc..)". Effort **S**.

- **[P3] Video-skip prompt text.** Joalor64 shows `skipVidTxt` = "Press Z to skip cutscene!" (`source/PlayState.hx:1177-1186`). Phoenix has the skip *logic* (`play/CutsceneHandler.hx:93-107`, `objects/VideoSprite.hx`) but no on-screen hint. Effort **S**; only if cutscenes become common.

### 38.3. Checked and already covered — do not port

- **Every Lua shader callback** (`addChromaticAbberationEffect`, `addScanlineEffect`, `addGrainEffect`, `addTiltshiftEffect`, `addVCREffect`, `addGlitchEffect`, `addPulseEffect`, `addDistortionEffect`, `addInvertEffect`, `addGreyscaleEffect`/`addGrayscaleEffect`, `add3DEffect`, `addBloomEffect`, `clearEffects`) — Phoenix `psychlua/callbacks/EffectCallbacks.hx:47-228`, with more effects and per-camera registries. The diff of the whole Lua callback table shows VS-Rob's only unique name is `clearUnusedMemory`, which is upstream Psych and already in Phoenix (`backend/Paths.clearUnusedMemory`).
- **Whole Lua global/hook surface** — diffed `set()` globals (VS-Rob 68 vs Phoenix 170; the only VS-Rob-only names are `os`/`require`, which Phoenix deliberately sandboxes), `callOnLuas`/`setOnLuas` (VS-Rob's only unique events are `goodNoteHit`/`opponentNoteHit`, both fired at `play/helpers/PlayStateNotes.hx:456,625`), and the HScript table (Phoenix's is a superset).
- **Gameplay changers** — `scrolltype` (multiplicative/constant), `scrollspeed`, `songspeed`, `healthgain`, `healthloss`, `instakill`, `practice`, `botplay`, `opponentplay`, `randommode`, `flip`, `stairmode`, `wavemode`, `onekey` all exist in `backend/ClientPrefs.hx:407-435` and Phoenix adds `bothSides`, `opponentdrain`, `drainlevel`, `jacks`, `randomspeed`, `randomspeedmin/max`, `thetrollingever`; UI in `states/substates/GameplayChangersSubstate.hx`, opened from Freeplay and Story.
- **Options** — `shaders`, `ghostTapAnim`, `displayMilliseconds` (Phoenix `showMS` + `play/objects/MSText.hx`), hit windows (`sickWindow`/`goodWindow`/`badWindow`/`shitWindow` → Phoenix `AccuracyPresets` + `perfectWindow`), `safeFrames`, `ratingOffset`, `comboOffset` (first 4), `noteSplashes`, `hideHud`, `timeBarType`, `flashing`, `camZooms`, `scoreZoom`, `healthBarAlpha`, `showFPS`, `pauseMusic`, `ghostTapping`, `noReset`, `hitsoundVolume`, `controllerMode`, `downScroll`, `middleScroll`, `lowQuality`, `globalAntialiasing`, `framerate` — all present, most with more styles.
- **Global colour-blind matrices** — present as Lua presets (`backend/ScreenFilters.hx:417-420`); only the Options row is missing (§38.1).
- **HScript** — Phoenix's is a rewrite with `addHaxeLibrary`, `createCallback`/`createGlobalCallback`, `getSystemInfo`, `getRunningScripts`, `callOnScripts`, `hxTrace`, sandboxing and `Function_StopLua`/`Function_StopAll`; only the aliases in §38.2 are missing.
- **Achievements** — including `toastie` ("Toaster Gamer"), `ur_bad`, `ur_good`, `hype`, `two_keys`, `oversinging`, `friday_night_play` (`backend/Achievements.hx`), plus the menu and the `checkForAchievement` hooks.
- **Discord RPC** — `backend/DiscordClient.hx` + `changePresence` Lua callback.
- **Windows integration** — dark mode (`system/Window.hx`, applied from `Main.hx:268` via `matchSystemTheme`), message box (`backend/WindowNative.messageBox`, used by `Dialog.hx`), borderless/frame refresh.
- **`Prompt` substate** — `objects/Prompt.hx`; **`InputFormatter`** — `backend/InputFormatter.hx`.
- **Loading screen** — `states/LoadingState.hx` + `objects/LoadingPieDial` + `backend/LoadingDialLogic` (§0 DONE); Joalor64's `funkay`/`loadBar` is a skin.
- **Crash handling** — `backend/CrashHandler`, `CrashBundle`, `CrashBundleWriter`, `DiagnosticsReport`, `RecoveryBoot`.
- **Linux GameMode** — `Main.hx:24,68-85` (`hxgamemode.GamemodeClient`), same as Joalor64's `external/gamemode_client.h`.
- **Mod packaging / data files** — `mods/<folder>/pack.json`, `modsList.txt`, `weekList.txt`, `stageList.txt`, `characterList.txt`, `data/introText.txt`, `credits/` icons.
- **Menus** — story-menu tracklist (`states/StoryMenuState.hx:208`), pause exit choices incl. "Exit to Freeplay" (`states/substates/PauseSubState.hx:32`), freeplay missing-chart error (`states/FreeplayState.hx:84-85,259-262`), `ResetScoreSubState` with difficulty.
- **Camera zooms / screen filters / chainable effects / note RGB** — `backend/CameraEffects.hx`, `psychlua/callbacks/FilterCallbacks.hx`, `backend/ChainableEffects.hx`, `shaders/RGBPalette.hx` + `objects/NoteColors.hx` (Joalor64's `arrowRGB` + `ColorMask` is the same idea as Phoenix's RGB palette shader).
- **Video cutscenes + skip** — `objects/VideoSprite.hx`, `play/CutsceneHandler.hx`, `play/helpers/PlayStateCutscenes.hx`.
- **Score/rating plumbing** — `ratingFC` (`play/helpers/PlayStateRating.hx:435-453`), `fcStrings`, NPS + `maxNPS`, `smoothHealth`, `randomSpeedThing`, `showComboNum`, `comboFunction` — all present.
- **`Conductor.bpm` setter, `CoolUtil.colorFromString`/`precacheSound`/`browserLoad`, `Highscore.formatSong`, `MenuCharacter`/`Character` fallbacks, `PlayerSettings.reset`, `FlashingState` warning, `MusicBeatState` transitions, `FPS` counter, `Macros.getCommitId`, `WindowsAPI`-equivalent, `ColorMask`-equivalent** — all present in a newer form.

### 38.4. Deliberately excluded — mod-specific content (not engine)

Per the request, none of these are TODO material: the Rob week/songs/charts (`assets/songs/hello-friend`, `tutorial`, `assets/data/charts/*`), the `rob` character and BF reskin (`assets/characters/rob.json`, `assets/images/characters/*`), the `cityStage`/Skytopia stage (`assets/images/cityStage/*`, `assets/stages/*`), the title screen art and `FlickeringStar` (`assets/images/title/*`), the MSN-style main menu and freeplay art (`assets/images/mainmenu/bg_msn`, `assets/images/freeplay/slash`, `assets/images/menubackgrounds/*`), the credits list and `assets/images/credits/*`, the Discord client ID (`1384970716440563782`) and "VS Rob Rebooted" presence text, the achievement art (`assets/images/achievements/*`), `assets/credits/*.txt`, `mods/pack.json`'s placeholder name/colour, and the `assets/data/introText.txt` meme list. If any of that art is wanted it belongs in a mod/asset pack with the original artists credited, not in the engine.

## 39. AUMSUM FUNKIN PARITY — DIFFERENCES NOT YET IMPLEMENTED

Source: [AumSum-Funkin-Team/AumSum-Funkin](https://github.com/AumSum-Funkin-Team/AumSum-Funkin), **Apache-2.0** (license-compatible, so everything here is portable), last commit `32b5132` (2022-10-11). It is a fork of **Psych Engine 0.6.2** (`pack.json` version `0.6.2`, `Project.xml` `0.2.8`) — 114 `.hx` under `source/` against Psych's 99, `+6876` diff lines over 58 files, 14 files that do not exist upstream at all. So rather than diffing it against Phoenix (two unrelated trees by now), it was diffed against `ShadowMario/FNF-PsychEngine` tag `0.6.2` first, and only what survived that filter was checked against this tree. Read in full on 2026-11-21; nothing was built or run.

Scope, per the brief: **engine capabilities only** — scripting APIs, options, editor/tooling and engine-level content. AumSum's own songs, weeks, characters, icons, fonts, menu art and its `Progression.hx`/story flags are its mod content and are deliberately absent from this list. Its `Modding.md` is a stale 12-line polymod stub and its `README`/`CHANGELOG` are stock Psych text, so the code is the only evidence used.

What AumSum is not: it has **no** engine-level feature set Phoenix lacks wholesale. Almost every Psych-0.6.2 → 0.7/0.6.3 feature Phoenix already has (note types with `extraData`, `strumsBlocked`, `Note.extraData`, `camOther`, shader API + `.frag/.vert` loading, `Change Scroll Speed`/`Change Song Name`, pause `End Song`/`Skip Time`/`Leave Charting Mode`, scroll-wheel navigation, custom `FlxUIInputText`, dialogue typewriter, `addAnimationByPrefix`, `directoryFileList`, chart-editor playback rate, chart-editor undo, ~360 Lua functions). Everything left below is a narrow, verified gap.

### 39.1. Event payloads & the chart editor

- **[P1] Optional `value3` on chart events.** AumSum's event pipeline carries a third value end to end: `EventNote.value3` (`source/Note.hx:18`), a 5th slot in the chart JSON (`source/PlayState.hx:2523`, `:2625`), `triggerEventNote(name, v1, v2, ?value3)` (`:3492`), the Lua hook `onEvent(name, v1, v2, value3, strumTime)`, `triggerEvent` growing an `arg3` (`FunkinLua.hx:1539`), and the chart editor's `needsValue3` list + `value3InputText` box shown only for the selected event (`editors/ChartingState.hx:95-97`, `:1107-1112`). Phoenix is stuck at two: `EventNote` is `{strumTime, event, value1, value2}` (`objects/Note.hx:14-19`), `PlayStateEvents.triggerEventNote` takes a `strumTime:Float` as its 4th parameter (`play/helpers/PlayStateEvents.hx:84`) — so the slot is occupied and cannot grow — and the loader reads only `subEvent[0..2]` (`play/helpers/PlayStateChartLoader.hx:414-424`). This is not a cosmetic extra: AumSum uses it to move `Change Scroll Speed`'s easing into the chart (`PlayState.hx:3849`) so a speed change can ease instead of snapping, which is exactly what our `Change Scroll Speed` cannot express (`PlayStateEvents.hx:429-443` handles `val2` as a duration, never an ease). Port as: `value3:Null<String>` on `EventNote`, 5th JSON slot accepted, `?value3` threaded through `triggerEventNote`, `onEvent` and Lua `triggerEvent` (keep the 4-arg order so existing scripts do not break — append, do not insert), a "Value 3" box in the chart editor that appears only for events that declare it, and `setTheNeedOfValue3` (AumSum `FunkinLua.hx:461`) renamed to something documented so a custom-event `.lua` can request the field. Then read `value3` as a `LuaUtils.getFlxEaseByString` name in `Change Scroll Speed` when present. Default: events without a third value behave exactly as today. Effort: M. Credit: AumSum Funkin.

- **[P2] Lua scripting inside the chart editor (per-notetype editor scripts).** `editors/EditorLua.hx` (268 lines, AumSum-only) is a small standalone Lua state bound to the editor with nine functions: `getProperty`, `setProperty`, `getPropertyFromGroup`, `setPropertyFromGroup`, `removeFromGroup`, `getColorFromHex`, `setGraphicSize`, `scaleObject`, `updateHitbox` (`EditorLua.hx:60-`). Its only consumer is the **test-play** state: for every note type in the section it starts `custom_notetypes/<type>.lua`, waits 0.1 s and kills it (`editors/EditorPlayState.hx:117-127`) — i.e. a custom note type can finally *be drawn* in test play exactly as it draws in the real song, which is the one thing a charter cannot check today. Phoenix has no editor-side script host at all: `ChartingState` loads nothing at all (`editors/ChartingState.hx:816`, `:1452` switch straight to `new editors.EditorPlayState(...)`, and no `LUA_ALLOWED`/`HSCRIPT_ALLOWED` import exists in either editor file), and `EditorPlayState`/`EditorPlayStateHelpers` contain no Lua, Python or HScript call whatsoever. Port the *capability*, not the hack: give `EditorPlayState` the real script hosts (`FunkinLua`/`PythonScript`/`HScript` on the current mod's `custom_notetypes/<type>` and `custom_events/<name>`, same paths the song uses at `play/PlayState.hx:1761-1783`) plus the `noteTypeMap`/`eventPushedMap` bookkeeping AumSum collects before dropping it (`EditorPlayState.hx:131-133`). Sandbox them like any other script (§6) rather than trusting the file. Option: "Editor scripts in test play" (default on, off switch for charts whose scripts assume a full stage). Effort: M–L. Credit: AumSum Funkin (`EditorLua`).

- **Difficulty dropdown in the chart editor — cross-referenced, no separate entry.** AumSum replaces the free-text field with a `FlxUIDropDownMenuCustom` over the known difficulties, labelled "Difficulty:" (`editors/ChartingState.hx:604-617`, `:657-664`). Phoenix still has a `UI_songDiff` input box reading arbitrary text (`editors/charting/ChartingUISections.hx:268`), which silently creates a new difficulty folder on a typo. Tracked generically in **§12.3 "In-engine song/difficulty creation"** — this is the concrete form a port should take, so build it there rather than opening a second entry.

- **Custom chart/meta property UI in the editor — cross-referenced.** AumSum has no equivalent either, so there is nothing to port; the work stays in **§12.3 "Custom property editing in Charter"**.

- **"Section" instead of "Beat" on the section counter — not a gap.** A one-line wording fix AumSum made to its own test-play HUD (`f63cecc`, "Thing to show sections said beats"): `sectionTxt.text = 'Beat: ' + curSection` → `'Section: ' + curSection`. Phoenix already reads `'Section: ' + curSection` (`editors/EditorPlayState.hx:296`), so **this is not a gap** — recorded only so the next pass does not re-open it.

### 39.2. Scripting APIs

- **[P2] `getColorFromRgb(r, g, b)` and `getDominantColor(tag)` in Lua.** Two colour helpers missing from the ~360 we expose: `FlxColor.fromRGB` for the first and `CoolUtil.dominantColor(sprite)` for the second (`source/backend/CoolUtil.hx:468` already implements it — only the script binding is absent, AumSum `FunkinLua.hx:1399`, `:1404`). The dominant-colour one is the interesting half: it lets a script tint HUD text, pops or sustains to the character's average colour instead of a hard-coded constant, and it is the exact primitive a "match the art" custom-event wants. Register both in Lua **and** Python/HScript so the three hosts stay symmetrical (Phoenix keeps them in lockstep; `pystdlib/PyScoreLib.hx:81` has the `getColorFromHex` precedent). Effort: S. Credit: AumSum Funkin.

- **[P2] Window geometry from scripts: `moveWindow(x, y)` and a script-callable resize.** AumSum's `ModchartFunctions.hx` is three functions, but two of them are the only way a mod can touch the OS window's position or the game's internal resolution from script: `moveWindow(x, y)` → `Lib.application.window.move(x, y)`, and `camZoom(w, h)` → `FlxG.resizeGame(w, h)`. Phoenix has the machinery and does not expose it: `backend/ModchartFunctions.hx` does not exist, `FlxG.resizeGame` is only called from the Flixel patch and from Options → Graphics (`options/GraphicsSettingsSubState.hx:211`, where the comment is explicit that `resizeGame` alone does not resize cameras — so a script version has to re-lay-out cameras the way that option does), and `psychlua/callbacks/WindowCallbacks.hx` is ten functions about transparency/alpha/colour-key/click-through and nothing about position or size. Port as `moveWindow(x, y)` (desktop only, refuse on mobile/web with a message, clamp to the available displays) and `setGameResolution(w, h)`/`resetGameResolution()` built on the *camera-aware* path from Graphics, not the bare `resizeGame`. Both default off-limits until a script calls them (no default behaviour change). Effort: M. Credit: AumSum Funkin.

- **[P2] OS titlebar theme from scripts (dark / light / opacity alias).** AumSum's `CppAPI.hx` exposes `darkMode()`, `lightMode()`, `setWindowColorMode(Bool)`, `setWindowOppacity(Float)` to Lua so a script can flip the native chrome to match a dark song or an in-game cutscene. Phoenix *has* the implementation and keeps it to itself: `system/Window.hx` (`darkMode`, `matchSystemTheme`, `redrawFrame`) is called exactly once, from `Main.hx:268`, and `backend/WindowColorMode.hx` still holds the legacy `setWindowColorMode`/`setDarkMode`/`setWindowBorderColor`/`setWindowTitleColor` entry points — none of which any script can reach (`WindowCallbacks.hx` has ten functions, none of them this). Wire the existing calls into `WindowCallbacks` (plus Python/HScript): `setWindowColorMode(dark)`, `setWindowOpacity(0..1)` as the correctly spelled alias of the existing `setWindowAlpha`, and `getWindowColorMode()`. Windows-only per `#if windows`, with the existing "not available on this platform" trace on the others. Note §1's `[P3]` about deleting the `WindowColorMode` *flash stubs* — that is about dead AIR paths, not about these real native calls, so the two do not conflict. Effort: S. Credit: AumSum Funkin.

- **[P3] `getGlobals(luaFile)` — investigate, do not port.** AumSum's copy of this function is **commented out** (`FunkinLua.hx:1317`), as are `getPropertyAdvanced`/`setPropertyAdvanced`, so there is no working implementation upstream to port and nothing to gain. Phoenix already has the safer pair (`getGlobalFromScript`/`setGlobalFromScript`, `psychlua/callbacks/ScriptCallbacks.hx:107-180`, mirrored in `PyScriptLib.hx:115`). No action beyond this line.

### 39.3. First boot on weak hardware

- **[P2] Hardware gate with "play anyway" on first launch (desktop).** `SpecsDetector.hx` (AumSum-only, 64 lines) is a pre-`InitState` state that checks `Capabilities.supports64BitProcesses` and physical RAM ≥ 4096 MB, then either continues silently or shows a native `MessageBox` — "Your PC does not meet the requirements… Do you want to play anyway?" — where "No" is `Sys.exit(0)`; the verdict is cached in `FlxG.save.data.cachestart` so the box appears once. Phoenix has every input this needs already (`debug/system/SystemInfo.hx:46` `logicalCPUCount()`, `:50` `totalPhysicalMemory()`, plus `backend/Native.hx:113` `getSystemInfo()` for CPU/GPU strings) and nothing that uses them at startup: there is no pre-`InitState` state and no notification of any kind. Do **not** copy AumSum's behaviour — a modal that blocks the game and can call `Sys.exit` is hostile on a machine the player cannot upgrade, and Phoenix's whole identity is running on those machines. Instead make it the trigger for the existing plan: on first run, if the device is below a threshold, apply the low-end preset automatically and *say so in-game* with a dismissible notice, then let the player opt into the benchmark (§18 `[P3]`). That is **§18 `[P1]` "First-run performance profile"** — this entry exists only to name AumSum's `SpecsDetector` as a second source for it and to record the native-message-box approach as rejected. Toggle: the notice, and the whole auto-detect, in Options → Optimization.

### 39.4. Menu & title content hooks

- **[P3] `title.json` GF scale and antialiasing keys.** AumSum's `TitleData` gained `gfscalex`, `gfscaley` and `gfantialiasing`, applied straight to the dancer (`TitleState.hx:50-52`, `:333-334`), so a mod can ship a title screen where the dancer is not the stock size and not smoothed. Phoenix's `TitleData` stops at `titlex/titley/startx/starty/gfx/gfy/backgroundSprite/bpm/endY` (`states/TitleState.hx:29-40`) and the fallback literal at `:135` matches, so a mod that reuses an AumSum-style `title.json` gets the dancer at 1× with global antialiasing. Cheap and additive: three optional fields, defaulted when absent. Effort: S. Credit: AumSum Funkin.

- **[P3] Per-JSON `noAntialiasing` for menu characters.** AumSum's `MenuCharacter` honours `noAntialiasing` from `menucharacters/<name>.json` (both `true` → off and `false` → follow the pref, `source/MenuCharacter.hx:90-93`). Phoenix hard-assigns `antialiasing = ClientPrefs.globalAntialiasing` in `changeCharacter()` (`objects/MenuCharacter.hx:36`) and its `MenuCharacterFile` typedef has no such field (`:9-16`), so a pixel-art menu character is always smoothed when global AA is on. Add the optional field with the same tri-state semantics. Effort: S. Credit: AumSum Funkin.

### 39.5. Mod folders & the mod list

- **[P3] Reserve a `haxescript/` folder (legacy alias only).** AumSum's `HAXE_EXTENSION` build autoloads global "HaxeScript" from a dedicated `haxescript/` folder (preload, `mods/haxescript/`, active mod, each global mod — `PlayState.hx:895-905`), and its own second pass looks for a file literally named `.hxs` (`:1317`) with a bare `hscript.Parser`/`interp` — no `Paths`, no callbacks, no error reporting, and the extension check can never match a real filename, so that pass is dead. Phoenix folds HScript into `scripts/` (`play/PlayState.hx:915-968` scans `scripts/`; `psychlua/HScript.hx:118` accepts `.hx` and `.hscript` only) and `ModPathPolicy.RESERVED_MOD_FOLDERS` (`backend/ModPathPolicy.hx:41-45`) contains neither `haxescript` nor `haxe`. Two real consequences: an AumSum-style mod that drops files in `mods/<mod>/haxescript/` loads nothing, and a mod *named* `haxescript` is legal here and would collide if we ever reserved the name. So: keep `scripts/` as the only autoload path (one path, one set of semantics — do not clone AumSum's duplicate), add `haxescript` and `haxe` to the reserved list so the name cannot be taken, and if compatibility ever matters, scan `haxescript/` as a silent alias after `scripts/` with the existing base-name dedupe. Do not copy the `.hxs` pass. Effort: S. Credit: AumSum Funkin (folder name only).

- **[P3] A third field in `modsList.txt`: per-mod "runs globally".** AumSum's `ModsListEntry` is `{dirName, globalEnabled, disabled}` and the file line grows to `folder|enabled|globalEnabled` (`c0cedaa`, `source/Mods.hx:100-140`), so a player can keep a mod enabled for its own content while stopping it from overriding the main-menu background — the mix of `pack.json`'s `runsGlobally` with a user override. Phoenix's parser reads `dat[0]` and `dat[1]` only (`backend/Mods.hx:212-231`) and `runsGlobally` is a `pack.json` field the *author* sets (`:60`), with no UI and no override in `ModInfoSubState` (it displays the flag at `:140`). Add the optional third field, default "inherit `pack.json`", and expose it as a second toggle next to the enable switch. Keep it optional both ways so an existing two-field file keeps working and a three-field file opened in an older build is not corrupted. Note this is a *compatibility/UX* gap, not the CNE addon priority work — that is **§12.6**. Effort: S–M. Credit: AumSum Funkin (and the Psych PR it came from).

### 39.6. Typography & timing ranges

- **[P2] Latin-1 glyphs in the `alphabet` spritesheet.** `objects/Alphabet.allLetters` already lists `á é í ó ú à è ì ò ù â ê î ô û ã ë ï õ ü ä ö å ø æ ñ ç š ž ý ÿ ß` (`objects/Alphabet.hx:414-420`), `AlphabetFontRenderer.PROBE` includes `ÄÁç`, and `AlphabetFont.supports()` accepts every code point ≥ 32 (`:246-251`) — but the shipped atlas has **zero** non-ASCII `<SubTexture>` names (`assets/preload/images/alphabet.xml`), so with the default `Alphabet` spritesheet style a Portuguese, Spanish, French or German menu string renders `?` for every accented letter, while the same string is correct on the font path. AumSum has 176 of them (inherited from Psych's `d3ea0a5` "Psych latinchar support"). Two honest routes: (a) draw and add the glyphs to the atlas, which is asset work and the reason this has not been done, or (b) make `Alphabet` fall back per character to the font renderer when the atlas has no animation for it, which is code work and fixes mod-supplied alphabets too. (b) is the better fit for a game whose UI text is player-visible and often localized — but it must respect the style the caller asked for, so prototype it on one screen first. "Phoenix is a Brazilian engine" makes this less cosmetic than it looks. Effort: M (b), L (a). Credit: Psych Engine upstream (AumSum just carries it).

- **[P3] Widen the note-offset range to −500 ms.** `NoteOffsetState` clamps `ClientPrefs.noteOffset` to `0…500` (`options/NoteOffsetState.hx:31-32`, enforced at `:326-347`), so a player whose output chain runs *late* can only push notes later; AumSum widened the minimum to `-500` (`e20b2c0`), letting an offset go negative and compensate both ways. One-line change to `delayMin` plus the bar's bounds — the `FlxBar` at `:184` already reads `delayMin`/`delayMax`, and the clamp helper at `:347` already uses both. Keep `delayMin`/`delayMax` as the single source so the bar, the stepper and the clamp cannot drift. Effort: S (XS really). Credit: AumSum Funkin.

### 39.7. Checked and deliberately not taken

- **`Progression.hx`** — `beatMainWeek`/`badEnding`/`goodEnding`/`beatHell` globals and a `weekProgress` map. Story progress for AumSum's own campaign; Phoenix's save schema (§13) is the place for any progress model, and a per-mod global of this shape would freeze a mod's story into the engine. Not a capability.
- **`HelperFunctions.hx`** — `truncateFloat`, `erf`, `getNotes`, `getHolds`, `getMapMaxScore`, `wife3`. No call sites anywhere in AumSum's tree and none of it is registered to any script host, so this is dead code, not an API. (`getTotalNotes`/`getMaxScore` do not exist in its Lua either — do not attribute them.) If a `wife3` accuracy model is ever wanted that is an accuracy/score change and belongs with the §22 accuracy presets, not here.
- **`Signal.hx`** — a 16-line `connect`/`disconnect`/`trigger` array wrapper duplicating `flixel.util.FlxSignal`, which Phoenix already uses. Nothing to gain.
- **`DumbKidLol.hx`** — an empty/placeholder class in the `default` package. No.
- **`Snd.hx` / `SndTV.hx`** — no call sites; dead experiments. Do not report these as features in any future pass.
- **`PlayerSettings.hx`** — present in Psych 0.6.2 itself (`diff` against the tag is empty), so it is not an AumSum contribution; Phoenix already has its own `backend/PlayerSettings.hx`.
- **Pause → "Configure Settings" (`7d2de92`)** — Phoenix is ahead: its pause menu already has an `Options` row (`states/substates/PauseSubState.hx:31`) and `OptionsState` returns to the song through `PauseSubState.inPause` (`options/OptionsState.hx:250-253`). AumSum's version restarts the song from scratch on the way back (`new PlayState()`), which would lose the run.
- **Chart-editor playback rate (`ee415ed`)** — already present and richer here: `playbackSpeed` 0.25–4 via `[`/`]`, `Alt` to reset, and it pitches both vocal tracks (`editors/ChartingState.hx:1850-1859`, the `sliderRate` row at `editors/charting/ChartingUISections.hx:1612-1615`).
- **Chart-editor undo** — AumSum has none; Phoenix already has undos (`editors/ChartingState.hx:88-89`). Not a gap in either direction.
- **Freeplay/Story `(N/total)` counters** — checked and absent from AumSum too: `scoreText` in `StoryMenuState` gets no index prefix (`:102`, `:254`) and neither tree has a `curSelected+1`-style counter. Do not attribute.
- **`splashSkinFramesMap`, `strumsBlocked` + `parseKeys`, `Note.extraData`, `ClientPrefs.shaders` + Lua `shadersEnabled`, `camOther`, `PlayStateShaders` + `.frag`/`.vert`, mouse-wheel menu navigation, `CustomFlxInputText`, dialogue typewriter, `addOffset`/`playAnim`/`addAnimationByPrefix`, `directoryFileList` + `SandboxPath` case folding, `MonitorRefreshRate`, `MemoryProfiles`/`LowMemory`, menu mouse navigation, FPS counter, UI scale** — all present in Phoenix already, most of them more developed than AumSum's copies; several are tracked in §12.4/§16/§18 and are not repeated here.
- **`hxvlc`, `PlatformSupport` capability tags, results screen, `ImGui` console, strumline editors** — Phoenix has these; AumSum does not.


## 40. PSYCH ONLINE (FPO) PARITY — DIFFERENCES NOT YET IMPLEMENTED (2026-11-21)

Source: [Snirozu/Funkin-Psych-Online](https://github.com/Snirozu/Funkin-Psych-Online) ("Psych Online", v0.14.9, Apache 2.0 — license-compatible with ours; engine ports credit Snirozu / Psych Online, and content ports keep their in-file credits — the Erect Remix charts name their chartists in-chart). It forks Psych 1.0 (upstream base `5d0a66dea226aa4a32ec5c41f113112ebb15e692`, "Merge PR #13201"), which makes it the closest thing to "vanilla Psych 1.0" this tree has been measured against — but its identity is the **online game**, not the engine: colyseus rooms against a hardcoded server list (`server_addresses.txt`), accounts and Funkin Points, leaderboards + ranked replays, an in-game GameBanana / Google Drive mod downloader, a Skins browser bound to the online account, and `sync.hxs` remote hscript fetched at launch on every client. None of that is in scope (see 40.11). This section lists only what the same tree ships that works **offline** and has no counterpart here. Compared by reading the FPO tree against this tree (against a `443ae01` checkout, and re-checked against `main` at `fd0f95d` — the results screen, the lane underlay and the mod-settings spec all shipped on `main` in the meantime, which is why they are cross-references below rather than port items); assets were enumerated from the repository tree, not copied. Nothing was built or run.

House rules apply verbatim: every port is **on by default with a toggle** (or, where noted, inherently opt-in per song/difficulty), every port gets **credits**, and only **Apache-2.0-compatible** material ships. Bold = biggest payoff. Audio splits of real songs are the risky license part — see 40.12 before importing any `.ogg`.

### 40.1. **Erect / Nightmare completion** [P2]

Phoenix already ships most of Psych 1.0's erect content: erect + nightmare charts for 13 songs (blammed, bopeebo, dad-battle, fresh, high, philly-nice, pico, roses, satin-panties, senpai, south, spookeez, thorns) and erect audio resolution (charts either set `specialAudioName: 'erect'` or fall back to the difficulty name itself, both paths landing on `Voices-<char>-erect.ogg` / `Inst-erect.ogg`), `Erect, Nightmare` in `CoolUtil.defaultDifficultiesFull` (`source/backend/CoolUtil.hx:22`), freeplay offering them for default songs (`source/states/helpers/FreeplayStateHelpers.hx:334-339`) and the `specialAudioName`/`specialEventsName` machinery (`source/data/Song.hx:41-42`, `source/play/helpers/PlayStateChartLoader.hx:52,88`). Still missing:

- **Charts/audio for Cocoa and Eggnog (week 5), Ugh (week 7) and Darnell (weekend 1).** `assets/songs/{cocoa,eggnog,ugh,darnell}` and `assets/preload/data/{cocoa,eggnog,ugh,darnell}/` contain no `*-erect*`/`*-nightmare*` at all; FPO ships `<song>-erect.json`, `<song>-nightmare.json`, `events-erect.json`, `Inst-erect.ogg` and both split erect vocals per song. Also missing: `events-erect.json` for **roses** and **thorns** (charts already shipped here, their erect events do not).
- **The erect stages, end to end.** FPO maps the difficulties onto dedicated classes — `StageErect` (week 1), `SpookyErect` (week 2), `PhillyErect` (week 3), `LimoErect` (week 4), `MallErect` (week 5), `SchoolErect` (week 6, Senpai/Roses), `FranksSpiritsBowling` (week 7, replaces the tank battlefield) — weekend 1 reuses `PhillyStreets` — plus simple-format stage JSONs (`assets/preload/stages/{stage,spooky,philly,limo,mall,school,tank,phillyStreets}-erect.json`), roughly **a hundred** erect stage-art files (`assets/week_assets/weekN/images/erect/…`: week 1 `backDark`/`crowd`/`lights`, week 3 `bloodPool/` + `pico_doppleganger/` atlases, week 4 `limoSunset`/`mist*`, week 5 `bgEscalator`/`snowflakes`, week 6 `evilSchoolBG` + `masks/` + `weebTrees`, week 7 `tankmanEnding/` atlas + `guy.png` + `sniper.png`, weekend 1 `mist*`/`paper`/`phillySkyline`), and the swap wiring (`isErect` on Erect/Nightmare, then `swagStage += '-erect'` for the vanilla stage names — FPO `PlayState.hx:637-638,752-759`). `source/stages/` has no `*Erect*` class and nothing anywhere does the swap, so erect charts today render on the **vanilla** stage art with none of the erect lighting, backgrounds or characters. Phoenix keeps week art in the `week2`…`week7`/`weekend1` openfl libraries (`project.hxp`) and week 1 in `shared`; the pack follows that layout and `project.hxp` already globs those folders, so files just drop in.
- **Erect difficulty icons.** `assets/preload/images/menudifficulties/` has easy…insane/jshard/noob but no `erect.png`/`nightmare.png` (StoryMenuState loads `menudifficulties/<diff>`; there is no null guard worth relying on). Only reached if story mode ever offers erect — same status as FPO.
- The erect classes import three shaders that don't exist here (`AdjustColor`, `DropShadow`, `DropShadowScreenspace`) — see 40.7.

Effort: **L** — mostly content plus porting the vanilla stages to their erect variants; the JSONs need no conversion. Not a pref: it's per-song difficulty content, on by nature of being picked. Weekend 1 note: Phoenix already ships `darnell-bf` as a `bf` difficulty (`FreeplayStateHelpers.hx:342-345`); that's the BF mix, unrelated to darnell's erect.

### 40.2. **Missing Pico game-over audio — referenced but not shipped** [P1]

This is a bug today, independent of the parity work: `source/stages/PhillyBlazin.hx:100-102` and `source/stages/PhillyStreets.hx:154-156,1000-1001` set `deathSoundName`/`loopSoundName`/`endSoundName` to `fnf_loss_sfx-pico-gutpunch`, `fnf_loss_sfx-pico`, `fnf_loss_sfx-pico-explode`, `gameOver-pico` and `gameOverStart-pico-explode` — **none of these files exist anywhere in `assets/`**. A failed weekend 1 run therefore points the game-over sound at files that aren't there. Fix one of two ways: ship the FPO Pico pack (`gameOver-pico.ogg`, `gameOverEnd-pico.ogg`, `gameOver-pixel-pico.ogg`, `gameOverStart-pico-explode.ogg`, `fnf_loss_sfx-pico{,-gutpunch,-explode}.ogg`, `jeffGameover-pico/1-10.ogg`, `breakfast-pico.ogg`, all Apache 2.0) or repoint the stages at the vanilla `fnf_loss_sfx` / `gameOver-pixel` files (week 6 shows the pattern: those live in the `week6` library with a loose-file fallback).

### 40.3. Character Mixes ("Pico Mixes" / "BF Mix") [P2]

Psych 1.0's character mixes — the same songs re-sung by other cast, exposed as a `Character Mix` entry in the freeplay grouping dropdown. Missing here end to end:

- **The mix table**: `assets/preload/characters_weeks/{pico,bf}.json` (song → difficulties). Pico: bopeebo, fresh, dad-battle (Hard only), spookeez, south, pico, philly-nice, blammed, cocoa, eggnog, senpai, roses, ugh, guns, stress. BF: darnell, lit-up.
- **The `-pico` charts and audio** for those 15 songs (`<song>-pico{-easy,-hard,}.json`, `Inst.ogg`, `Voices-Opponent.ogg`, `Voices-Player.ogg`), plus `stress-pico/picospeaker.json`. Phoenix ships the Darnell BF mix (`assets/preload/data/darnell/darnell-bf.json`, `Inst-bf.ogg`, `Voices-{Opponent,bf}-bf.ogg`) but **not** the Lit Up BF mix (`lit-up-bf` charts/audio absent) and none of the pico mixes. `assets/shared/music/breakfast-pico.ogg` and the pico game-over pack are part of the same content delta (40.2).
- **The freeplay glue**: FPO resolves the played song via a mix suffix (`FreeplayState.getMixSuffix`, appending `-pico`/`-bf` to track/chart/audio), scans `characters_weeks/` per character, and lists them under a `Character Mix` group (`GROUPS`, `searchGroup`). Phoenix has no `characters_weeks/` support and no mix resolution — adding it means teaching the freeplay song-resolution path to accept a suffix, not writing a new menu.
- `characters_results/{bf,pico}.json` — only worth it together with §21.1's results screen (already on `main`; see 40.4).

Scope call (keep the offline part): the resolution above is portable; FPO's Skins browser (`online/states/SkinsState.hx`, binds mixes to the online account) is **not** — do not port it. Cross-ref §25.1: mix charts are plain 4-lane, no conflict with EK+ work. Cross-ref §22: the `Character Mix` group belongs to the same `groupSongsBy` family as the freeplay-grouping option in 40.8.

Effort: **M** (bulk asset import + one resolution branch). Port behind a `characterMixes` bool, default **on**.

### 40.4. Results screen — already on this tree; FPO's variant is not portable [P3 optional reskin only]

This was verified against `main` at `fd0f95d`, where §21.1 landed: `states/ResultsState.hx` + `ResultsData.hx` ship the post-song screen (score, accuracy, judgement breakdown, hit histogram, `naughtynessResults`), opened from `play/helpers/PlayStatePlayback.hx:530,570`. Phoenix does **not** lack a results screen; what it lacks of FPO's is:

- `assets/shared/images/resultScreen/` (~90 files: `rankText/rankScroll*`, `clearPercent/`, `score-digital-numbers`, `tallieNumber`, `soundSystem`, `scorePopin`, `ratingsPopin`, `tardlingSpritesheet(FP)`, `results-bf/` + `results-pico/` spritemap atlases, `diff_erect.png`/`diff_nightmare.png`),
- `assets/shared/music/results*` (EXCELLENT/GREAT/GOOD/NORMAL/SHIT × default/pico, several mp3+ogg pairs with intros and `-metadata.json`), and
- `characters_results/{bf,pico}.json`.

FPO's `ResultsSoloState`/`ResultsState` themselves are Funkin-Points/replay/leaderboard machines — porting them would replace a working local screen with an online-wired one (see 40.11). The only sane scope is an **optional P3 reskin** of the existing screen, and only if the maintainer wants FPO's rank presentation; skip the online parts outright. Cross-ref §22.6 (`autoScreenshot` on the results screen), §35.3.3 (`resultsScreen` skip toggle, P3 — owns the toggle; don't re-add), §37.7 (Kathy's letter-rank extras — owns the rank work).

Effort: **S** for the toggle-adjacent wiring, **L** if the full art+music reskin is taken. No toggle of its own — the existing screen is the feature.

### 40.5. Mod settings from `data/settings.json` (Psych 1.0) — duplicate of an existing spec; do not implement twice [cross-ref]

Reviewed on `main`: this is already the subject of **§27.1.1 (Mixtape Engine review, [P1])** with a complete spec — the commented `// import options.ModSettingsSubState;` (`source/states/ModsMenuState.hx:19`), the `// not yet` settings-button block (`ModsMenuState.hx:307-325`), the missing `source/options/ModSettingsSubState.hx`, the commented `data/settings.json` loader in `ModItem` (`ModsMenuState.hx:933-952`) with the `settings` field declared (`:925`), the `FlxG.save.data.modSettings[folder]` save map, the `getModSetting()` script API, keybind-option support, TJSON parsing, and the §13 save-namespace note. Re-confirmed from two more independent references on `main`: §31.1 (SeiunEngine, [P1], `ClientPrefs.modSettings[mod]` + `getModSetting` in Lua/HScript) and §32.3 (Super Plus, [P2]) with §34.1 (Cheese) noting §27.1.1 as the canonical item. FPO is simply Psych 1.0's own version of the same feature — it adds **nothing** to those specs; `tjson` is already a dependency here (`project.hxp:960`, `hmm.json`). Read §27.1.1's spec and implement that, not a second one; FPO's `ModSettingsSubState.hx` can serve as a second Apache-2.0 reference while doing it. §21.9 (per-mod engine-preference profiles) is a different feature and must not be conflated.

### 40.6. Psych 1.0 stage file format: static `objects` + `LoadFilters` [P2]

Phoenix's `StageFile` (`source/data/StageData.hx:9-33`) is the Psych 0.7 shape: `directory`, `defaultZoom`, `isPixelStage`, `stageUI`, `boyfriend/girlfriend/opponent`, `hide_girlfriend`, `shaders`, `camera_*` — nothing else is parsed or rendered. Psych 1.0 stages add, and **none of it works here**: **`preload`** (images/sounds to warm), **`characterFeetPos`** (feet-anchored character positioning), **`reservedNames`**, and **`objects`** — an array of static stage sprites with types `gf | gfGroup | dad | dadGroup | boyfriend | boyfriendGroup | square | sprite | animatedSprite`, each with `x,y,name,scale,scroll,color,alpha,angle,filters,animations` (indices/offsets/firstAnimation), `antialiasing`, `flipX`, `flipY`. `filters` are `LoadFilters` bitflags (`LOW_QUALITY`, `HIGH_QUALITY`, `STORY_MODE`, `FREEPLAY`) honored by `validateVisibility()`; instantiation goes through `addObjectsToState()` via `ModchartSprite`, which Phoenix already has (`source/psychlua/ModchartSprite.hx`).

Why it matters: any stage JSON written against current Psych — the common case for new mods — silently loses its `objects` here; the stage's decorative art just never appears, with no error. Fix is contained to `StageData` + wherever Phoenix builds the stage sprite group (the stage creation path in `play/PlayState.hx` / `objects` setup), not to any renderer.

Effort: **M**. Cross-ref §12 — same class of "upstream format compatibility" work as CNE parity; consider moving this entry there when it gets picked up. Cross-ref §17 — data format only, no script surface.
